# PHASE 2 — Executable specification v5

Status: **AMENDMENTS 1, 2, AND 3 APPLIED; OWNER HASH ADOPTION REQUIRED BEFORE EXECUTION.** The owner adopted root `PHASE2_PLAN.md` v5 and root `PHASE2_AMENDMENT_1.md` through `PHASE2_AMENDMENT_3.md` on 2026-09-01. This document is the complete §5.1 contract package with P1-A through P1-U, Amendment 2 findings 1–8, and Amendment 3 findings 1–9 resolved normatively. It authorizes documentation and read-only review only. A Phase 2 worktree, RED test, source edit, seed, deploy, production-connected test, StoreKit action, remote write, FCM/APNs send, or Build 25 edit is forbidden unless an external review report bound to the exact whole-file tuple records zero P0/P1 and the owner then adopts that reported SHA-256.

## 2. Canonical immutable policy contract

### 2.1 Value domain, limits, and canonicalization

- The only policy values are `null`, Boolean, NFC string, safe integer in `[-9007199254740991, 9007199254740991]`, millisecond-aligned Firestore timestamp/JavaScript `Date`, array, and plain string-keyed map. A timestamp must have nanoseconds divisible by 1,000,000 and an epoch-millisecond value exactly representable by both JavaScript `Date` and Swift `Date` in `0001-01-01T00:00:00.000Z...9999-12-31T23:59:59.999Z`; sub-millisecond values are invalid rather than rounded. Binary, reference, geopoint, undefined, functions, cycles, nonfinite/fractional numbers, negative zero, invalid Unicode, and non-NFC strings are invalid.
- Maximum nesting depth is 16; map width 64; array length 64; ordinary string 1,024 UTF-8 bytes; visible label 256 UTF-8 bytes; complete canonical policy 65,536 UTF-8 bytes. Blank map keys and keys beginning with `$` are invalid.
- Policy/registry IDs match `^[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,127}$`. Capability, kind, status, source, and effect enums are the uppercase literals named here. Set-valued arrays are unique and ordered by their frozen enum order; presentation arrays retain declared order.
- Canonical maps sort keys by unsigned lexicographic comparison of raw UTF-8 bytes, not JavaScript UTF-16. Arrays retain order. Strings retain non-control Unicode; encode quote as `\"`, backslash as `\\`, and every U+0000–U+001F control as six lowercase bytes `\u00xx` (never `\b`, `\f`, `\n`, `\r`, or `\t`). Integers use minimal base-10 with no leading plus/zero. Emit no whitespace and no trailing newline.
- A policy/domain millisecond timestamp encodes as the reserved map `{"$timestamp":"YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.SSSZ"}` in UTC and therefore must satisfy the millisecond rule above. A Firestore snapshot `updateTime` is different internal authority: it is never converted through `Date` and encodes losslessly as reserved map `{"$firestoreTimestamp":{"nanoseconds":N,"seconds":S}}`, where `S` is the signed Firestore seconds integer and `N` is integer `0...999999999`. JS uses Firestore Timestamp `seconds/nanoseconds`; Swift uses `Timestamp.seconds/nanoseconds`. Every `snapshot_update_time` and source-version preimage in this specification uses that lossless form. User maps cannot contain `$` keys, so neither representation can collide.
- Fingerprint input is the complete canonical map with only the top-level `policy_fingerprint` member omitted. `policy_fingerprint = "p2i1_" + lowercase SHA-256`. The full map is canonicalized again and size-checked.
- JS and Swift implement the algorithm independently. Neither implementation accepts Firestore serialization bytes as canonical policy bytes.

`CallableTimestampWireV1` is the only timestamp representation accepted anywhere in a callable request: a JSON string matching exact grammar `^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}T[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}\.[0-9]{3}Z$`, denoting a valid proleptic-Gregorian instant in years `0001...9999`, with no leap second, and parsing then formatting back to byte-identical text. Offsets, lowercase `z`, missing or extra fractional digits, numbers, JavaScript `Date`, Swift/Firestore `Timestamp`, `{"$timestamp":...}`, callable special-protocol timestamp maps, and every `FieldValue`/server-timestamp/delete/increment/array sentinel reject recursively before operation lookup or fingerprinting. The complete accepted-request canonical bytes retain each timestamp as that exact JSON string; no parse-and-reserialize step changes the fingerprint. Only after request shape/fingerprint validation does the server parse each schema-declared timestamp to a concrete millisecond-aligned Firestore Timestamp for comparisons and stored projections. An ISO-looking ordinary string remains a string unless its schema declares a timestamp.

`TaskCanonicalV1` is the separate canonical domain for complete Firestore task maps, semantic snapshots, operation records that embed task/content values, component-cap sizing, the notes/quotes request members that admit fractional values, and the complete accepted requests for legacy `REPLACE_QUOTES`, `COMPLETE_BOOKING`, `COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION`, and `persistLegacyPackingPlan`. It inherits §2.1's map ordering, string escaping/NFC, depth, and forbidden-type rules, **not** the policy-only timestamp encoding, 64-member map/array caps, or 65,536-byte whole-policy cap; every task member and aggregate instead obeys its explicitly named schema/cap in §§3–7. A Firestore Timestamp in a stored map encodes losslessly as `{"$firestoreTimestamp":{"nanoseconds":N,"seconds":S}}`, including a historical sub-millisecond value; a JavaScript/Swift Date encodes through the same form with millisecond-aligned `N`. A schema-declared callable wire timestamp remains its exact string in an accepted-request preimage. Its numeric union is a safe integer or a finite IEEE-754 binary64 value. A binary64 value is emitted as the shortest correctly rounded decimal that parses back to the identical 64-bit value under round-to-nearest/ties-to-even. Negative zero emits `0`; a mathematically integral double emits the same minimal integer spelling as that value; NaN and either infinity reject. For nonzero magnitude in `[10^-6,10^21)`, emission is ordinary decimal with no leading plus, no superfluous leading/trailing zero, and no decimal point when the fractional suffix is empty. Every other nonzero magnitude uses one nonzero digit before an optional decimal point, lowercase `e`, an explicit `+` only for a nonnegative exponent, and an exponent with no leading zero. This is the ECMAScript/Ryu shortest-round-trip boundary: `1e-6` emits `0.000001`, `1e-7` emits `1e-7`, and `1e21` emits `1e+21`. Swift uses an independently implemented binary64 shortest-round-trip encoder and compares bytes with Node; locale-aware formatters and Firestore serialization bytes are forbidden. Integer-versus-double storage type is validated by each field schema before canonicalization; canonical bytes intentionally normalize numerically equal `1` and `1.0`.

Unless a clause explicitly says policy canonicalization, every phrase in §§3–7 of the form “canonical task document,” “complete canonical task,” “semantic snapshot hash/size,” or “operation record hash/size” means `TaskCanonicalV1`. Firestore transaction storage/index charge is measured only by `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` in §4.6; `TaskCanonicalV1` is not its proxy. Policy fingerprints, policy fixtures, evidence facts that forbid fractions, ordinary command fingerprints, and registry maps remain in the strict integer-only policy/request domain. The policy notes/quotes actions use the strict command envelope plus `TaskCanonicalV1` for their admitted content and complete accepted-request fingerprint. Legacy `REPLACE_QUOTES`, `COMPLETE_BOOKING`, `COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION`, and the bulk `persistLegacyPackingPlan` request use `TaskCanonicalV1` for the entire complete accepted request, including operation ID. In every accepted-request domain, `CallableTimestampWireV1` values remain strings.

The scalar parity bytes are exactly:

```json
{"array":[3,"é",false],"bool":true,"int":7,"null":null,"text":"2026-09-01T12:34:56.789Z","timestamp":{"$timestamp":"2026-09-01T12:34:56.789Z"}}
```

They are 144 UTF-8 bytes with SHA-256 `36f6b4f811149133ae987c544a8359ed485a917bade6934a9f642c2b909eaf3d`.

The source-version parity suite additionally freezes one Firestore update time at seconds `1788266096`, nanoseconds `123456789`, its exact canonical reserved map, and adjacent values `123456788`/`123456790`. All three must produce distinct JS/Swift canonical bytes and hashes even though a `Date` conversion would place them in the same millisecond; any conversion/rounding fails the fixture.

The exact `TaskCanonicalV1` finite-double parity value is:

```json
{"large":1e+21,"negativeZero":0,"one":1,"quarter":0.25,"smallDecimal":0.000001,"smallExponent":1e-7}
```

It is exactly 100 UTF-8 bytes with SHA-256 `567cfbf90b0a4d5fcea2937b8d2c941fb1bc674bfa234dc3859f6a0e1e8cd47a`. JS and Swift additionally fixture the adjacent representable values around every number above, the smallest positive subnormal, the largest finite binary64, negative fractional/exponent forms, integral doubles on both sides of `2^53`, negative zero input, and NaN/infinities. Valid field schemas still reject out-of-range values before these bytes are used.

### 2.2 Exact top-level and member schemas

Required top-level keys are `schema_version`, `policy_version`, `policy_fingerprint`, `capabilities`, `case_key_policy`, `milestone_profiles`, `milestones`, `waiting_transitions`, `handoffs`, `outcome_policies`, `trigger_policies`, `eligible_events`, and `completion_policy`. Optional keys are `not_applicable_policy`, `deadline_policy`, `plan_change_policy`, `disposition_policies`, and `producer_bindings`. No other key is accepted. Generated `dueDate` and move pacing remain non-authoritative: a threshold is usable only through the exact deadline-evidence and derivation closure below.

Unless a field below declares a different literal/registry grammar, every policy-local ID, code, `cta_key`, workflow ID, trigger/threshold ID, profile/milestone/outcome ID, and reference uses ASCII `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$`. Catalog task IDs retain their existing Firestore document-ID grammar and are not policy-local IDs. User-visible labels/copy are never IDs.

| Member | Exact schema |
|---|---|
| version/fingerprint | `schema_version == 1`; `policy_version` integer `1...2147483647`; fingerprint as §2.1 |
| `capabilities` | unique subsequence of `ALREADY_HANDLED, NOT_APPLICABLE, DEFER, SELF_HANDLE, WAIT_EXTERNAL, HANDOFF`; at least one |
| `case_key_policy` | exact `{subject_kind,subject_id_field,institution_id_field?,move_event_id_field?,task_type}`; kind is `household/person/child/pet/vehicle/property/service/account`; subject/institution paths are `id`, `taskId`, `userId`, `subject.id`, `institutionId`, or `supersedes`; move-event path is only `moveEventId`, `subject.moveEventId`, or `spawnedFrom.move_event_id` |
| profile | exact `{id,resolver_id,milestone_ids,companion_ids,resolver_inputs?}`; required IDs are nonempty and companion IDs may be empty; each array is unique, they are disjoint, and every ID resolves; `resolver_inputs` is exact `{match_all:[{field_path,equals}]}` with 1–8 clauses |
| milestone | exact `{id,label,subject_scope,evidence_rule_id}`; scope is `case` or `subject`; the same milestone may be required in one profile and companion in another because outcome applicability is explicit |
| waiting transition | exact `{code,channel_kind,owner_rule,expectation_template_id,trigger_policy_id,external_submission_effect,evidence_rule_id,outcome_policy_id,stuck_policy,second_cycle_channel,display_name?}`; channel kind is `CALL/PORTAL/WEB/MAIL/MAP/SHARE`; optional trusted policy `display_name` is trimmed NFC 1–512 UTF-8 bytes; external effect is `PRESERVE`, `ESTABLISH_FROM_OUTCOME_EVIDENCE`, or `FORBID`; stuck policy is exact `{same_channel_max_cycles:1,first_failure_outcome_id,second_failure_resolver_id:"resolver.waiting_change_channel.v1"}`; `second_cycle_channel` is exact `{transition_code,nudge_template_id}` and resolves as below |
| handoff | exact `{id,cta_key,kind,presentation_mode,expected_action,outcome_policy_id,trigger_policy_id,presenter_cancellation_policy?}`; `cta_key` is a policy ID unique across handoffs; kind is `CALL/PORTAL/WEB/MAIL/MAP/SHARE`; mode is `EXTERNAL_APP/IN_APP_SHEET/SHARE_SHEET`; SHARE/MAIL require cancellation policy `RESTORE_PRIOR|RETURN_FOR_OUTCOME`, every other kind forbids it |
| outcome policy | exact `{id,prompt_kind,outcomes}` with 1–5 primary outcomes; prompt is `CALL/PORTAL/WRITTEN_NOTICE/WAITING/GENERIC`; every outcome accepts optional nonempty unique `profile_ids` in policy profile order, absent meaning every profile |
| trigger policy | exact `{id,allowed_sources,safe_threshold?,hard_threshold?,move_anchor_derivation?,eligible_event_ids}`; each threshold member may carry the optional validated `consequence_class` below and otherwise uses the exact deadline derivation below |
| eligible event | exact `{id,event_name,canonical_key,publisher,evidence_rule}`; Phase 2's publisher registry is empty, so production-valid policies keep this array empty |
| completion | exact `{kind:"ALL_PROFILE_REQUIRED_MILESTONES"}` |
| not applicable | exact `{reason_codes,direct_reason_code,contradiction_evaluator_id,resolver_id,fact_selectors,reason_rules}`; schema v1 requires exactly one uppercase reason code, `direct_reason_code` equals it, and exactly one matching rule; selectors and the rule are exact as defined below |
| deadline | exact `{evidence_rule_id:"evidence.deadline_authority.v1",display_rule:{kind:"EVIDENCE_LABEL_ONLY"}}`; it authorizes no client/task `dueDate` inference and is required by any threshold member |
| disposition policies | exact map containing `defer:{trigger_policy_id}` iff `DEFER` is declared, `self_handle:{trigger_policy_id,next_action_rule_id}` iff `SELF_HANDLE` is declared, and `wait_external:{transition_code}` iff `WAIT_EXTERNAL` is declared; missing/extra branches invalidate the map |
| producer binding | exact `{producer_id,workflow_id,task_selector_id,outcome_policy_id,outcome_id}`; tuples `(producer_id,workflow_id)` are unique; guidance may bind only `COMPLETE_MILESTONES`, while vendor submission may bind only `COMPLETE_MILESTONES` or `WAIT_EXTERNAL` |
| plan change | exact `{replacements:[{catalog_task_id,replacement_policy_fingerprint,amendment_profile_id,amendment_action_handoff_id,amendment_action_trigger_policy_id,original_verification_transition_code,confirmation_outcome_policy_id,confirmation_outcome_id,amendment_anchor_kind}]}`; 1–16 unique catalog IDs; `replacement_policy_fingerprint` uses the exact 69-byte `p2i1_` plus 64 lowercase hex grammar and is expressly exempt from the 64-character policy-local/reference grammar; anchor kind is `NONE`, `MOVE_DATE`, or `ADDRESS`; the verification transition resolves in the current policy; every replacement reference resolves in the exact replacement policy at runtime; the named handoff uses the named trigger |

Every primary outcome has required base keys `{id,label,effect}` and optional `profile_ids`; its complete allowed key set is exactly those base members plus only the members named in its effect row below. ID follows the policy-ID syntax, label is trimmed NFC 1–256 UTF-8 bytes, and effect is `COMPLETE_MILESTONES|WAIT_EXTERNAL|CONTINUE_USER_ACTION|DEFER|REQUIRES_RESOLVER`. `profile_ids`, when present, remains the nonempty unique policy-order subsequence above. A key named optional in the effect row may be absent; every other additional/cross-effect/unknown key is forbidden. Fixtures remove ID/label/effect, use blank/257-byte label, add a surplus key, and move every effect-specific key to every other effect.

Catalog validation computes the complete task-producer closure before accepting a policy. `P = {id,taskId,userId}`, `S = subject.id`, `I = institutionId`, and `X = supersedes`. The exact field-production matrix is:

| Producer context | Legal subject/institution paths | Legal move path | Authority/write |
|---|---|---|---|
| owner TaskGenerationService (`spawnedOnly != true`, no policy on `rowGeneration`) | each selected path must be in `P` | absent or `moveEventId` | catalog ID/task ID, authenticated UID; root active move event |
| generic `spawnTasks` non-subject-aware conversation/nudge/onComplete request | each selected path must be in `P` | absent, `moveEventId`, or `spawnedFrom.move_event_id` | deterministic t1 ID, catalog task ID, authenticated UID, normalized source, root move event |
| generic `spawnTasks` subject-aware request | each selected path must be in `P ∪ {S,I}` | absent, `moveEventId`, `subject.moveEventId`, or `spawnedFrom.move_event_id` | deterministic t2 ID plus normalized caller-declared subject/institution; root move event |
| terminal `onCompleteSpawns` child | each selected path may be in `P ∪ {S,I,X}` | absent or any of the three move paths | deterministic t3/catalog/auth fields; S copies validated source-case subject ID with target subject kind; I copies source-case institution/account ID; X is source task document ID; root move event |
| external H54 replacement | every a2 row unconditionally carries normalized S, I, and X; selector paths may be in `P ∪ {S,I,X}` | absent or any of the three move paths | deterministic a2/catalog/auth fields; `subject:{kind,id}` and `institutionId` copy the normalized replacement descriptor, `supersedes` and `spawnedFrom.id` equal the original task document ID; root move event |

`subject_id_field` and optional `institution_id_field` are validated independently against the row; selecting the same path for both intentionally yields the same canonical value. A producer creates `subject:{kind,id}` exactly when S or `subject.moveEventId` is needed, **except that every H54 a2 replacement requires it regardless of selector use**; generic/H54 subject kind must equal the policy's `subject_kind`, and a terminal child requiring S must have the same subject kind as its initialized source case. A producer creates `institutionId` exactly when I is selected or existing legacy projection requires it, **except that every H54 a2 replacement requires it**; terminal use requires a present source-case institution/account ID. It creates `supersedes` exactly when X is selected and only in terminal/H54 modes, **except that every H54 a2 replacement requires it and requires the same original task ID at `spawnedFrom.id`**. Only the move-event alias remains selector-conditional for a2. `spawnedFrom.move_event_id`, `subject.moveEventId`, and root `moveEventId` are each present exactly when selected and all surplus move aliases are forbidden. Every move writer transactionally rereads root `activeMoveEventId`; terminal/H54 additionally require any initialized source-case move value to match it. Validator and fixture closure include a2 policies that select and omit each S/I/X path and require the four unconditional provenance fields byte-identically in both cases.

A statically reachable row must satisfy every applicable fixed producer context: an owner-reachable row cannot select S/I/X or a nested move path even if it is also referenced elsewhere. Generic request mode is validated dynamically before any read/write: a policy requiring S/I/subject-move rejects a non-subject-aware request rather than invalidating the subject-aware mode. Seeder graph closure validates owner eligibility, every `onCompleteSpawns` edge, every plan-change replacement, and valid target fingerprints; the generic callable applies the same validator at runtime. It rejects an unproducible path combination, absent source authority, kind mismatch, mixed subject-aware modes, target fingerprint mismatch, or catalog/user move-event byte. Fixtures exercise every legal subject+institution+move combination per producer, fixed-context intersections, both generic modes, all impossible combinations, forged/surplus paths, first reducer initialization, and reset rotation.

`profile.single_case.v1` is legal only with exactly one profile and no `resolver_inputs`. `profile.task_field_match.v1` requires at least two profiles; every profile has `resolver_inputs.match_all`, whose field paths are `task.taskType`, `task.workflowId`, `task.category`, `task.actionCategory`, `case.subject_kind`, or `case.task_type`. `equals` is Boolean, safe integer, or an NFC string of at most 512 bytes; null is not a legal comparator. A path absence is the distinct result `MISSING`, which matches no clause. Stored Firestore null is invalid in every owner/server policy projection and also matches no clause. Exactly one profile must match immutable task/case facts; zero or multiple matches fails without mutation.

`not_applicable_policy.fact_selectors` contains 1–16 exact `{id,source,field_path}` maps. `source` is `TASK`, `IDENTITY`, or `USER_KNOWLEDGE`. `TASK` permits only `id`, `taskId`, `taskType`, `workflowId`, `category`, `actionCategory`, `moveEventId`, `subject.id`, `subject.kind`, `subject.moveEventId`, `institutionId`, `supersedes`, `spawnedFrom.id`, `spawnedFrom.move_event_id`, or `spawnedFrom.source_task_instance_id`. The Firestore snapshot document ID is deliberately not a selectable virtual field; policy tasks already store and freeze `id`, and a stored `documentId` key is forbidden. `IDENTITY` reads `users/{uid}/identity/identity`; its path has 1–8 identifier segments. `USER_KNOWLEDGE` reads `userKnowledge/{uid}.entries[firstSegment].value` followed by at most seven declared nested segments. All three use the Firestore document snapshot update time as source version; an owner-supplied nested `updatedAt` is data only and never authority. Segment syntax is `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}$`. The evaluator reads each distinct document once inside the command transaction. Missing document/path, unsupported value type, or missing snapshot update time yields `unknown`; raw selected values never enter evidence or a receipt. Forged/reused nested timestamps, concurrent edits, and all three spawned-task selector paths are mandatory tests. This three-source set is exhaustive; assessment, roster, or typed-evidence selectors remain unavailable until a later owner amendment freezes a literal document identity, version authority, privacy projection, producer availability, and tests.

Catalog validation intersects every required `TASK` selector with every statically reachable producer projection; a fixed context is invalid if any required path is absent. Generic requests perform the same intersection against their selected request mode before any write. This table is exhaustive; a parenthesized field is available only when the catalog/policy/request condition is true:

| Producer context | Unconditionally available `TASK` paths | Conditionally available paths |
|---|---|---|
| owner | `id,taskId,taskType,category,actionCategory` | `workflowId` iff projected from catalog; `moveEventId` iff it is the selected move path |
| generic non-subject t1 | owner base plus `spawnedFrom.id` | `workflowId`; `moveEventId` or `spawnedFrom.move_event_id` exactly when selected |
| generic subject-aware t2 | generic t1 base plus `subject.id,subject.kind,institutionId` | `workflowId`; exactly one selected move alias among `moveEventId,subject.moveEventId,spawnedFrom.move_event_id` |
| terminal t3 | owner base plus `spawnedFrom.id,spawnedFrom.source_task_instance_id` | `workflowId`; `subject.id,subject.kind` iff copied from source authority; `institutionId` iff copied from source authority; `supersedes` iff selected; exactly one selected move alias |
| H54 a2 | owner base plus `subject.id,subject.kind,institutionId,supersedes,spawnedFrom.id` | `workflowId`; exactly one selected move alias; `spawnedFrom.source_task_instance_id` is never available |

For fixed owner, terminal, and H54 graph edges, seeder validation requires every selector in every reachable policy to occur in that row of the table, including the conditional prerequisite. Terminal runtime revalidates the frozen source authority. For generic targets the callable rejects the non-subject mode when any selected path requires subject/institution authority, and rejects either mode when a required conditional catalog field is absent. Impossible selector/producer combinations invalidate the policy or request; they do not initialize a permanently `unknown` task. Missing `IDENTITY` or `USER_KNOWLEDGE` data remains intentionally runtime-unknown. Fixtures provide one success and one unavailable-path rejection for every selector in each producer row, plus mixed fixed-context reachability.

`reason_rules` contains the one exact `{reason_code,required_fact_ids,contradiction_if_any}` map for the sole reason. Required fact IDs are nonempty, unique, and resolve. `contradiction_if_any` has 1–8 exact predicates `{fact_id,operator,value?}`; every predicate fact is required. `EQUALS`, `NOT_EQUALS`, and `CONTAINS` require a null/Boolean/safe-integer/NFC-string `value`; `EXISTS` forbids it. `CONTAINS` accepts only an array of those scalar values. Once every required fact and version resolves, any true predicate means `present`; no true predicate means `none`.

The evaluator orders required selectors by their declaration order and forms `source_ref = "cr1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical [{selector_id,source,document_path,field_path}]))`, `source_version = "cv1_" + SHA-256(canonical [{document_path,snapshot_update_time}])`, and `facts = {reason_code,selector_ids,result,selection_digest}` where `selection_digest` is lowercase SHA-256 over canonical `[{selector_id,canonical_selected_value}]`. Duplicate source documents appear once in the source-version array at first selector occurrence; their single transaction snapshot supplies every selector on that document. The operation stores this exact evaluation tuple for `present`; a successful `none` terminalization also stores it as `evidence.contradiction_evaluation.v1`. Neither record stores a raw selected value or user-facing source path. A changed snapshot update time changes `source_version` and the evidence ID. This is the complete composite encoding for TASK/IDENTITY/USER_KNOWLEDGE selectors; no client fact or absence is affirmative evidence.

`not_applicable_policy.direct_reason_code` is the one-tap button binding. The client sends exactly it; no label, array position, or “first” rule chooses a reason. A request naming any other reason is invalid. Likewise `disposition_policies.wait_external.transition_code` is the direct WAIT binding and must resolve exactly once. `markWaitingOnExternal` must name that code; other transition codes are reachable only through their bound outcomes. Thus policy multiplicity never makes a direct payload ambiguous.

For a `present` evaluator result, `evaluatorDigest = "ced1_" + SHA-256(canonical {source_ref,source_version,facts})`, using the exact composite strings/facts above and all 64 lowercase hex characters. The contradiction resolver receipt, its stored ordinary operation response, and a later choice all carry that exact value; recomputation from any changed selector order/value/update time fails. JS cross-module fixtures freeze the full digest for TASK-only, shared-document, and three-source evaluations.

Every waiting transition uses `evidence.wait_entry.v1` only when a trusted display name resolves. At entry time the server first uses the pinned transition's optional policy `display_name`; otherwise, in the same transaction, it reads only `providerDirectory/{case_key.institution_or_account_id}` and accepts only a document whose path ID, stored trimmed `providerId`, and case ID are byte-identical, whose literal `source == "seeded"`, whose immutable snapshot has an update time, and whose trimmed NFC `name` is 1–512 UTF-8 bytes. Policy authority produces source `{kind:"POLICY",policy_fingerprint,transition_code}`. Directory authority produces `{kind:"PROVIDER_DIRECTORY",provider_id,source_ref:R("pdr1_",{document_path}),source_version:V("pdv1_",{snapshot_update_time,provider_id,source,name})}`. The task's `institution`, an opaque case ID such as `mapkit:chase`, `reportedFacts`, `presentation_snapshot.provider_display_name` or `source_refs`, caller strings, a directory row with `source:"resolved"`, and every missing/malformed/mismatched row are display context only and never authority. Existing rules keep `providerDirectory/**` authenticated-readable but client-write-denied; the rules suite reasserts both branches and direct-write denial, so only backend-owned seeded evidence can qualify.

A direct WAIT operation with resolved display authority creates an operation-bound WAIT_ENTRY from its authenticated transition selection, bounded facts, confirmed-trigger evidence, exact display name, and exact source union and never establishes external submission. An outcome-backed entry creates the same composite record but must reference its matching handoff/workflow outcome evidence; only that form can establish external submission. `PRESERVE` never establishes it. `ESTABLISH_FROM_OUTCOME_EVIDENCE` establishes it only for the outcome-backed form sourced by `evidence.handoff_outcome.v1` or `evidence.workflow_submission.v1`; direct waiting and later same-channel renewal preserve the prior value. `FORBID` rejects entry when the value is already established. A transition naming any other evidence rule is structurally invalid. Every `second_cycle_channel.transition_code` resolves to exactly one different waiting transition in the same policy whose `channel_kind` differs from the source transition; it may point back only when validating that target's own independent second-cycle edge, never to itself. Exactly one declared handoff must have that target channel kind, a compatible prompt/mode, and an outcome policy that can enter the target transition for every source-applicable profile; its `action_id` is the deterministic TRY_CHANNEL action and is returned in the resolver receipt, never selected by label/array position. `nudge_template_id` resolves in the closed registry and is copied into the second-cycle receipt/evidence. Missing, duplicate-action, same-channel, dangling, or cyclic self-edge data is structurally invalid; a two-transition A↔B alternate graph is legal because each edge changes channel.

If neither trusted display source resolves, the transition is ineligible for actual WAIT but the already-selected semantic outcome remains owned: the reducer applies its declared milestone sets and explicit external-submission effect, retains the exact newly confirmed trigger and evidence, creates `WAIT_DISPLAY_FALLBACK` evidence, creates no WAIT_ENTRY/`waiting_state`, and writes `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED` with owner `user:{uid}`, next action `Check for an update`, the same `next_trigger`, destination `row`, and visible copy `You’re handling this`. Direct entry has no source evidence; outcome entry retains its qualifying typed source. This total branch applies to `markWaitingOnExternal`, handoff/waiting/workflow outcomes, and H54 original verification. It is not validated through `disposition_policies.self_handle`: its required `waiting_fallback_state` binds the contract trigger to the selected transition. It has no WAIT exits or Chat draft; its scheduler wake uses the USER_ACTION row route. Exact operation/submission replay retains the chosen fallback even if directory data later appears. A later fresh command may enter WAIT only after trusted authority exists.

Waiting closure is checked per profile, not merely per outcome-policy object. For every profile that can enter a transition through its direct disposition binding, a handoff outcome, or a workflow binding, the transition's WAITING prompt policy must contain both: (a) its exact `stuck_policy.first_failure_outcome_id`, applicable to that profile, whose effect is WAIT_EXTERNAL back to the same transition and is callable only by the post-Chat renewal coordinator at live cycle `0`; and (b) at least one distinct applicable non-renewal outcome whose effect is COMPLETE_MILESTONES, CONTINUE_USER_ACTION, DEFER, or REQUIRES_RESOLVER and whose ordinary structural closure is valid for that profile. The named renewal is the only `WAIT_EXTERNAL` member permitted in that `prompt_kind:"WAITING"` outcome policy across all profiles. The renewal ID may not serve as the second branch, and an outcome that waits on this or another transition is not a non-renewal exit. The visible WAITING surface never renders the hidden renewal as a third action: it renders exactly two fixed one-tap exits, `It happened` (which opens only the applicable nonrenewal outcome question) and `It's stuck` (which opens the exact local prefilled Chat draft with zero server/support mutation). On return from Chat, cycle `0` may open the shared trigger selector for the hidden same-channel renewal once; cancellation preserves WAIT/attention. At cycle `1`, the recovery control is the required single `TRY_CHANNEL` descriptor from `second_cycle_channel`, never another same-channel renewal. Policies with renewal-only loops, an additional same/cross-transition WAIT outcome, an exit applicable to the wrong profile, dangling renewal/alternate/template IDs, or an exit that cannot reach an owned/terminal state are invalid. Fixtures cover the exact two visible exits, every effect family, multi-profile applicability, forged outcome IDs, an extra WAIT member, Chat cancellation/no write, prefilled context bounds, renewal plus each non-renewal branch at cycle 0/1, and deterministic alternate-channel transition.

Outcome members are exact by effect:

| Effect | Required additional keys | Runtime rule |
|---|---|---|
| `COMPLETE_MILESTONES` | at least one member across `milestone_ids?`, `not_required_milestones?`, and `companion_milestone_ids?`; `external_submission_effect:"PRESERVE"|"ESTABLISH_FROM_BOUND_EVIDENCE"` | close/waive the named required milestones and close named companions with the current outcome evidence; preserve any already-closed member's original evidence; apply the explicit submission effect; if any required member remains OPEN, reject with zero mutation; otherwise terminalize |
| `WAIT_EXTERNAL` | required `transition_code`; optional `milestone_ids`, `not_required_milestones`, `companion_milestone_ids` | apply the three exact milestone sets, require one required milestone still OPEN, and use one newly confirmed transition trigger; trusted display enters actual WAIT, otherwise the same semantic effect takes the exact owned USER_ACTION fallback |
| `CONTINUE_USER_ACTION` | required `next_action_rule_id`; optional `milestone_ids`, `not_required_milestones`, `companion_milestone_ids`; `external_submission_effect:"PRESERVE"|"ESTABLISH_FROM_BOUND_EVIDENCE"` | apply the three exact milestone sets, apply the explicit submission effect, require one required milestone OPEN, resolve a concrete action, and require an unfired stored fallback or newly confirmed trigger |
| `DEFER` | `external_submission_effect:"PRESERVE"` | close no milestone and require a newly confirmed trigger |
| `REQUIRES_RESOLVER` | `resolver_id`; `external_submission_effect:"PRESERVE"` | first response is a receipt-backed resolver descriptor with zero task mutation; a new operation submits a valid resolver choice |

`milestone_ids` and `companion_milestone_ids` are unique ID arrays. `not_required_milestones` is a unique-by-ID array of exact `{milestone_id,reason_code}` maps; `reason_code` matches `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,63}$`. The three ID sets are pairwise disjoint. Runtime first requires the selected profile be in `profile_ids` (or the member be absent), then applies only IDs in that selected profile. Structural validation forbids an outcome ID whose required/companion role does not match every profile to which that outcome applies. Only OPEN members are writable. Required completion writes state `COMPLETED` plus the new wrapper evidence ID; a waiver writes state `NOT_REQUIRED`, the wrapper ID, `not_required_reason` from the policy member, and `not_required_source` equal to that wrapper's typed backing evidence ID; companion closure writes only `COMPLETED` plus the wrapper ID. Every already-COMPLETED or NOT_REQUIRED required member and every completed companion is preserved byte-for-byte regardless of what a later reachable outcome names, so no sequence can overwrite evidence or dead-end solely because a prior outcome used another closure kind/reason. The wrapper's three effect arrays contain only members newly changed by this invocation, in policy order; preserved members are omitted. Companions never become NOT_REQUIRED. DEFER and REQUIRES_RESOLVER forbid all three milestone fields. Tests cover WAIT/CONTINUE→terminal with the same and different requested kind/reason, no overwrite, empty applied arrays when all members were already closed, and successful terminal closure.

WAIT_EXTERNAL forbids an outcome-level submission field because its referenced waiting transition is the sole authority. `ESTABLISH_FROM_BOUND_EVIDENCE` is legal only at a handoff outcome with matching `evidence.handoff_outcome.v1` or a vendor workflow binding with matching `evidence.workflow_submission.v1`; guidance, direct disposition, label/milestone inference, and caller Booleans cannot establish it. PRESERVE retains an existing fact and creates none. A terminal COMPLETE archives whichever monotonic fact exists after this explicit rule. Structural validation rejects an establishment effect with no reachable qualifying handoff/producer and tests COMPLETE/CONTINUE/DEFER/WAIT plus preexisting-fact preservation.

Trigger sources are a nonempty unique subsequence of `PROMISED_DATE, ACCEPTANCE_DATE, SAFE_THRESHOLD, USER_PICKED, LABELED_ESTIMATE, EVENT` in that exact order. `safe_threshold` and `hard_threshold` are independently optional exact `{threshold_id,derivation_id:"trigger.deadline_offset.v1",offset_seconds,protected_outcome,label,consequence_class?}` maps; IDs are unique in the trigger policy, offsets are safe integers `-31536000...0`, and labels are trimmed NFC 1–256 bytes. A present `consequence_class` is a lowercase registry ID and must resolve in the closed `CONSEQUENCE_CLASS_RANKS_V1` server registry to exactly one safe nonnegative integer rank. When both thresholds are present, their class is either absent on both or byte-identical on both; mixed presence/value is invalid. Either threshold requires top-level `deadline_policy`. When both exist, they require the same protected outcome and `safe_threshold.offset_seconds <= hard_threshold.offset_seconds <= 0`. Because task state has one deadline-evidence slot, every threshold member across every trigger policy in one task policy must name the same `protected_outcome`; mixed outcomes are structurally invalid. SAFE_THRESHOLD requires `safe_threshold`; USER_PICKED also requires it and may follow SAFE_THRESHOLD in the source order whether or not SAFE_THRESHOLD itself is offered. A present `safe_threshold` forbids LABELED_ESTIMATE and `move_anchor_derivation`; the labeled estimate is available only when no safe threshold exists. LABELED_ESTIMATE requires `move_anchor_derivation` and is the final date source. EVENT requires nonempty `eligible_event_ids`, while its absence requires that array empty. The move derivation is exact `{derivation_id:"trigger.labeled_move_anchor_offset.v1",anchor,offset_seconds,protected_outcome,label}`; anchor is only `MOVE_DATE` or `DUE_DATE`, offset is `-31536000...31536000`, and both label and UI identify the result as an estimate. A move/due anchor is never deadline authority.

`CONSEQUENCE_CLASS_RANKS_V1` is an immutable server-source registry, not caller/catalog free text. Its Phase 2 production value is exactly the empty map `{}`; therefore all Phase 2 production-valid policies omit `consequence_class` and use the A12-12 interim nearest-threshold ordering. Validator and grouping code nevertheless implement the optional branch against an injected immutable registry and the offline parity fixture uses exact test registry `{"fixture_consequence_first":0,"fixture_consequence_second":1}`. Phase 3 may activate real classes only by one separately reviewed source change that adds the closed IDs/ranks and policy data together; neither this specification nor UI labels invent a real-world rank. Missing, duplicate-rank, negative/unsafe rank, unknown ID, or policy/evidence rank drift fails closed.

The two pacing anchors have no fallback chain. `MOVE_DATE` reads only `users/{uid}/identity/identity` field `moveDate`; `DUE_DATE` reads only the current `users/{uid}/tasks/{taskDocumentId}` field `dueDate`. The selected field must be a Firestore Timestamp whose millisecond value is representable as a JavaScript `Date`; strings, numbers, nested alternatives, `user_assessments`, catalog dates, and locally calculated dates are invalid. The source version is that exact document snapshot's Firestore update time. The Swift selector reads the same literal document/field, computes `anchor + offset_seconds`, and offers it only while its `DispositionDraftKey` is current and the result is strictly future. It sends only the computed `at` with literal empty `derivation_inputs`; the server transaction rereads the same source, requires a snapshot update time, creates `PACING_ANCHOR` evidence, recomputes the result, and requires exact millisecond equality. Missing document/field, wrong type, absent update time, changed anchor, changed task instance, or a now-nonfuture result removes the client candidate or rejects fresh submission with zero mutation. Tests cover identity-only move date, the deliberately ignored assessment fallback, task due date, both wrong types, source changes between display and submit, unrelated task update changing the DUE_DATE source version, and exact replay after a later source change.

`deadline_evidence` is usable only as exact server-owned state `{evidence_id,deadline_at,protected_outcome,label,observed_at}` whose ID resolves to an `evidence.deadline_authority.v1` record, whose outcome equals the policy threshold outcome, and whose policy fingerprint/case/task instance are current. Its sole source seam is client-denied `users/{uid}/taskDeadlineEvidence/{task_instance_id}`, exact `{schema_version:1,task_document_id,task_instance_id,policy_fingerprint,case_key_digest,authority_source_id,authority_source_version,deadline_at,protected_outcome,label,observed_at}`. A future trusted importer must write that source document and the matching bounded task-state evidence record atomically; the client can then read the bounded task state but never the authority document. `taskDisposition.js` transactionally rereads the source document and requires every identity/policy/case/version/value byte to match the already-embedded state before accepting a threshold. Missing, malformed, stale, or mismatched data yields no candidate. `trigger.deadline_offset.v1` recomputes `deadline_at + offset_seconds`; a fresh selected result must be strictly future. Phase 2 has no client, catalog, `dueDate`, move-date, model, seeder, or production importer for this collection; offline reducer/rules tests seed both documents atomically under the demo project. A later trusted server import needs separate authorization. Production policies may declare the shape, but the selector remains hidden until valid task state exists. This is a deliberate capability gate, not an unowned fallback.

Every reference resolves exactly once. Every declared milestone appears in at least one profile. Local IDs and handoff `cta_key` values are unique within their namespace. Outcome profile applicability resolves exactly; for every applicable profile, completed/waived IDs have the required role and companion IDs have the companion role. A COMPLETE outcome's completed-plus-not-required required IDs must equal each profile to which that outcome applies; profiles with different sets therefore use distinct profile-scoped outcomes rather than intersections or ignored IDs. For every profile that can select a handoff or waiting transition, its referenced outcome policy has at least one primary outcome applicable to that profile. The stuck-renewal outcome is applicable to every profile that can enter its transition. A workflow producer binding is not profile-scoped, so its bound outcome must apply to every declared profile; runtime still verifies the selected profile before mutation. Waiting `first_failure_outcome_id` resolves inside its named waiting outcome policy to WAIT_EXTERNAL on the same transition, and its second resolver is exactly `resolver.waiting_change_channel.v1`. A WAIT outcome referenced by a handoff must name a transition whose `channel_kind` equals that handoff's kind; a waiting policy's renewal outcome must name its own transition. Sharing a WAIT outcome policy across different channel kinds is invalid. Unused policy objects are invalid. HANDOFF requires a handoff plus referenced outcome/trigger policies; WAIT_EXTERNAL requires a transition and its direct disposition binding; DEFER and SELF_HANDLE require their explicit disposition branch; NOT_APPLICABLE requires its complete single reason/rule. A catalog row with `rowGeneration` must reject `taskInteraction`.

Structural validation also computes producer closure per profile. The only v1 milestone evidence rules are `evidence.explicit_user_confirmation.v1` and `evidence.policy_outcome.v1`. Every outcome-named milestone must use the latter. `assertAlreadyHandled` creates one explicit-confirmation record; a milestone using that rule references it directly, while a milestone using policy-outcome receives a server-created `evidence.policy_outcome.v1` wrapper whose exact encoding is in §2.3. Handoff/wait/workflow/amendment outcomes always use a policy-outcome wrapper backed by their typed source evidence. For every profile-required milestone, at least one reachable enabled terminal producer must close it as COMPLETED or NOT_REQUIRED compatibly: ALREADY_HANDLED, a COMPLETE outcome applicable to that profile and reachable from a declared handoff/waiting transition/producer binding, or an amendment confirmation path. Every companion role in every profile must likewise have at least one reachable applicable policy-outcome writer, although it does not enter terminal closure. Reachability follows typed references only; labels and unused objects do not count. A required milestone with no terminal writer, a companion with no writer, an outcome naming an explicit-confirmation-only milestone, an unbacked waiver, or a producer/evidence mismatch is structurally invalid. Fixtures cover different multi-profile sets, each producer family, companion closure/no-writer, policy waiver, and required no-writer/mismatched-rule failures.

For each outcome and direct whole-case assertion, structural validation constructs the maximum exact `POLICY_OUTCOME` facts map that its declared IDs/reasons can emit, including the fixed fields and a 59-byte evidence ID, and requires canonical size at most 16,384 bytes. It performs the analogous maximum-row check for every other policy-dependent evidence constructor. A policy whose legal effect could exceed a row cap is invalid before fingerprint acceptance; runtime never discovers that its sole producer is unencodable.

`ContextualOutcome`, the waiting outcome surface, and the workflow result surface render only primary outcomes applicable to the task state's already-selected profile, preserving policy order. They never display a profile-inapplicable choice and the server rejects a forged one. A valid policy/action whose applicable filtered list is empty is structurally impossible; malformed persisted policy/state renders the control passive. Fixtures cover different per-profile labels/effects, filtered ordering, one reachable outcome per profile, profile-inapplicable forgery, a stuck transition missing one profile's renewal, and an all-profile workflow binding that points at a scoped outcome.

Handoff/outcome compatibility is exact: CALL handoffs require prompt CALL; PORTAL and WEB require PORTAL; SHARE and MAIL require WRITTEN_NOTICE; MAP requires GENERIC. A waiting transition requires prompt WAITING. No outcome policy may be shared across incompatible prompt classes. Resolver subchoices are not primary outcomes and do not count against the five-primary cap.

For every plan-change entry, the replacement handoff ID resolves in the pinned replacement policy, its `trigger_policy_id` equals `amendment_action_trigger_policy_id`, and its expected action is the replacement USER_ACTION copy. The confirmation outcome resolves in that replacement policy, is COMPLETE_MILESTONES, applies to `amendment_profile_id`, closes/waives that profile's entire required set, and is reachable from at least one declared replacement handoff, waiting transition, or workflow producer binding with compatible typed evidence. A resolver, label, unreachable object, partial outcome, or direct client confirmation cannot satisfy this closure.

Registry references are also use-site typed:

| Registry family/value | Only legal use site and prerequisite |
|---|---|
| `owner.case_institution.v1` | waiting transition whose case policy declares `institution_id_field`; direct and outcome entry both fail structural validation otherwise |
| `owner.authenticated_user.v1` | DEFER/SELF_HANDLE reducers only |
| expectation templates | waiting transition only; its reported-fact key set must match §2.3 |
| `next_action.bounded_user_text.v1` | SELF_HANDLE disposition or CONTINUE outcome whose wire input supplies `nextAction` |
| `next_action.handoff_expected_action.v1` | CONTINUE outcome inside a policy referenced by a handoff; runtime requires a matching returned/pending action |
| `resolver.not_applicable_conflict.v1` | only `not_applicable_policy.resolver_id`, with SELF_HANDLE closure |
| `resolver.handoff_failure.v1` | only REQUIRES_RESOLVER inside an outcome policy referenced by at least one handoff |
| `resolver.waiting_change_channel.v1` | only `waiting_transition.stuck_policy.second_failure_resolver_id` |
| `evidence.wait_entry.v1` | every waiting transition and no milestone |
| `evidence.policy_outcome.v1` | milestone evidence rule or internal reduced-outcome evidence only; never direct WAIT authority |
| profile/trigger/producer IDs | only their same-named typed member/binding and with every required source/binding present |

Wrong-site registry IDs, missing institution fields, handoff-next-action without a handoff, and dangling/zero applicable resolver choices are invalid-fixture cases before fingerprint checking.

The Phase 2 CTA adapter registry is closed:

| Adapter site | Exact `cta_key` | Required kind | Required mode |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ProviderActionCard` phone primary | `provider_call` | `CALL` | `EXTERNAL_APP` |
| `ProviderActionCard` provider portal primary | `provider_link` | `PORTAL` | `IN_APP_SHEET` |
| `ProviderActionCard` written-notice primary | `provider_mail` | `MAIL` | `IN_APP_SHEET` |
| `ProviderActionCard` defensive citation | `provider_citation` | `WEB` | `EXTERNAL_APP` |
| task-detail trip-kit directions | `task_directions` | `MAP` | `EXTERNAL_APP` |
| task-detail catalog deep link | `task_deep_link` | `WEB` | `EXTERNAL_APP` |
| movers submitted-inventory share | `movers_inventory_share` | `SHARE` | `SHARE_SHEET` |

Any other `cta_key` is structurally invalid in v1. A mount emits only its literal above and enables only when exactly one policy handoff has that key and exact kind/mode. `provider_mail` is rendered by the new embedded composer inside `TaskExternalActionButton.swift`; it does not require another source path. Zero, duplicate, or mismatched matches render the external control passive; they never fall back to a different handoff. Research sources, scanner/Inventory share, Settings, paywall/legal, and system-remediation URLs are the explicit untracked exclusions in §8.5.

### 2.3 Closed server registry

`functions/taskInteraction.js` owns these IDs. Any addition is a later source change.

| Registry IDs | Exact behavior |
|---|---|
| profile | `profile.single_case.v1` and `profile.task_field_match.v1` behave exactly as §2.2 |
| evidence | the closed rule set and every canonical writer encoding are frozen in the table below; no module may hand-build an evidence ID or substitute a prose/label-derived fact |
| owner | `owner.authenticated_user.v1` yields `user:{uid}`; `owner.case_institution.v1` yields `institution:{case_key.institution_or_account_id}` and fails if absent |
| expectation | the five exact templates and fact/copy rules immediately below; unknown facts reject rather than entering copy |
| consequence class | immutable `CONSEQUENCE_CLASS_RANKS_V1`; production Phase 2 is exactly `{}` and the offline two-class injected fixture is the only Phase 2 ranked test authority; §2.2 defines activation/versioning |
| next action | `next_action.bounded_user_text.v1` accepts trimmed 1–500 UTF-8 bytes; `next_action.handoff_expected_action.v1` copies the pinned handoff's visible `expected_action` byte-for-byte |
| resolver | `resolver.handoff_failure.v1` permits `TRY_ALTERNATE`, `DO_LATER`, `OPEN_CHAT`; `resolver.not_applicable_conflict.v1` permits edit underlying fact, self-handle, or return; `resolver.waiting_change_channel.v1` returns exactly one `TRY_CHANNEL` choice derived from the current transition's `second_cycle_channel` and forbids caller-selected action/template/channel values |
| nudge | the sole ID is `nudge.waiting_follow_up.v1`, exact template `Following up about {task_title}. Please confirm the current status and next step.` with the sole placeholder `task_title`; rendering substitutes the immutable trimmed task title, rejects a title over 256 UTF-8 bytes, and is capped at 512 UTF-8 bytes |
| trigger | `trigger.labeled_move_anchor_offset.v1` recomputes only a policy-declared MOVE_DATE/DUE_DATE pacing offset and always labels it an estimate; `trigger.deadline_offset.v1` recomputes only from current `evidence.deadline_authority.v1` state |
| producer | `producer.workflow_guidance_complete.v1` binds only `COMPLETE_MILESTONES`; `producer.workflow_vendor_submission.v1` binds only `COMPLETE_MILESTONES` or `WAIT_EXTERNAL`; both apply only their exact binding and `task_selector.explicit_task_document.v1` is defined in §4.5 |

`functions/taskInteraction.js` exports one shared evidence constructor used by `taskDisposition.js`, `taskPlan.js`, and `getWorkflowQualifying.js`. Define `R(tag,value) = tag + first40(SHA-256(canonical(value)))` and `V(tag,value) = tag + SHA-256(canonical(value))`. Every record uses exact keys `{id,kind,interaction_epoch,producer,source_ref,source_version,observed_at,facts}`; `id` is derived by §3.1 after all seven non-ID members are frozen. `interaction_epoch` is the safe nonnegative prospective post-mutation task epoch for an epoch-advancing transaction and the unchanged current task epoch for a revision-only or zero-task-mutation operation. `observed_at` is the transaction's server time. The following rows are exhaustive; optional fact keys are present exactly under the condition stated, and arrays are in policy/source declaration order:

| Evidence rule / `kind` | Writer and exact `producer` | Exact `source_ref`; `source_version`; `facts` |
|---|---|---|
| `evidence.explicit_user_confirmation.v1` / `EXPLICIT_USER_CONFIRMATION` | `taskDisposition.js`; `producer.task_disposition.v1` | `R("or1_",{operation_id})`; `V("rv1_",canonical_request)`; `{confirmation:true,closed_milestone_ids}` |
| `evidence.task_identity_projection.v1` / `TASK_IDENTITY_PROJECTION` | first reducer initialization; the invoking module's producer ID | `R("tr1_",{uid,task_document_id,task_instance_id})`; `V("iv1_",immutable_projection)`; `{case_key_digest,milestone_profile_id,subject_keys_digest}` |
| `evidence.user_reported_date.v1` / `USER_REPORTED_DATE` | disposition, task-plan, or workflow reducer; respectively `producer.task_disposition.v1`, `producer.task_plan.v1`, or the bound workflow producer | `R("or1_",{request_identity})`; `V("rv1_",canonical_request)`; `{source,at,reported_at,bounded_facts_digest}` |
| `evidence.pacing_anchor.v1` / `PACING_ANCHOR` | trigger resolver; invoking reducer producer | `R("ar1_",{document_path,field_path})`; `V("av1_",{snapshot_update_time})`; `{anchor,at}` |
| `evidence.deadline_authority.v1` / `DEADLINE_AUTHORITY` | server-only fixture/import seam; `producer.trusted_deadline_import.v1` | `R("dr1_",{authority_source_id,case_key_digest})`; `V("dv1_",{authority_source_version})`; `{deadline_at,protected_outcome,label}`; consequence class is deliberately policy-owned and absent here |
| `evidence.confirmed_trigger.v1` / `CONFIRMED_TRIGGER` | invoking reducer; same producer as its command/outcome | underlying report/anchor/deadline/event evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; exact `{kind,trigger_policy_id,source,at,confirmed_at,source_evidence_id,derivation_id?,threshold_id?,protected_outcome?,consequence_class?}` for a date, with class present exactly for a threshold whose current policy member has one, or `{kind,trigger_policy_id,eligible_event_id,event_name,canonical_key,after_source_version,confirmed_at,source_evidence_id}` for an event |
| `evidence.wait_entry.v1` / `WAIT_ENTRY` | disposition/outcome reducer; invoking producer | confirmed-trigger evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; `{transition_code,channel_kind,entry_kind:"DIRECT"|"OUTCOME",trigger_evidence_id,reported_facts_digest,display_name,display_name_source,source_evidence_id?}` where display/source are the exact trusted union above, the digest is over canonical `reportedFacts` including `{}`, and source evidence is required exactly for OUTCOME |
| `evidence.wait_display_fallback.v1` / `WAIT_DISPLAY_FALLBACK` | disposition/outcome reducer; invoking producer | confirmed-trigger evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; `{transition_code,outcome_policy_id,entry_kind:"DIRECT"|"OUTCOME",trigger_evidence_id,reported_facts_digest,reason:"TRUSTED_DISPLAY_NAME_UNAVAILABLE",source_evidence_id?}` where source evidence is required exactly for OUTCOME |
| `evidence.handoff_lifecycle.v1` / `HANDOFF_LIFECYCLE` | `taskDisposition.js`; `producer.task_disposition.v1` | `R("hr1_",{session_id,adapter_operation_id})`; `V("hv1_",{session_id,prior_state,event_kind})`; exact lifecycle adapter evidence plus `{session_id,event_kind}` |
| `evidence.user_confirmed_handoff_return.v1` / `USER_CONFIRMED_HANDOFF_RETURN` | `taskDisposition.js`; `producer.task_disposition.v1` | opened lifecycle evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; `{session_id,adapter_operation_id,observed_at,opened_evidence_id,asserts_institutional_success:false}` |
| `evidence.handoff_outcome.v1` / `HANDOFF_OUTCOME` | `taskDisposition.js`; `producer.task_disposition.v1` | return evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; `{session_id,outcome_policy_id,outcome_id,return_evidence_id}` |
| `evidence.waiting_outcome.v1` / `WAITING_OUTCOME` | `taskDisposition.js`; `producer.task_disposition.v1` | current WAIT_ENTRY evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; `{transition_code,outcome_policy_id,outcome_id,operation_id,entry_evidence_id,same_channel_cycle_count}` |
| `evidence.workflow_submission.v1` / `WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION` | `getWorkflowQualifying.js`; bound guidance/vendor producer ID | workflow submission document ID; `V("wv1_",canonical_request)`; `{workflow_id,submission_token_hash,outcome_policy_id,outcome_id}` |
| `evidence.policy_outcome.v1` / `POLICY_OUTCOME` | invoking outcome reducer; same producer as its typed source | typed source evidence ID; pinned policy fingerprint; `{outcome_policy_id,outcome_id,effect,completed_milestone_ids,not_required_milestones,companion_milestone_ids,source_evidence_id}`; all three arrays are present, including empty arrays; direct whole-case assertion uses `outcome_policy_id:"__direct__"`, `outcome_id:"assert_already_handled"`, and its explicit-confirmation source |
| `evidence.event_high_water.v1` / `EVENT_HIGH_WATER` | reserved; no Phase 2 production writer | future publisher record ID/version; `{eligible_event_id,event_name,canonical_key,after_source_version}`; any production attempt fails because the publisher registry is empty |
| `evidence.amendment_confirmation.v1` / `AMENDMENT_CONFIRMATION` | `taskPlan.js`; `producer.task_plan.v1`; always a newly constructed original-task-instance record | `R("acr1_",{original_task_instance_id,plan_change_revision,confirmation_ordinal})`; `V("acv1_",replacement_evidence_copy)`; `{plan_change_revision,replacement_task_document_id,replacement_task_instance_id,confirmation_outcome_policy_id,confirmation_outcome_id,source_kind,source_receipt_identity,replacement_evidence_copy:{task_instance_id,evidence_id,digest,observed_at}}`, where the nested tuple is copied by value after same-transaction verification and is not a dependency edge |
| `evidence.contradiction_evaluation.v1` / `CONTRADICTION_EVALUATION` | `taskDisposition.js`; `producer.task_disposition.v1` | exact `cr1_...` and `cv1_...` values from §2.2; exact `{reason_code,selector_ids,result,selection_digest}` |
| internal `evidence.rollup.v1` / `EVIDENCE_ROLLUP` | shared constructor; `producer.evidence_rollup.v1` | `R("er1_",{task_instance_id,digest})`; `V("erv1_",{count,first_observed_at,last_observed_at,digest})`; exact bounded `{count,first_observed_at,last_observed_at,digest}` accumulator defined below |

The exact producer IDs above are also the `producer` bytes in the evidence-ID preimage. `request_identity` is the operation ID for a command/H54 request and the submission token for workflow. `canonical_request` omits transport-only authentication context but includes every accepted request member. A `source_ref` or a named `*_evidence_id` fact that is an `ev1_` ID is a typed dependency edge only within the same task instance. The sole apparent-name exception is `AMENDMENT_CONFIRMATION.facts.replacement_evidence_copy.evidence_id`: that complete four-member tuple is bounded audit data copied by value, never a resolver input, root, or closure edge. WAITING_OUTCOME is the only typed source for a waiting-policy result; its POLICY_OUTCOME wrapper can complete/continue/renew, and a renewal WAIT_ENTRY references that wrapper, but neither WAITING_OUTCOME nor renewal can establish external submission.

For that exception, `replacement_evidence_copy` is frozen before any write as the exact `{task_instance_id,evidence_id,digest,observed_at}` tuple from the current replacement's retained `POLICY_OUTCOME` record. `digest` is the 64-character lowercase SHA-256 of `TaskCanonicalV1(complete replacement evidence record)`, including its own task-bound ID and stored Firestore `observed_at`; the copied `observed_at` is that same Timestamp. `confirmAmendment` reads the original task, replacement task, immutable confirmation snapshot, receipt/submission, and the replacement evidence record in one Firestore transaction; it requires the document/instance/policy/outcome/source/receipt tuple and recomputed digest to match before it creates the new original-instance evidence. No lookup ever resolves the copied evidence ID through the original's evidence array. A missing/reset/recreated/concurrently changed replacement aborts with `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED` and zero task, evidence, history, or operation mutation.

For `TASK_IDENTITY_PROJECTION`, the shared constructor first freezes this exact canonical `immutable_projection` map:

```text
{
  policy_version, policy_fingerprint,
  task_document_id, task_instance_id, task_generation_epoch,
  case_inputs:[{field_path,value}],
  case_key:{move_event_id?,subject_kind,subject_id,
            institution_or_account_id?,task_type},
  profile_resolver_id,
  profile_inputs:[{field_path,value_or_missing}],
  milestone_profile_id,
  required_milestone_ids,
  companion_milestone_ids,
  milestone_subject_keys:[{role:"REQUIRED"|"COMPANION",milestone_id,subject_key}]
}
```

`case_inputs` is in exact order `subject_id_field,institution_id_field?,move_event_id_field?`; each entry stores the policy path string and the immutable selected scalar value. `profile_inputs` is empty for `profile.single_case.v1`; otherwise it is the selected profile's `match_all` declaration order and stores `{field_path,value_or_missing}`, where `value_or_missing` is exact `{kind:"VALUE",value}` or `{kind:"MISSING"}` under §2.2—never raw Firestore null. Required then companion milestone arrays retain profile order. `milestone_subject_keys` has all required rows followed by all companion rows in those same orders, and each subject key is the already-derived `ck1_` or `sk1_` value. `case_key_digest = "ckd1_" + SHA-256(canonical(case_key))`; `subject_keys_digest = "skd1_" + SHA-256(canonical(milestone_subject_keys))`; and `source_version = V("iv1_",immutable_projection)`. The stored facts are exactly `{case_key_digest,milestone_profile_id,subject_keys_digest}`. `taskDisposition.js`, `taskPlan.js`, and `getWorkflowQualifying.js` must call this one exported constructor; none may reorder or rederive a member. Byte fixtures for owner/t1/t2/t3/a2 projections, optional workflow absence, each move alias, single/matched profiles, and cross-module equality freeze the complete canonical projection, both digests, source version, and final evidence ID.

Retention is computed over the complete prospective record set after adding the transaction's new rows. The rooted closure starts with `milestone_profile.resolved_from_evidence_ids`, every required/companion milestone evidence member, trigger, deadline, waiting-entry, waiting-display-fallback, contradiction, external-submission, wake, resolution, active-handoff, and evidence IDs in ordinary history rows that remain after the history-fit step below. `waiting_state.entry_evidence_id` or `waiting_fallback_state.fallback_evidence_id` roots its complete typed dependency chain. Let `R` be that stable-ID ordered rooted set and `E` every nonroot non-rollup record ordered by `observed_at` then ID. Structural validation/runtime first require that live roots plus the newest prospective history row's dependency closure fit the 128-record/131,072-byte caps. It then retains the newest prefix-ending sequence of at most 32 ordinary history rows whose combined rooted closure fits; every older ordinary row enters the bounded interaction-history rollup in the same transaction and ceases to root evidence only after all prospective checks succeed. Thus an otherwise valid task never bricks solely because 32 historical rows referenced too many old records.

After the history-fit step, if the exact prospective `evidence_records` set fits both caps, retain it. Otherwise select the shortest oldest prefix `K` of `E` that represents at least one record and whose replacement by one rollup makes both caps fit. Let `D` be the lowercase SHA-256 digests of the complete canonical selected logical records in order, excluding any prior rollup. With prior evidence rollup `P` optional, the replacement is exact: `count=(P?.count ?? 0)+K.length`; first time `P?.first_observed_at ?? K[0].observed_at`; last time `K.last.observed_at` (or P's last only when K is empty, which selection forbids); and `digest=SHA-256(canonical {domain:"evidence_rollup.v2",prior:P ? {count,first_observed_at,last_observed_at,digest} : null,appended_record_digests:D})`. The prior rollup is replaced, never flattened and never retained alongside the new one. Facts remain constant-size regardless of lifetime. A rollup is never a dependency edge/root. If `R` plus the newest history row cannot fit, reject that specific fresh mutation before writing; policy validation must reject any single legal effect whose maximum closure could cause that state. Cross-module fixtures cover exact caps, profile-proof retention, history shrinking below 32, thousands of successive mutations/rollups, prior accumulator chaining, rooted overflow, one oversized new row, WAIT renewal, H54 union, digest tampering, and evidence-root release only on commit.

Expectation template input is the transition's `reportedFacts` map after the general bound. Each template accepts only its one optional trimmed NFC label, 1–128 UTF-8 bytes when present, and renders exactly:

The rooted set `R` always includes every ID in `milestone_profile.resolved_from_evidence_ids` and every required/companion milestone evidence member; the earlier shorthand “milestone” includes those profile-proof roots. The identity-projection record therefore can never enter `E` while its profile pointer exists. Overflow fixtures explicitly place the profile proof among the oldest records and prove it remains retained through single and nested rollups.

| Template | Only optional fact | Exact expectation copy |
|---|---|---|
| `expectation.process_submission.v1` | `submission_label` | present: `Process {submission_label}`; absent: `Process the submission` |
| `expectation.deliver_artifact.v1` | `artifact_label` | present: `Deliver {artifact_label}`; absent: `Deliver the requested item` |
| `expectation.confirm_amendment.v1` | `amendment_label` | present: `Confirm {amendment_label}`; absent: `Confirm the amendment` |
| `expectation.issue_refund.v1` | `refund_label` | present: `Issue {refund_label}`; absent: `Issue the refund` |
| `expectation.respond_alternate_channel.v1` | `channel_label` | present: `Respond by {channel_label}`; absent: `Respond through another channel` |

Braces denote byte concatenation, not interpolation syntax retained in storage. Labels may not contain control characters. The rendered copy is capped at 256 UTF-8 bytes after concatenation. Institution display names are never taken from `reportedFacts`.

Every live WAIT mapper additionally produces the exact read-only `WaitingFactProjectionV1` map `{schema_version:1,office_display,owner,expectation,follow_up_trigger,nonresponse_effect,backup_action,resume_rule}`. Its six visible fact rows, in that fixed order, are: owner (`owner`), expected response (`expectation`), follow-up (`follow_up_trigger`), failure consequence (`nonresponse_effect`), backup (`backup_action`), and resume behavior (`resume_rule`). `office_display` and the visible `owner` are both exactly `waiting_state.display_name`; the machine contract owner remains `institution:{case institution/account id}` but is never rendered. `expectation` copies the current WAIT contract. `follow_up_trigger` is the lossless UI projection of the current confirmed date/event trigger. `nonresponse_effect` is exact fixed copy `After one unsuccessful follow-up, change channel.`. `backup_action` copies the unique alternate handoff's `expected_action`. `resume_rule` is exact fixed copy `This returns to your attention when the follow-up fires.`. The six facts are always visible above the two fixed exits and are recomputed from the same pinned state/policy on every snapshot. No task institution, case/provider identifier, label, support record, presentation context, caller string, or client default supplies a visible name; fixtures assert no value beginning `institution:` and no opaque case ID occurs anywhere in the projection, Chat draft, or waiting copy.

`It's stuck` constructs only the local exact `WaitingChatDraftV1` map `{schema_version:1,task_document_id,task_instance_id,office_display,task_title,expectation,follow_up_trigger}` from the same mapper projection, then opens `SupportChatView` with those fields prefilled. The task title is immutable trimmed NFC 1–256 bytes, the complete draft is at most 2,048 `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes, and a malformed/oversize source keeps the control passive. Opening/dismissing Chat creates no callable, support document, support status, reply-by promise, trigger change, or task mutation and leaves the owned WAIT disposition intact. The post-Chat coordinator may offer the hidden cycle-0 renewal selector; Chat itself never dispatches it. A task carrying `waiting_fallback_state` never constructs either WAIT projection. Tests assert all six rows and sources, exact two visible exits, named-office/task prefill, no opaque machine owner/ID in visible bytes, malformed bounds, zero support/server writes, WAIT preservation, and complete fallback exclusion.

## 3. Mutable state, evidence, and projections

### 3.1 Exact server-owned task state

Every fresh owner-created task carries immutable root `task_generation_epoch`; every policy-bearing owner task additionally has immutable `task_instance_id` and initial root `flowWriteRevision:0`, `notesWriteRevision:0`, and `quotesWriteRevision:0`. The fence covers every owner create because a policy-only fence would leave the production legacy population able to recreate deleted tasks after reset finalization. Existing stored policy-absent tasks are not rewritten and remain decode/callback compatible, but a newly generated policy-absent task gains only this ignored fence field. User root `taskGenerationEpoch` is a persistent safe nonnegative integer, absent-as-0 before the first reset. `resetAllTasks` increments and stores it atomically when it creates the deleting marker; finalize deletes only the marker and retains the epoch. Owner generation reads/captures the current epoch with its durable candidate set, creates a lowercase RFC 4122 task UUID once for each policy row, and sends the epoch on every create plus the UUID/revisions on policy creates; rules require candidate epoch equal the current root value. Thus every delayed pre-reset owner create is denied during reset and after finalize. Server-created policy tasks use the exact H54/spawn derivations in §§4.4/4.6 and current root epoch. Rules deny generation change/removal, require all five policy identity/revision fields, and permit revision changes only through §4.2/§5.5's exact +1 actions. Initial policy create forbids `stage`, all five live attempt-tail members, and both last-cleared members. Reset/regeneration necessarily changes epoch and, for policy tasks, the instance/epoch pair. The instance ID is part of every fresh policy command/producer expectation, session, operation receipt, wake/intent/route identity, flow proof, and local durable request namespace. Matching document ID, epoch, revision, and policy fingerprint without matching instance is stale. An op-first policy replay for an old instance may return its old receipt, but the client must not apply that receipt to a live different instance; §4.7 legacy replays instead revalidate live generation and string-or-null instance before returning any stored result.

The all-owner fence explicitly includes synthetic packing rows `PACKING_SESSION_*`, `PACKING_READINESS_GATE`, and `PACKING_SUPPLIES_KIT`. `TaskActionService` captures root and every existing target expectation, builds the exact §4.7 `persistLegacyPackingPlan` request before awaiting, rejects more than 128 sessions, and puts the same captured `task_generation_epoch` on every synthetic task/plan set. The server transaction rereads and compares all expectations; an existing pre-v5 synthetic task may receive the field exactly once only when policy/contract/audit are absent, `generatedBy == "packingPlan"`, and immutable synthetic ID/kind match. Thereafter direct change/removal is denied. Any present policy/state/contract aborts the whole persistence before writes, as §4.5 requires. Each complete prospective synthetic task map is capped at 32,768 canonical bytes; the packing-plan document is capped at 262,144; and canonical `{packing_plan,task_sets_in_path_order,delete_paths_in_path_order}` is capped at 4,194,304 bytes. The exact §4.7 equation caps data writes at 259; the same transaction also creates one compact operation record, so its total maximum is 260 writes. Bounds are measured before dispatch and again on the server before writes; overflow returns deterministic validation failure and writes/deletes nothing. The four-MiB semantic cap is independent of §4.6's exact Firestore document/index charge; §7.4 exempts the schema-large exact-ID packing plan/readiness maps, and the same independent oracle proves the maximum batch within the 8 MiB transaction budget.

The emulator suite constructs 128 session maps plus readiness/supplies, a plan, and the maximum compatible stale-delete set whose aggregate canonical preimage is exactly 4,194,304 bytes and includes at least one exact 32,768-byte task and the exact 262,144-byte plan; it must succeed atomically under the 500-write and service request limits. It also tests each equal/one-byte-over component/aggregate/write cap, reset racing after capture, old-client omitted epoch, one-time legacy upgrade, delayed pre-reset recreation, and every target expectation mismatch. Direct owner task/plan/readiness writes are denied as §4.7 states. The server transaction remains all-or-nothing with no partial chunk or visible preparing state. If the exact within-cap emulator worst case still exceeds a service limit, the adopted all-owner atomic packing design is contradicted and execution stops rather than weakening the fence or splitting the atomic relation.

`TaskActionService.clearPackingPlan(userId:)` closes the inverse ABA without changing its protected `InventorySessionManager` caller and without attempting a service-infeasible 130-document transaction. It sends the exact §4.2 `clearPackingPlan` action to `taskDisposition.js`; the user root carries the exact camelCase `packingClear` marker in §3.3. `scannedCount/deletedTaskCount` are `0...130`; `deletedAuxiliaryCount <= auxiliaryIndex <= 2`; `scanAfterPath` is present only while scanning after a result; `auxiliaryIndex` is `0` during scanning/task deletion, `1` after the plan step, and `2` after the readiness step. Begin/resume reads root plus retained operation, requires reset inactive and marker generation equal root generation, creates `scanning` for a fresh validated operation ID, or adopts the existing same-generation operation only when every shared projected field matches. Each invocation advances at most one transactionally compared transition; no lease member exists. While either active state exists, rules and every server producer deny packing task, `packingPlan/current`, and `readiness/current` create/update/delete except the exact server transitions below; `persist` also checks absence of the marker before building a batch.

`scanning` uses a server query `generatedBy == "packingPlan"`, ordered by document ID, `startAfter(scanAfterPath)` when present, and literal `limit(1)`, and never deletes. Each result is transactionally reread with root+marker+operation and must be policy/state/contract absent, have an exact closed packing ID/kind tuple, and have `task_generation_epoch` either absent for a pre-v5 legacy row or equal to the marker generation; success advances cursor and count exactly once. The scan also validates `packingPlan/current` and `readiness/current` in separate root+one-document invocations before it can enter `deleting`. A 131st row produces `ROW_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`; wrong-generation/identity or policy/state/contract collision produces `ROW_INVALID`; malformed auxiliary data produces `AUXILIARY_INVALID`. Each atomically removes only a still-scanning marker, advances the operation to `failed_validation`, retains the exact error receipt, and returns that deterministic no-delete result. The empty task scan after both auxiliary validations changes marker and operation state to `deleting`, clears the cursor, and retains counts.

`deleting` repeatedly queries the first remaining matching task with the same `limit(1)`. One invocation's transaction rereads root, marker, operation, and that one document; it requires the same generation/tuple and deletes only that task while incrementing `deletedTaskCount`. A missing raced result is a zero-count transition. When the task query is empty, one root+plan invocation deletes `packingPlan/current` when present, increments `deletedAuxiliaryCount` only on an actual delete, and advances `auxiliaryIndex` 0→1; a separate root+readiness invocation applies the same rule and advances 1→2.
