A final root-only invocation rechecks empty task/plan/readiness queries under the marker, requires `deletedTaskCount == scannedCount`, writes the operation's `finalized` response, then removes only `packingClear`. No destructive transaction reads more than root plus one potentially maximum-size document; the delete proof enumerates that document's actual removable index entries under §7.4 rather than assuming a constant. Physical deletes are resumable rather than globally atomic; the durable marker is the logical exclusion fence and the retained terminal operation is replay/adoption authority after marker deletion. A crash restarts scanning from its cursor or resumes deleting/auxiliary index. `resetAllTasks` may remove an active marker only while atomically rotating generation and marks its operation with `PACKING_CLEAR_FENCE_INVALID/FENCE_INVALID`; an old clear can never delete a regenerated same-path row. A process-lost client adopts the active server operation and loops one transition per request until its exact terminal receipt.

Rules use the already-read literal user root, so a synthetic write still has one distinct rules read. Mandatory fixtures use a service-maximum legacy row, 130 success, 131/invalid fail before deletion, crashes and response loss in both phases, same-document mutation, a missing row, marker-blocked persist/direct writes, reset at every boundary, same-ID post-reset regeneration, malformed/wrong-generation markers, absent/current/wrong row epochs, and plan/readiness finalization races. Each delete transaction has the same one-document service-size proof as §4.4 reset.

Both TaskGenerationService loops use one `CatalogOwnerTaskProjection`; no loop hand-builds a variant. Its complete allowed fresh-create key set is:

```text
{
  id, taskId, title, desc, category, actionCategory, actionType, taskType,
  urgencyPercentage, estHours, tips, whyNeeded, conditions,
  dueDate, status:"Upcoming", userId, createdAt, selfServiceOnly,
  workflowId?, surfaceAfterDaysPastMove?, tier?,
  nudgePrompt?, nudgeSpawnsId?, flowRows?,
  task_generation_epoch,
  taskInteraction?, task_instance_id?, flowWriteRevision?,
  notesWriteRevision?, quotesWriteRevision?, moveEventId?
}
```

The document path ID, stored `id`, stored `taskId`, and catalog document/`taskId` are identical. `documentId` is path-derived and forbidden as a stored key. Exact catalog transforms are: `title ?? ""`, `desc ?? ""`, `category ?? "custom"`, `actionCategory ?? ""`, `actionType ?? "off-app"`, string `taskType ?? "provide_info"`, finite `urgencyPercentage ?? 50`, `estHours ?? 0`, `tips ?? ""`, `whyNeeded ?? ""`, `conditions ?? {}`, and Boolean `selfServiceOnly ?? false`. `workflowId`, safe-integer `surfaceAfterDaysPastMove`, and string `tier` are present iff the catalog member is present and equal it. `nudgePrompt` and `nudgeSpawnsId` are present iff catalog `nudge.cardPrompt`/`nudge.spawnsId` is present and equal that nested value. Catalog strings are NFC; title is 1–1,024 bytes, desc/tips/whyNeeded 0–8,192, category/actionCategory/actionType/taskType/workflow/tier/nudge IDs 0–256 with any semantically required ID nonempty, and nudge prompt 1–1,024. Urgency is an integer 0–100; estHours is finite 0–1,000; conditions is a JSON-compatible map of at most 128 keys/depth 4/32,768 canonical bytes. Any wrong type/bound invalidates a policy-bearing catalog row before projection.

Runtime-owned fields are `userId == auth.uid`, current root generation, Timestamp `dueDate`, and Timestamp `createdAt` no more than five minutes before rules `request.time`. Due date is produced by the existing move/nudge pacing algorithm, type-checked at create, and immutable on a policy task; rules intentionally do not recompute it because identity move date and a nudge target would add unauthorized reads. It is estimate/display pacing, never deadline authority. A policy-present owner row additionally requires owner eligibility (`spawnedOnly != true`, no `rowGeneration`), exact catalog `taskInteraction`, a lowercase UUID-v4 `task_instance_id`, literal `flowWriteRevision:0`, literal `notesWriteRevision:0`, literal `quotesWriteRevision:0`, and root `moveEventId == activeMoveEventId` iff selected; it forbids `flowRows` and every other case/provenance/move alias. A policy-absent row omits policy instance/revision/move fields but still carries generation. Only a policy-absent `rowGeneration` row may add its existing assessment-derived bounded `flowRows`; rules retain that legacy shape without treating it as authority.

Owner projection never copies `researchScope`, `researchPrefs`, `estPeezy`, `dateRule`, `onCompleteSpawns`, `notesEnabled`, `quoteTracker`, `content`, `spawnedOnly`, `rowGeneration`, or catalog `createdAt`, and forbids caller `subject`, `institutionId`, `institution`, `canonicalKeyVersion`, `supersedes`, `supersededBy`, `spawnedFrom`, state/contract/H54/handoff/wake/resolution/history, flow tail/fences, and every unlisted key. Raw `taskType`, optional `workflowId`, `category`, and `actionCategory` are retained in the strict immutable mapper projection because policy profile/workflow/contradiction selection consumes them; presentation defaults never confer authority. The seeder additionally requires every producer binding's workflow ID equal the row's projected workflow ID. JS/Swift projection parity and rules tests mutate/delete/type-change each field and optional-presence branch one at a time, cover ordinary/workflow/post-move/nudge/policy rows, both loops, stable candidate UUID/timestamps across retry, and prove policy-absent rowGeneration create/current epoch plus delayed post-reset denial.

`taskInteractionState` accepts only these keys:

```text
{
  policy_version, policy_fingerprint,
  interaction_epoch, interaction_revision,
  case_key: { move_event_id?, subject_kind, subject_id,
              institution_or_account_id?, task_type },
  milestone_profile: { id, resolver_id, resolved_from_evidence_ids },
  required_milestones: [{ id, subject_key,
                          state: OPEN|COMPLETED|NOT_REQUIRED,
                          evidence_id?, not_required_reason?, not_required_source? }],
  companion_milestones: [{ id, subject_key,
                           state: OPEN|COMPLETED, evidence_id? }],
  contradiction?: { state: none, evidence_ids },
  deadline_evidence?: { evidence_id, deadline_at, protected_outcome,
                        label, observed_at },
  amendment_anchor?: { schema_version:1, kind:MOVE_DATE|ADDRESS,
                       batch_id, source_ref, source_version, value_digest,
                       label:"Update your active dates" },
  external_submission?: { established, evidence_id? },
  waiting_state?: { transition_code, outcome_policy_id, channel_kind,
                    display_name, display_name_source,
                    same_channel_cycle_count:0|1, entered_at,
                    entry_evidence_id, second_cycle_transition_code,
                    second_cycle_action_id, nudge_template_id },
  waiting_fallback_state?: { transition_code, outcome_policy_id,
                             fallback_evidence_id },
  pending_handoff?: { action_id, source_kind:HANDOFF_FAILURE|WAITING_CHANNEL,
                      source_session_id?, source_transition_code?,
                      source_entry_evidence_id?, source_same_channel_cycle_count?,
                      target_transition_code?, nudge_template_id?,
                      nudge_rendered_sha256?, selection_operation_id, selected_at },
  evidence_records: [{ id, kind, interaction_epoch, producer, source_ref, source_version,
                       observed_at, facts }]
}
```

`policy_version` and fingerprint equal the pinned policy. Epoch/revision are safe nonnegative integers; absence before the first policy command means `0/0`. Every successful semantic task mutation increments revision. Fresh dispositions, begin/cancel/outcome resolution, H54 transitions, and policy migrations increment epoch; acknowledgement, return, scheduler wake, and intent claim do not.

The first successful policy mutation initializes state in its transaction before applying the command. It resolves the exact case key from immutable task fields, creates one `evidence.task_identity_projection.v1` digest, selects exactly one profile, and stores that evidence ID as the profile's one-element `resolved_from_evidence_ids`. `move_event_id` is present exactly when `case_key_policy.move_event_id_field` is present; the one immutable task path selected by §2.2 must equal persistent user-root `activeMoveEventId`, and omission/wrong type/mismatch or a surplus move-event alias rejects initialization. The root field's exact domain is a lowercase RFC 4122 UUID or `^me1_[0-9a-f]{40}$`. Before the first owner-generation candidate snapshot, `TaskGenerationService` may establish an absent field once only as a lowercase RFC 4122 UUID in its own transaction, requiring authenticated ownership, absent field, absent/inactive reset, and unchanged `taskGenerationEpoch`; it then rereads the root. The field is immutable to every client after creation. `resetAllTasks` is the sole `me1_` writer and rotates it atomically with the generation-epoch increment to `"me1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,new_task_generation_epoch,reset_operation_id}))`; finalize retains both. A move-date/address edit within the same move does not rotate it. Every producer accepts/copies either valid union member and applies the exact §2.2 matrix; catalog data never contains a per-user move ID. No reducer derives a move event from a date/address or caller label. Required and companion milestones are exactly the selected profile's two ID arrays in declared order, all OPEN with no evidence/reason/source members. The case subject key is `sk1_` plus first40 SHA-256 over canonical `{subject_kind,subject_id}`; a case-scoped milestone uses `ck1_` plus first40 over the complete canonical case key, and a subject-scoped milestone uses that subject key. No client supplies a subject key. OPEN forbids evidence/reason/source; COMPLETED requires only `evidence_id`; NOT_REQUIRED requires all of `evidence_id`, `not_required_reason`, and `not_required_source`. A failed command commits no partial initialization. A later source change does not silently switch profiles; policy migration or H54 must name old/new fingerprints. Fixtures prove both root ID variants, same-move multi-task equality, two otherwise-identical subjects in different moves do not collide, two users remain isolated, every producer/path combination, forged catalog/task values, delayed old generation after rotation, and reset/regeneration retaining only the new instance's case projection.

On a live state, absent `external_submission` means false. A present map is exactly `{established:true,evidence_id}` and its evidence ID resolves to the immutable `POLICY_OUTCOME` wrapper whose explicit establishment effect is backed by a policy-bound handoff outcome or durable vendor workflow submission. `{established:false,...}` is never stored. The fact is monotonic through every nonterminal builder, scheduler wake, and H54 transition. Upcoming omits the contract projection but retains the state map. A terminal transition removes the live map only while atomically writing the exact archive below and retaining the complete typed evidence dependency chain.

`waiting_state` is required exactly while the task is `matching_in_progress + WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL` and forbidden in every other live projection. It is exact `{transition_code,outcome_policy_id,channel_kind,display_name,display_name_source,entry_evidence_id,same_channel_cycle_count,entered_at,second_cycle_transition_code,second_cycle_action_id,nudge_template_id}`. Transition/outcome/channel come from the pinned transition; display name/source are the exact trusted entry-time values above; `entry_evidence_id` resolves to the current byte-matching WAIT_ENTRY record; and `entered_at` is the concrete server-authored write time of that current entry. The last three values are server-derived from the transition's validated `second_cycle_channel`, its one compatible handoff, and nudge registry; callers never send them. A new entry starts cycle count `0`. The policy's first same-channel stuck outcome increments it to `1`, creates a new WAITING_OUTCOME→WAIT_ENTRY dependency chain, copies display name/source byte-for-byte without rereading the directory, and replaces `entered_at` with that renewal transaction's server time. A further same-channel outcome cannot increment and returns the exact different-channel resolver descriptor with zero mutation. Exact replay and scheduler wake preserve every map byte. Directory changes never rename a live wait. Leaving WAIT archives the tuple in history and removes it from live state.

`waiting_fallback_state` is required exactly with `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED` when trusted-name failure selected the §2.2 fallback and is forbidden everywhere else. It is exact `{transition_code,outcome_policy_id,fallback_evidence_id}`; the evidence resolves to the current `WAIT_DISPLAY_FALLBACK`, transitively roots the same confirmed-trigger evidence used by the contract, and proves whether entry was DIRECT or OUTCOME without persisting any untrusted name or opaque case ID. Its contract is validated against that transition's trigger policy rather than the ordinary SELF_HANDLE branch. A scheduler wake preserves the marker while replacing the fired trigger with ATTENTION_NOW; the next fresh disposition, terminalization, or H54 lifecycle transition archives the evidence/history and clears the live marker. Exact replay returns the same branch.

`pending_handoff` is written only by a committed TRY_ALTERNATE or TRY_CHANNEL selection and does not itself open anything or consume a trigger. Its required common map is exactly `{action_id,source_kind,selection_operation_id,selected_at}`, where `action_id` is the selected target handoff, `selection_operation_id` is its receipt-bound operation, and `selected_at` is that transaction's concrete server-authored write time. For HANDOFF_FAILURE the only additional member is `source_session_id`: the choice transaction requires the matching `returned` source session and changes it to `resolved` while retaining lifecycle/evidence bytes; every transition/entry/cycle/nudge member is forbidden. Thus the persisted post-selection invariant is one matching resolved source, never a still-returned session. WAITING_CHANNEL is available only from the second-cycle resolver at live count `1`; its additional exact members are `{source_transition_code,source_entry_evidence_id,source_same_channel_cycle_count:1,target_transition_code,nudge_template_id,nudge_rendered_sha256}` and `source_session_id` is forbidden. `action_id` and all target/template members must equal `waiting_state` and the pinned registry; `nudge_rendered_sha256` hashes the exact rendered nudge copy. The action kind differs from the source transition channel. The next `beginHandoff` must name that `action_id`, use a presentation snapshot whose script equals the rendered nudge, and supply the one newly confirmed fallback trigger valid for the target transition. HANDOFF_FAILURE begin rereads the resolved source session, its return/outcome evidence, and selection receipt; WAITING_CHANNEL begin rereads the retained transition/entry/count/target/template tuple and receipt. It appends the old tuple/evidence to interaction history, clears the pending map, and creates the new session atomically. Any generic `beginHandoff` directly from W, stale/cycle-0 pending selection, returned/cancelled/missing HANDOFF_FAILURE source, same-kind action, changed evidence/template/rendering, or mismatched receipt rejects with zero mutation. Any other fresh disposition clears the pending map and archives the resolved source when present. OPEN_CHAT writes no pending map. Selection replay preserves `action_id` and `selected_at` byte-for-byte; crash/relaunch maps the resolved-source invariant and renders the selected CTA without repeating the choice.

Absent `contradiction` means unevaluated/unknown, never `none`. A `markNotApplicable` evaluation of `present` commits only its replayable resolver operation record, including the evaluator digest, and performs zero task mutation. `unknown` commits neither task nor success receipt and returns `failed-precondition`. Only `none` may be written into the terminal task state/resolution as part of the successful NOT_APPLICABLE transaction. A later source change cannot turn a prior resolver receipt into authority. Reducer receipts use evaluator result `present`, and absence represents unknown, so no evidence meaning is lost. Mapper fixtures accept stored `none`, reject stored `present/unknown` as malformed-present dynamic state, and cover the receipt/absence branches.

`amendment_anchor` is absent unless the active external H54 entry declares `MOVE_DATE` or `ADDRESS`. `taskPlan.js` derives it in the supersede transaction from only `users/{uid}/identity/identity`. MOVE_DATE requires Firestore Timestamp `moveDate` and hashes canonical `{$timestamp:...}`. ADDRESS requires exact maps `currentAddress` and `newAddress`, each containing trimmed NFC `street,city,state,zip,raw` strings and optional trimmed `unit`, with each string at most 4,096 UTF-8 bytes and the combined canonical pair at most 32,768 bytes; it hashes the pair but stores no address bytes. `source_ref = R("aar1_",{document_path:"users/{uid}/identity/identity",field_paths})`, `source_version = V("aav1_",{snapshot_update_time})`, `value_digest = SHA-256(canonical selected value)`, and `batch_id = "ab1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,kind,source_version,value_digest}))`. Missing/wrong/malformed source fails external supersede before mutation. The exact anchor is copied to original and replacement state and retained through PA/PC/CF/history; an entry declaring NONE forbids it. Home/Tasks group live anchors with identical `batch_id` into one “Update your active dates” card in first-task order while each line renders and routes its own task owner, action, handoff session, trigger, and return question. A line never writes through the group. Tests cover same-source batching, a source update splitting batches, MOVE_DATE versus ADDRESS, NONE, malformed identity, cross-account digest isolation, line-specific routes, and H54 snapshot/undo retention.

Evidence IDs are server-generated as `ev1_` plus 40 lowercase SHA-256 hex characters over canonical `{uid,task_document_id,task_instance_id,interaction_epoch,producer,source_ref,source_version,facts}`. Every stored record belongs to that one task instance, and every typed evidence resolver rejects an edge whose record is absent from or names a different instance than the owning task's `evidence_records`. The record's stored `interaction_epoch` must equal that preimage member; the constructor receives the reducer's prospective post-mutation epoch, or the unchanged current epoch for a revision-only/zero-task-mutation operation, and never infers it from an already-written task. `facts` is the rule-specific exact map in §2.3, capped at 16,384 canonical bytes, and contains no raw answer, credential, account, protected identity, authorization value, phone, URL, or free-form script. Retain every record in the same-instance transitive typed dependency closure described in §2.3; the Amendment-confirmation copied tuple is nonresolving data under that section. `evidence_records` is capped at 128 records and 131,072 canonical bytes total. Before writing, apply §2.3's exact prospective-set algorithm; a dependency-rooted record is never rolled. If the resulting set cannot fit both caps, reject the fresh mutation atomically with `failed-precondition/EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN`; exact replay remains available. `taskInteractionState` including evidence is capped at 196,608 canonical bytes, `interactionHistory` at 32 ordinary rows plus one rollup and 65,536 canonical bytes, and the complete canonical task document after every proposed write at 655,360 bytes. The reducer measures the exact prospective canonical maps before any transaction write and rejects rather than relying on Firestore's 1 MiB failure. These component caps reserve 393,216 bytes inside the per-task service limit for Firestore encoding/noncanonical bookkeeping; the independent transaction/index charge still uses §4.6's `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1`.

`external_submission_archive` is exact `{established:true,evidence_id}` and the evidence ID must remain present in `evidence_records`; it is forbidden when no fact existed. Terminal Completed/Not Applicable writers and H54 confirmation write it into `resolution`, and the same operation's history record copies it. H54 undo/reopen restores the snapshot's semantic case/profile/milestone/contract/handoff/resolution fields, but `evidence_records` is a stable-ID ordered union of the snapshot rows followed by every post-snapshot immutable row not already present, and post-snapshot `interactionHistory`/`planChangeHistory` remains. The full transitive retention/rollup algorithm then runs; audit-only post-snapshot evidence never re-closes a restored milestone. This mandated evidence/history preservation is part of “exact” restoration and prevents a retained history/cycle edge from dangling. Mapper/recap code resolves only retained evidence IDs and never infers authority from a Boolean.

`interactionHistory` is a separate server-only array of exact ordinary records `{operation_id,task_instance_id,interaction_epoch,revision_before,revision_after,command,redacted_effect,evidence_ids,external_submission_archive?,occurred_at}`. User/H54 commands use their operation ID, workflow effects use the submission token, and a firing scheduler mutation uses the deterministic wake ID. A later urgency escalation uses distinct `operation_id = "ue1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,task_instance_id,wake_id,threshold_id,deadline_evidence_id,threshold_at}))`. Stored array order is commit/append order and is authoritative; operation ID plus task instance deduplicates only exact replay. Retain the newest 32 ordinary rows plus at most one oldest-position rollup `{schema_version:1,command:"ROLLUP",count,first_at,last_at,digest}`. On a prospective append or H54 stable union, remove exactly the oldest `ordinary_count - 32` ordinary rows. Compute each removed row digest as lowercase SHA-256 of its complete canonical ordinary row. The new rollup is exact: `count = prior.count + removed.length` (prior absent means zero), `first_at = prior.first_at ?? removed[0].occurred_at`, `last_at = removed.last.occurred_at`, and `digest = SHA-256(canonical {domain:"interaction_history.v1",prior_rollup: prior ? {count,first_at,last_at,digest} : null,appended_row_digests})`. A prior rollup is replaced, never nested as an array row. Removed rows cease to root their evidence only after the prospective state, evidence retention, and size checks all succeed atomically. It supports recap and archives prior waiting cycles; live stuck-channel authority is the exact `waiting_state`, never a recount over rolled-up history. It is not rollback authority. Fixtures cover 32→33, multiple rows appended in one transaction, repeated rollup, exact replay, equal timestamps, wake→escalate, repeated/equal-time escalation scans, H54 stable union, digest tampering, and evidence-root release only on commit.

`redacted_effect` is exact `{from_status,to_status,effect_code,closed_milestone_ids,transition_code?,channel_kind?,resolution_code?}`. Status values are the task statuses in §3.2. Effect code is the command action, one of the five policy outcome effects, literal `WAIT_DISPLAY_UNAVAILABLE_SELF_FOLLOW_UP`, `SCHEDULER_WAKE`, literal `URGENCY_ESCALATION`, or an H54 lifecycle action. The display-fallback effect requires its transition code, forbids channel/resolution, and stores no name or identifier. `URGENCY_ESCALATION` requires `from_status == to_status`, an empty milestone array, and forbids transition/channel/resolution; it records only the normal→urgent wake-evidence change. Milestone IDs are unique policy IDs; transition/channel appear exactly for actual WAIT entry/exit; resolution code appears exactly for resolver/terminal choices. No copy, reported fact, URL, phone, answer, trigger payload, or identity value is stored there.

In the interaction-history paragraph, “newest 32” is an upper bound, not a minimum: §2.3's history-fit step may roll additional oldest ordinary rows until their evidence-root closure fits, but always retains the newly appended row. The interaction rollup `count/first_at/last_at/digest` absorbs every additionally removed row in order using the same accumulator formula. A legal effect whose new row plus live nonhistory roots cannot fit is rejected structurally/runtime before mutation.

### 3.2 Contract/status projection

| Task `status` | `dispositionContract` | Exact coherence |
|---|---|---|
| `Upcoming` | no disposition/terminal/owner/action/trigger/resume/external projection | state evidence may remain; wake reactivate uses this pair |
| `InProgress` | `disposition: USER_ACTION_TRACKED` | owner, concrete `next_action`, structural trigger, resume destination, visible copy required; `waiting_fallback_state` is present exactly for the trusted-display fallback and otherwise forbidden |
| `matching_in_progress` | `disposition: WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL` | policy-derived owner/expectation, structural trigger, resume destination, visible copy required |
| `Snoozed` | `disposition: DEFERRED` | authenticated owner, return action, fresh trigger, row destination, visible copy required |
| `Completed` | `disposition: COMPLETED` | no owner/action/trigger/resume/live handoff/wake/intent |
| `Dismissed` | `NOT_APPLICABLE + terminal_kind:not_applicable`, internal graph `RETIRED`, or external graph `SUPERSEDED` | no nonterminal fields; SUPERSEDED requires `superseded_by` equal to the replacing task-instance ID |

For a valid policy, the complete contract is one of these exact maps; every string is trimmed NFC, unknown/null optional fields reject, and `external_submission` appears only as literal true when the live state archive is established:

```text
USER_ACTION = {schema_version:1,disposition:"USER_ACTION_TRACKED",
  owner:"user:{uid}",next_action,next_trigger,resume_destination:"row",
  visible_status_copy:"You’re handling this"|"Ready for your attention",
  external_submission?:true}
WAITING = {schema_version:1,disposition:"WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL",
  owner:"institution:{case institution/account id}",next_action:expectation_copy,
  next_trigger,resume_destination:"outcome",
  visible_status_copy:waiting_copy|"Check for an update",
  external_submission?:true}
DEFERRED = {schema_version:1,disposition:"DEFERRED",
  owner:"user:{uid}",next_action:"Return to this task",next_trigger,
  resume_destination:"row",visible_status_copy:"Do this later",
  external_submission?:true}
UPCOMING = {schema_version:1,visible_status_copy:"Ready to continue"}
COMPLETED = {schema_version:1,disposition:"COMPLETED",
  visible_status_copy:"Completed"}
NOT_APPLICABLE = {schema_version:1,disposition:"NOT_APPLICABLE",
  terminal_kind:"not_applicable",visible_status_copy:"Not applicable"}
RETIRED = {schema_version:1,terminal_kind:"retired",
  visible_status_copy:"Plan updated"}
SUPERSEDED = {schema_version:1,terminal_kind:"superseded",superseded_by,
  visible_status_copy:"Replaced by an updated task"}
```

`SUPERSEDED` is written only to the original whose external amendment was confirmed, with `superseded_by == replacement.task_instance_id`. `RETIRED` is exclusively an internal graph-retirement projection: the original retired by internal supersede, or the untouched proposed replacement internally retired when initial PA is reopened. The latter's retained replacement-link word `retired` is audit state consistent with that internal retirement; neither internal case may carry `superseded_by`. A document ID, plan revision, label, caller value, or stale instance in `SUPERSEDED.superseded_by` is malformed.

`next_action` is 1–500 UTF-8 bytes and for a handoff is the policy's visible expected action. `expectation_copy` is one §2.3 template result. `waiting_copy` is exactly `Waiting on {waiting_state.display_name}` and requires the concatenated result to fit 1,024 UTF-8 bytes; missing/oversize trusted authority takes the exact USER_ACTION fallback rather than emitting a generic or opaque institution label. Owner IDs are 1–512 UTF-8 bytes. Visible copy is never label-derived beyond these exact rules. Scheduler wake changes only the copy and trigger members needed by its target map. Completed, Dismissed, and Upcoming omit external projection. `pending + SUPPORT_ACTIVE` remains legacy-decodable but no Phase 2 writer creates it.

The sole uninitialized valid-policy projection is `status:"Upcoming"` with absent `taskInteractionState` and absent `dispositionContract`, immediately after a valid create and before its first policy mutation. The mapper exposes that combination as contracted Upcoming with no actions beyond policy capabilities; any other absent-state/contract status is malformed. The exact UPCOMING contract map is server-written only on wake/reactivation or another reducer that already initializes state. The only Phase 2 `resume_destination` wire values are the strings `row` and `outcome`; a slash, flow step, free-form route, or any other string is malformed. The server writes `row` for DEFERRED, SELF_HANDLE, handoff fallback, CONTINUE_USER_ACTION, and alternate-channel USER_ACTION; it writes `outcome` for actual WAITING. `activeHandoff.resume_destination` is `outcome`. A wake stores the route selected by §5.2 rather than copying an arbitrary legacy string. H54 replacement descriptors are accepted only with `amendmentAction.resumeDestination == "row"` and `verification.resumeDestination == "outcome"`; the latter is a closed semantic assertion that the verification action intends the bound WAIT transition, not authority for the stored route. The reducer stores `outcome` only when trusted display resolves and stores `row` for the exact waiting fallback. Policy-absent legacy builders retain their old strings, but no valid-policy mapper or writer accepts them.

For a valid policy-bearing task, `dispositionContract.next_trigger` is one and only one of these exact Firestore tagged maps; unknown keys and null optionals are forbidden:

```text
date = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"date", trigger_policy_id,
  source:"PROMISED_DATE"|"ACCEPTANCE_DATE"|"SAFE_THRESHOLD"|
         "USER_PICKED"|"LABELED_ESTIMATE",
  at:Timestamp, evidence_id, fired:false
}
event = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"event", trigger_policy_id, eligible_event_id,
  event_name, canonical_key, after_source_version, evidence_id, fired:false
}
attention = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"ATTENTION_NOW", wake_id
}
```

The active date/event form always has literal `fired:false`; only `wakeEvidence.cause.original_trigger` in the TRIGGER cause stores the otherwise byte-equivalent fired copy with `fired:true`. `at` is a Firestore Timestamp; Swift maps it to Date and canonical JSON renders it as a §2.1 timestamp value. The trigger policy ID resolves in the pinned policy and the source must be declared by it. LABELED_ESTIMATE requires the pinned move derivation; SAFE_THRESHOLD requires the exact safe member/current deadline evidence; USER_PICKED requires its exact safe threshold ID and `at <= recomputedSafeAt`. PROMISED_DATE and ACCEPTANCE_DATE require a matching operation-bound user-report record. Every date must be strictly future on a fresh write.

`evidence_id` resolves to an inline `evidence.confirmed_trigger.v1` record created in the same transaction with the exact §2.3 encoding. Its source is the promised/acceptance report, immutable pacing anchor evidence, deadline-authority evidence, or event high-water record used by the resolution. For event triggers, the eligible-event entry must resolve through the publisher registry; the server copies its event name/key and current high-water safe integer, using `-1` only when no high-water record yet exists. `attention` is never accepted from a command: the scheduler may write it only while a matching same-task `wakeEvidence.wake_id` exists and that evidence contains the fired date/event copy. No active date/event payload, legacy `payload`, caller authority field, or fired trigger is retained beside ATTENTION_NOW.

`PeezyCardFirestoreMapper` maps policy presence as `absentLegacy`, `valid(TaskInteraction)`, or `malformedPresent`; it separately maps the dynamic values above. Malformed dynamic state on a valid policy is passive. The client never repairs, synthesizes, or defaults a positive evidence fact.

### 3.3 Handoff, wake, operation, intent, and local records

`activeHandoff` is exact:

```text
{ schema_version:1, task_instance_id, session_id, interaction_epoch,
  installation_id, auth_epoch_uuid,
  state: prepared|open_requested|opened|returned|resolved|cancelled,
  action_id, handoff_kind, expected_action, outcome_policy_id,
  started_at, state_updated_at, resume_destination,
  prior_dynamic_snapshot_id, flow_attempt_proof?,
  adapter_operation_id, opened_evidence_id?, return_evidence_id?,
  restoration?: {kind:"H54_UNDO",operation_id},
  presentation_snapshot: {
    action_label, origin:DIRECTORY_ROW|RESOLVER_CALLABLE|GENERIC_GUIDANCE|
                         CATALOG_TASK_DIRECTIONS|CATALOG_TASK_DEEP_LINK|
                         USER_INVENTORY_SHARE|USER_ENTERED_MAIL,
    provider_display_name?, phone?, url?, mail_to?, mail_recipient_source?,
    mail_subject?, script?,
    share_derivation?:{kind:"MOVERS_INVENTORY_SHARE_V1",payload_sha256},
    source_refs?, display_deadline?:{at,label}
  }
}
```

The transaction snapshot, not caller-declared UI origin, determines `flow_attempt_proof`: a live complete flow tail requires and validates it; no live tail forbids it; a partial/malformed tail rejects begin. `adapter_operation_id` is supplied in `beginHandoff`, stored before any opener can run, and immutable through the session. `restoration` is present only on the fresh `prepared` session created by H54 undo; every ordinary session forbids it. `prepared/open_requested` forbid only the two lifecycle evidence IDs. A successful acknowledgement requires evidence with that stored adapter operation ID and writes opened evidence; `opened` requires it and forbids return evidence. `returned` requires both evidence IDs. Resolved/cancelled history retains whichever lifecycle fields had already been established. Presentation values preserve their origin label byte-for-byte across relaunch but are never server authority. Phone, URL, and mail recipient are mutually exclusive. MAIL requires origin `USER_ENTERED_MAIL`, `mail_to`, and literal `mail_recipient_source:"USER_ENTERED"`; it permits subject/body script and forbids phone/URL/share derivation. Every non-MAIL adapter forbids `mail_recipient_source`. SHARE requires only the exact share derivation and forbids phone/URL/mail fields/script. Secrets/raw identity/inventory payload are forbidden. Provider call/portal/citation uses `ProviderResolution`; provider MAIL uses the separately confirmed user-entered recipient below. S6 directions/deep-link/share use their three literal origins above. `ProviderActionCard` receives defaulted optional handoff context so its DEBUG fixture remains source-compatible.

Presentation action/provider/script strings are trimmed NFC; action/provider/subject are at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes and mail body script at most 8,192. Phone is at most 64 bytes; URL and mail recipient at most 4,096; the recipient must be one RFC-5322-address-shaped ASCII value with no display name, CR, or LF; subject is at most 998 UTF-8 bytes and has no CR/LF. `payload_sha256` is exactly 64 lowercase hex. Source references are at most 16 unique 1–256-byte IDs. `display_deadline` is exact `{at,label}` where request `at` is `CallableTimestampWireV1`, server storage is the parsed Timestamp, and label is trimmed NFC 1–256 bytes; it has no general five-minute bound. The complete snapshot is at most 16,384 canonical bytes. A field not applicable to the chosen adapter is forbidden rather than stored null.

For `movers_inventory_share`, the user first reviews the exact UTF-8 value returned by current `InventoryLockedView.shareText(for:)`; the client stores only its lowercase SHA-256 in `share_derivation` and then begins the session. Before every first/continued presenter invocation—including server-active/local-absent reconstruction—`MoversEquipView` re-derives the value from its current `inventoryRooms` and requires the digest to match. Match presents those re-derived bytes. Mismatch or unavailable inventory never opens and offers “Review updated inventory” or Cancel; review cancels the old session, shows the new payload, and only a fresh user confirmation may create a new begin. No inventory payload is written to UserDefaults or the server, and no protected Inventory path changes. Tests cover unchanged reconstruction, inventory edit/deletion, local-store loss, digest mismatch, and cancel/rebegin.

`wakeEvidence` is exact `{schema_version:1,task_instance_id,wake_id,cause,fired_at,resume_destination,urgency:"normal"|"urgent_recovery",urgency_basis?,intent_id}`. `cause` is the disjoint union `{kind:"TRIGGER",original_trigger,event_high_water?}` or `{kind:"THRESHOLD",threshold_id,deadline_evidence_id,threshold_at,consequence_class?,displaced_trigger?}`. TRIGGER requires the fired date/event copy and requires `event_high_water` exactly for an event; it forbids every threshold member. THRESHOLD requires current server deadline authority, carries optional class exactly when the policy threshold does, and may carry the displaced active unfired date/event trigger; it forbids original-trigger/event members. Every newly created valid-policy wake atomically creates its one intent, so `intent_id` is required and equals that notification-intent document ID. An in-place normal→urgent upgrade retains its existing pointer and does not create another intent. `urgency_basis` is required exactly for urgent recovery and is exact `{threshold_id,deadline_evidence_id,threshold_at,consequence_class?,consequence_rank?}`. Class and rank are both present or both absent; when present, class equals threshold projection/cause/policy and rank equals the immutable registry lookup. Both are absent for the Phase 2 production empty registry. The basis is forbidden for normal urgency. `urgent_recovery` is written only under §5.2's current server-authored threshold proof; otherwise urgency is exactly `normal`.

The server-owned root `thresholdProjection` is forbidden on policy-absent/malformed tasks and exact on an eligible policy task: `{schema_version:1,state:"armed"|"attended",task_instance_id,policy_fingerprint,threshold_id,threshold_at,deadline_evidence_id,consequence_class?}`. Its selected threshold is always `hard_threshold ?? safe_threshold` from the one trigger policy whose protected outcome matches the current exact deadline evidence; ambiguous distinct candidates invalidate projection creation rather than selecting by order. The trusted deadline-import seam writes/refreshes the task evidence and projection atomically; every server reducer that changes pinned policy, instance, deadline evidence, or terminal/nonterminal state either recomputes it from the same source or removes it. Clients and direct rules cannot create/update/delete it. A valid U/A/W/D task with matching authority has `armed` until the first threshold-attention transaction and `attended` afterward; a fresh disposition before the threshold may re-arm only by a transaction that also invalidates/cancels the old wake/intent. `threshold_at`, ID, class, fingerprint, instance, and evidence must remain byte-identical to policy/evidence. The projection is query authority only, never display/deadline evidence. Rules and reducer fixtures deny forged/stale/class-drifted projections and prove importer, disposition, threshold, terminal, H54, reset, and exact replay transitions.

`resolution` is exact `{schema_version:1,reason_code,policy_version,resolved_at,external_submission_archive?}`; `evidence_version` is forbidden for every schema-v1 writer because no independent evidence-version authority exists. The archive is exactly §3.1 and is present iff monotonic external-submission evidence exists at terminalization. `reason_code` is one of these literal producer-derived values: `ALREADY_HANDLED` for direct assertion; `POLICY_OUTCOME_COMPLETED` for a terminal COMPLETE outcome including a replacement's named confirmation outcome; `NOT_APPLICABLE` for the successful contradiction-`none` disposition; `PLAN_SUPERSEDED_INTERNAL` for internal H54 retirement; `AMENDMENT_CONFIRMED` for original-task CF terminalization; or `AMENDMENT_CANCELLED_UNTOUCHED` for the untouched replacement internally retired by PA reopen. Completed and Not Applicable policy tasks require resolution. `Dismissed` requires one exact H54 terminal pairing: an internal original is RETIRED with `PLAN_SUPERSEDED_INTERNAL`; a confirmed original is SUPERSEDED by the replacement instance with `AMENDMENT_CONFIRMED`; and an untouched replacement discarded by initial-PA reopen is RETIRED with `AMENDMENT_CANCELLED_UNTOUCHED`. Every nonterminal U/A/W/D/reopened task, the original PA/PC task, and a nonterminal replacement PA task forbids it except while it is present only inside an immutable rollback snapshot; the Completed replacement PC/CF task retains its `POLICY_OUTCOME_COMPLETED` resolution. The reason is never caller text or a policy label; policy/outcome/reason specificity remains in the retained typed evidence/history. Terminal mapper/reducer fixtures cover every writer, wrong/missing/surplus reason, forbidden evidence version, archive presence, confirmed SUPERSEDED, both internal RETIRED cases, and nonterminal leakage.

Policy-bearing H54 uses disjoint original/replacement ownership. An original task alone may contain `planChangeCycle`, `pendingAmendmentTaskId`, and `planChangeHistory`, and it always forbids `planChangeLink`. A replacement task alone may contain `planChangeLink`, and it always forbids the cycle, pointer, and history. Both linked tasks contain the same positive safe-integer `planChangeRevision`; a later new cycle, never a transition within one cycle, increments it. Policy-absent legacy H54 retains the committed five-field/history behavior byte-for-byte and never mixes these schemas.

The original-only cycle is exact:

```text
planChangeState: retired|pending_amendment|pending_confirmation|confirmed|reopened
pendingAmendmentTaskId?: task document ID
planChangeCycle: {
  schema_version:1, revision, lifecycle_state,
  original_task_document_id, original_task_instance_id,
  replacement?: {
    task_document_id, task_instance_id, policy_fingerprint,
    amendment_profile_id, amendment_action_handoff_id,
    amendment_action_trigger_policy_id,
    original_verification_transition_code,
    confirmation_outcome_policy_id, confirmation_outcome_id,
    creation_document_sha256
  },
  pre_supersede_operation_id, pre_supersede_snapshot_sha256,
  confirmation_attempts:[{
    ordinal:1|2, snapshot_record_id, snapshot_sha256,
    source:{
      kind:"HANDOFF_OPERATION"|"WAITING_OPERATION"|"WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION",
      evidence_id, receipt_identity
    },
    confirmation_evidence_id?, confirmed_at?, undone_at?
  }],
  current_confirmation_ordinal?,
  confirmation_undo_used,
  first_confirmation_undo_until?,
  terminal_spawn_closure?
}
planChangeHistory:[{
  schema_version:1, revision,
  action:"supersede"|"replacement_outcome"|"confirm_amendment"|
         "undo_confirmation"|"reopen",
  request_identity:{kind:"OPERATION",operation_id}|
                   {kind:"WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION",submission_token},
  original_task_instance_id, replacement_task_instance_id?,
  snapshot_record_id, evidence_ids, occurred_at
} | {schema_version:1,action:"ROLLUP",count,first_at,last_at,digest}]
```

The replacement-only link is exact `{schema_version:1,role:"REPLACEMENT",original_task_document_id,original_task_instance_id,replacement_task_document_id,replacement_task_instance_id,plan_change_revision,state}`, where state is `pending_amendment|pending_confirmation|confirmed|retired`. All IDs/revision/instances match the original cycle. `creation_document_sha256` is lowercase SHA-256 of the complete canonical replacement document exactly as first created, including root PA state/link, policy identity/generation/revision, move-event projection, and initial dynamic state, and excluding only Firestore metadata not stored as fields. Initial-PA reopen transactionally hashes the current complete replacement document and requires byte equality; any status, dynamic state, flow/content field, history, policy, identity, or link change makes it touched and rejects reopen. No later state uses that hash as rollback authority.

The exhaustive lifecycle shapes are:

| Cycle state | Exact original cycle/pointer shape | Exact replacement shape |
|---|---|---|
| internal R | lifecycle `retired`; original `Dismissed + RETIRED + PLAN_SUPERSEDED_INTERNAL`; replacement/pointer/current/deadline/closure absent; attempts `[]`; undo false | no replacement |
| internal RO | retained internal cycle with lifecycle `reopened`; same absences; attempts `[]`; undo false | no replacement |
| initial PA | replacement and pointer required; attempts `[]`; current/deadline/closure absent; undo false | PA link; document hash equals creation hash |
| first PC | attempt 1 pending; current `1`; closure required even with empty entries; deadline absent; undo false | PC link; replacement Completed |
| first CF | attempt 1 confirmed; current `1`; closure and first undo deadline required; undo false; original `Dismissed + SUPERSEDED + AMENDMENT_CONFIRMED`, with `superseded_by` equal to the replacement instance | CF link; replacement `Completed + COMPLETED + POLICY_OUTCOME_COMPLETED` |
| PA after undo | attempt 1 undone; current absent; deadline/closure retained; undo true | PA link; restored non-flow semantics |
| second PC | attempt 1 undone plus attempt 2 pending; current `2`; deadline/closure retained; undo true | PC link; replacement Completed |
| second CF | attempt 1 undone plus attempt 2 confirmed; current `2`; deadline/closure retained; undo true; original has the same exact `Dismissed + SUPERSEDED` pairing to the current replacement instance | CF link; replacement `Completed + COMPLETED + POLICY_OUTCOME_COMPLETED` |
| external RO | legal only from untouched initial PA; lifecycle `reopened`; replacement provenance retained; pointer/current/deadline/closure absent; attempts `[]`; undo false | retired link; untouched replacement internally retired as `Dismissed + RETIRED` with `AMENDMENT_CANCELLED_UNTOUCHED`; `superseded_by` forbidden |

A pending attempt forbids `confirmation_evidence_id`, `confirmed_at`, and `undone_at`; a confirmed attempt requires the evidence ID/time and forbids `undone_at`; an undone attempt requires all three. Ordinals are stable and ordered `[1]` or `[1,2]`. `first_confirmation_undo_until` is exactly first attempt `confirmed_at + 300 seconds`, is written only on first CF, and is retained after undo. `confirmation_undo_used` changes false→true exactly once and never reverses. `terminal_spawn_closure` is absent before the first PA→PC outcome, created by that outcome, required thereafter even when `entries:[]`, retained unchanged through CF/undo/second PC/second CF, and revalidated rather than recomputed on the second outcome. Every reference is checked against loaded immutable instances before mutation, so reset/recreation cannot alias a cycle.

`planChangeHistory` uses stored append order and retains 15 ordinary rows plus one oldest-position rollup. When ordinary count exceeds 15, remove exactly the oldest overflow rows; digest each complete canonical removed row; replace any prior rollup with `{schema_version:1,action:"ROLLUP",count,first_at,last_at,digest}` where count/times update as in §3.1 and `digest = SHA-256(canonical {domain:"plan_change_history.v1",prior_rollup: prior ? {count,first_at,last_at,digest} : null,appended_row_digests})`. `snapshot_record_id` is the pre-supersede operation ID for supersede/reopen and the current confirmation snapshot ID for replacement-outcome/confirm/undo. `evidence_ids` is the stable creation-order list made by that transition, including empty. A workflow replacement outcome uses the workflow request-identity variant; every other action uses OPERATION. The rollup is audit only. Fixtures cover 15→16, repeated rollups, workflow identity, exact replay, digest tampering, and every lifecycle presence/absence cell.

`semanticSnapshotV1(task)` is exact `{schema_version:1,status,disposition_contract?,task_interaction_state?,active_handoff?,wake_evidence?,resolution?}`. It excludes flow/content/immutable fields, interaction and plan-change histories, every H54 control/link field, and external intent documents. Every valid-policy writer and mapper requires this complete projection to be at most 262,144 canonical bytes, in addition to the component and whole-task caps, so every admitted live task is snapshotable. `pre_supersede_snapshot_sha256 = SHA-256(canonical semanticSnapshotV1(original_before_supersede))`; the named supersede operation contains that snapshot and matching hash.

Immediately before the named replacement outcome mutates either task and PA→PC, the same transaction creates client-denied `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{snapshot_record_id}` with exact `{kind:"CONFIRMATION_SNAPSHOT",schema_version:1,account_uid,plan_change_revision,ordinal,original_task_document_id,original_task_instance_id,replacement_task_document_id,replacement_task_instance_id,source,original_snapshot,replacement_snapshot,snapshot_sha256,created_at}`. `snapshot_record_id = "pcs1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,original_task_instance_id,replacement_task_instance_id,plan_change_revision,ordinal,source}))`; `snapshot_sha256 = SHA-256(canonical {original_snapshot,replacement_snapshot})`. Each snapshot is `semanticSnapshotV1`, each is at most 262,144 canonical bytes, and the complete confirmation record is at most 589,824 canonical bytes. Oversize rejects before any outcome mutation. The cycle appends ordinal 1, or ordinal 2 after the sole undo, with the ID/hash/source. Taking a later snapshot in `confirmAmendment` is forbidden.

For `HANDOFF_OPERATION`/`WAITING_OPERATION`, `receipt_identity` is exact `{operation_id}`; for `WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION` it is exact `{submission_token}`. `evidence_id` is the current replacement POLICY_OUTCOME wrapper. The named outcome transaction also writes this source into the cycle, so PC is independently recoverable after response loss, local-store loss, relaunch, or auth purge. `PeezyCardFirestoreMapper` exposes only that bounded typed source and linkage. The PC task surface renders “Finish plan update”; it reconstructs the exact confirmation request from the cycle rather than querying evidence or trusting a label. A scheduler firing the original verification trigger while PC creates a row route to that recovery surface, never a waiting-outcome route. `confirmAmendment` rereads the named snapshot record, source receipt/submission, complete wrapper evidence, both task documents/instances, and both PC markers in one transaction; it verifies and copies the §2.3 replacement-evidence tuple into a new original-instance AMENDMENT_CONFIRMATION record, writes the original `Dismissed + SUPERSEDED` contract naming the replacement instance plus `AMENDMENT_CONFIRMED` resolution, retains the snapshot/source records, and advances both markers to CF. It never inserts the replacement's evidence row into the original or resolves that copied ID later. Undo rereads the same immutable snapshot pair, validates all restoration triggers before mutation, restores its non-flow semantic fields subject to the mandated fresh-session/no-old-intent transformations, performs §3.1's same-instance evidence/history stable union, advances both epochs/revisions, sets both markers back to PA, clears current ordinal, sets `confirmation_undo_used:true`, and marks attempt 1 `undone_at`. Reopen applies the same evidence/history union to the pre-supersede snapshot; initial-PA reopen internally retires the untouched proposed replacement with RETIRED and never marks either task SUPERSEDED. A second named outcome freezes ordinal 2; a second undo is forbidden from every state. Tests cover PA→snapshot loss→PC recovery→CF, handoff/wait/workflow sources, sign-out/relaunch, response loss at each transaction, trigger fire during PC, evidence digest/provenance mismatch, reset/recreated or concurrently reset replacement failure with zero write, PA→PC→CF→undo→PA with every same-instance typed evidence edge resolvable after rollup, confirmed SUPERSEDED versus both internal RETIRED pairings, retired/PA reopen retention, second outcome/reconfirm, and second-undo denial.

All records in server/client-denied `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{recordId}` are one exact disjoint union; an existing malformed or wrong-kind record is never adopted:

```text
ordinary operation = {
  schema_version:1,
  kind:"TASK_OPERATION"|"TASK_FIELD_OPERATION"|"INTENT_CLAIM",
  state:"COMMITTED",
  account_uid, operation_id, action,
  request_fingerprint, request_sha256,
  task_identities:[{
    role:"TARGET"|"ORIGINAL"|"REPLACEMENT",
    task_document_id, task_generation_epoch, task_instance_id:string|null
  }],
  prior_snapshots:[{
    role:"TARGET"|"ORIGINAL"|"REPLACEMENT",
    task_document_id, task_generation_epoch, task_instance_id:string|null,
    snapshot, snapshot_sha256
  }],
  contradiction_evaluation?: {
    reason_code, source_ref, source_version,
    selector_ids, result:"present", selection_digest
  },
  response, response_sha256,
  created_at, committed_at
}

active reset operation = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"RESET_OPERATION",
  state:"deleting"|"awaiting_local_reset",
  account_uid, operation_id, aliases,
  reason:"retake_assessment", request_fingerprint,
  expected_task_generation_epoch, task_generation_epoch, active_move_event_id,
  target_index,
  page_after_path?,
  deleted_counts:{tasks,notification_intents,
                  task_deadline_evidence,confirmation_snapshots},
  deleted_count,
  lease?:{owner_token,expires_at},
  created_at, updated_at, awaiting_local_reset_at?
}

finalized reset tombstone = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"RESET_OPERATION", state:"finalized",
  account_uid, operation_id, aliases,
  reason:"retake_assessment", request_fingerprint,
  expected_task_generation_epoch, task_generation_epoch, active_move_event_id,
  deleted_counts:{tasks,notification_intents,
                  task_deadline_evidence,confirmation_snapshots},
  deleted_count, final_receipt_digest, created_at, finalized_at
}

packing clear operation = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"PACKING_CLEAR_OPERATION",
  state:"scanning"|"deleting"|"finalized"|"failed_validation",
  account_uid, operation_id, action:"clearPackingPlan",
  expected_task_generation_epoch, request_fingerprint,
  scan_after_path?, scanned_count, deleted_task_count,
  auxiliary_index, deleted_auxiliary_count,
  failure_code?, final_response?,
  created_at, updated_at, finalized_at?, failed_at?
}

legacy packing persist operation = {
  schema_version:1, kind:"LEGACY_PACKING_PERSIST_OPERATION",
  state:"COMMITTED", account_uid, operation_id,
  task_generation_epoch, request_fingerprint, request_sha256,
  expectation_digest, set_path_digest, delete_path_digest,
  set_count, delete_count,
  response, response_sha256, created_at, committed_at
}

confirmation snapshot = the exact `CONFIRMATION_SNAPSHOT` map above
```

For an ordinary record, `recordId == operation_id`; `request_sha256 = SHA-256(complete accepted-request canonical bytes)` and `request_fingerprint = "op1_" + request_sha256`. The domain is the strict request canonicalizer except that policy notes/quotes plus legacy `REPLACE_QUOTES`, `COMPLETE_BOOKING`, and `COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION` use `TaskCanonicalV1` for the complete accepted request; `persistLegacyPackingPlan` uses the same complete-map rule in the bulk record below. Every callable wire timestamp remains its exact string in those bytes, `operationId` is included, and authenticated transport context is omitted. `TASK_FIELD_OPERATION` is required exactly for `initializeFlowProgress`, `continueFlowProgress`, `clearFlowProgress`, `replaceTaskNotes`, and `replaceTaskQuotes`; `INTENT_CLAIM` is required exactly for `claimTaskIntent`; every other single-/two-task action uses `TASK_OPERATION`. `response` is the action-specific closed typed response/descriptor defined in §§4.2–4.7 with `replayed:false`, capped at 65,536 canonical bytes; `response_sha256` hashes that exact map. Replay returns the stored response with only transport projection `replayed:true` and never rewrites the record, subject to §4.7's live-generation adoption check. `task_identities` is empty only for INTENT_CLAIM, otherwise contains every request-targeted task in TARGET or ORIGINAL/REPLACEMENT order; generation is always present and instance is the exact string or explicit null. `prior_snapshots` contains one `semanticSnapshotV1` for every preexisting task actually mutated, with the same generation/string-or-null identity and role order, and is empty for claim and every zero-task-mutation resolver/evaluator receipt; a task created by the transaction has identity but no prior snapshot. Each snapshot hash is SHA-256 of its exact canonical map. The `contradiction_evaluation` member is present only for a committed `markNotApplicable` `present` resolver receipt and is copied from §2.2; unknown creates no operation, while terminal `none` uses ordinary task evidence and forbids this member. `created_at == committed_at == server_write_time` for that committed attempt. An ordinary record is at most 589,824 canonical bytes.

`LEGACY_PACKING_PERSIST_OPERATION` is the sole snapshot-free bulk exception and may be created only by `persistLegacyPackingPlan`. Its complete request binding and exact digest preimages are:

```text
bulk_request_preimage = TaskCanonicalV1(exact complete accepted request)
request_sha256 = SHA-256(bulk_request_preimage)
request_fingerprint = "op1_" + request_sha256
expectation_digest = SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({
  expected_packing_plan:request.expectedPackingPlan,
  expected_readiness:request.expectedReadiness,
  task_sets:request.taskSets.map(({taskDocumentId,expectedExisting}) =>
    ({task_document_id:taskDocumentId,expected_existing:expectedExisting})),
  stale_task_deletes:request.staleTaskDeletes.map(({taskDocumentId,expectedExisting}) =>
    ({task_document_id:taskDocumentId,expected_existing:expectedExisting}))
}))
set_paths = ["users/{account_uid}/packingPlan/current"]
  + request.taskSets.map(x => "users/{account_uid}/tasks/" + x.taskDocumentId)
delete_paths = request.staleTaskDeletes.map(x =>
    "users/{account_uid}/tasks/" + x.taskDocumentId)
  + (request.deleteReadiness ? ["users/{account_uid}/readiness/current"] : [])
set_path_digest = SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1(set_paths))
delete_path_digest = SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1(delete_paths))
set_count = set_paths.length
delete_count = delete_paths.length
```

The bulk preimage includes `action`, `operationId`, and every other accepted request member, strips nothing, and omits only authenticated transport context. `{account_uid}` above is substituted with the authenticated UID before the three path-digest preimages are hashed; no braces remain. The order is exactly as shown and inherits §4.7's path-ordered request arrays. The exact retained response is at most 65,536 bytes; the complete operation is at most 131,072 bytes. It never embeds 258 prior snapshots, task maps, plan bytes, or caller data. Fresh transaction validation nevertheless compares every complete request expectation before its atomic batch. Replay rereads root generation, recomputes request SHA/fingerprint/digests, and returns the retained response only while current; stale generation returns `STALE_TASK_GENERATION`. Any other action/kind reuse rejects.

For a reset request let `e = expectedTaskGenerationEpoch`, require safe `r = e + 1`, and derive `reset_record_id = "rso1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({account_uid:auth.uid,task_generation_epoch:r})))`. Its sole canonical path is `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{reset_record_id}`; `operation_id == reset_record_id`. Caller operation IDs are `ResetAliasV1`, exact `rsa1_` followed by one lowercase RFC 4122 UUID. They are aliases stored only in that record and never create alias documents. Before appending a new alias, the transaction also requires `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{alias}` absent; every non-reset action rejects the reserved `rsa1_` namespace, so an accepted alias cannot collide now or later with an ordinary record identity. `aliases` is the unique first-seen ordered list, contains the winning suggestion first, and is capped at 16. A later distinct alias after capacity is not persisted but must still satisfy the absent-alias-path check and resolves from `(uid,e)` to the same record/receipt; capacity can never create another reset. `expected_task_generation_epoch == e`, `task_generation_epoch == r`, and the §4.4 family fingerprint binds `e`. Counts are safe nonnegative integers and `deleted_count` equals their sum. The complete active record is capped at 32,768 `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes and the complete finalized tombstone at 16,384; either one byte over its cap fails closed before a write.

An active `deleting` record requires target index `0...3`, permits only that target's document-ID `page_after_path` and an unexpired UUID owner-token lease, and forbids awaiting/final members. A completed target atomically increments the index, clears the page cursor, and releases the lease. `awaiting_local_reset` requires target index `4`, no cursor/lease, and `awaiting_local_reset_at`. Finalization does not retain the active record: before exposing the first final receipt, its transaction deletes the marker and replaces the active map with the exact permanent tombstone above. Active-only `target_index`, cursor, counts-in-progress lease, `updated_at`, and awaiting time are forbidden on the tombstone. The normalized final receipt is reconstructed exactly as `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"reset_final",operationId:reset_record_id,replayed:false,accountUid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch:e,taskGenerationEpoch:r,activeMoveEventId,deletedCount,deletedCounts:{tasks,notificationIntents,taskDeadlineEvidence,confirmationSnapshots},state:"finalized"}` from the tombstone projection and is capped at 8,192 canonical bytes. `final_receipt_digest` is the full lowercase SHA-256 of those `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes. Every terminal lookup recomputes and verifies it, returns that receipt with only `replayed:true`, and never increments generation, recreates a marker, or deletes another document. Digest mismatch, malformed deterministic-path content, or UID/epoch/formula mismatch fails closed. Tombstones have no TTL/eviction and remain one bounded document per reset epoch. A terminal alias append may change only the bounded `aliases` list: `created_at`, `finalized_at`, the receipt projection, and `final_receipt_digest` remain byte-identical.

The active user-root marker is named `taskReset` and is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"reset",operationId,requestFingerprint,state:"deleting"|"awaiting_local_reset",expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,taskGenerationEpoch,activeMoveEventId,targetIndex,pageAfterPath?,deletedCounts:{tasks,notificationIntents,taskDeadlineEvidence,confirmationSnapshots},deletedCount,lease?,createdAt,updatedAt,awaitingLocalResetAt?}`. `operationId` is the deterministic `reset_record_id`, never a caller alias. It is present iff the matching canonical record is active. The total equality projection is `schemaVersion↔schema_version`, literal `kind:"reset"↔kind:"RESET_OPERATION"`, `operationId↔operation_id`, `requestFingerprint↔request_fingerprint`, identical lowercase `state`, `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch↔expected_task_generation_epoch`, `taskGenerationEpoch↔task_generation_epoch`, `activeMoveEventId↔active_move_event_id`, `targetIndex↔target_index`, optional `pageAfterPath↔page_after_path`, `deletedCounts.tasks↔deleted_counts.tasks`, `deletedCounts.notificationIntents↔deleted_counts.notification_intents`, `deletedCounts.taskDeadlineEvidence↔deleted_counts.task_deadline_evidence`, `deletedCounts.confirmationSnapshots↔deleted_counts.confirmation_snapshots`, `deletedCount↔deleted_count`, optional `lease.ownerToken↔lease.owner_token`, optional `lease.expiresAt↔lease.expires_at`, `createdAt↔created_at`, `updatedAt↔updated_at`, and optional `awaitingLocalResetAt↔awaiting_local_reset_at`. Lease is wholly present or wholly absent on both sides. Every projected value is byte-equal. Marker absence with an active operation is malformed except inside the transaction that finalizes it; a tombstone always requires marker absence. Progress receipts use the public reset union with `kind:"reset_progress"`, lowercase state `deleting|awaiting_local_reset`, and committed counts. `created_at` is immutable and `updated_at` changes on each committed page/state/alias transition. Fixtures freeze deterministic ID, alias order/cap, marker, active operation, progress, awaiting, tombstone, digest reconstruction, every crash boundary, and replay; replay changes only `replayed`.

PACKING_CLEAR_OPERATION uses `request_fingerprint = "packing1_" + SHA-256(canonical {action:"clearPackingPlan",expectedTaskGenerationEpoch})`. Counts are safe nonnegative integers; `scanned_count/deleted_task_count <= 130`, `deleted_auxiliary_count <= auxiliary_index <= 2`. `scanning` permits only `scan_after_path?`; `deleting` forbids it; both active states forbid failure/final members. `finalized` requires `final_response` and `finalized_at`, literal `auxiliary_index:2`, `deleted_task_count == scanned_count`, and forbids cursor/failure members. `failed_validation` requires `failure_code` and `failed_at`, forbids cursor/finalized time, and retains the exact error response. The closed failure codes are `ROW_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `ROW_INVALID`, `AUXILIARY_INVALID`, and `FENCE_INVALID`; the first three map to `PACKING_CLEAR_INVALID`, while only the last maps to `PACKING_CLEAR_FENCE_INVALID`.

The active root marker is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"packing_clear",operationId,requestFingerprint,taskGenerationEpoch,state:"scanning"|"deleting",scanAfterPath?,scannedCount,deletedTaskCount,auxiliaryIndex,deletedAuxiliaryCount,startedAt,updatedAt}`. Its total equality projection is `schemaVersion↔schema_version`, literal `kind:"packing_clear"↔kind:"PACKING_CLEAR_OPERATION"`, `operationId↔operation_id`, `requestFingerprint↔request_fingerprint`, `taskGenerationEpoch↔expected_task_generation_epoch`, identical lowercase `state`, optional `scanAfterPath↔scan_after_path`, `scannedCount↔scanned_count`, `deletedTaskCount↔deleted_task_count`, `auxiliaryIndex↔auxiliary_index`, `deletedAuxiliaryCount↔deleted_auxiliary_count`, `startedAt↔created_at`, and `updatedAt↔updated_at`; every projected value is byte-equal. It is present iff the operation is active and absent iff the operation is `finalized` or `failed_validation`. Each callable invocation commits at most one scan, phase, single-document delete, auxiliary delete, validation-failure, or terminal transition. A same-operation active retry resumes exactly one next transition; a finalized/failed exact retry returns its retained response; a different operation while a valid marker exists returns no-commit `PACKING_CLEAR_ACTIVE` with `{activeOperationId}`. The client adopts that exact ID and retries `clearPackingPlan` with the same expected generation; the server—not the client—reads and validates the retained operation before advancing one transition. Transaction comparison prevents concurrent retries from advancing the same state twice; no lease is needed or permitted.

The response union is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"packing_clear_progress",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskGenerationEpoch,state:"scanning"|"deleting",scannedCount,deletedTaskCount,auxiliaryIndex,deletedAuxiliaryCount,totalDeletedCount}` or `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"packing_clear_final",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskGenerationEpoch,state:"finalized",scannedCount,deletedTaskCount,auxiliaryIndex:2,deletedAuxiliaryCount,totalDeletedCount}` or `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"packing_clear_error",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskGenerationEpoch,state:"failed_validation",reason:"PACKING_CLEAR_INVALID"|"PACKING_CLEAR_FENCE_INVALID",failureCode:"ROW_LIMIT_EXCEEDED"|"ROW_INVALID"|"AUXILIARY_INVALID"|"FENCE_INVALID",scannedCount,deletedTaskCount,auxiliaryIndex,deletedAuxiliaryCount,totalDeletedCount}`. `totalDeletedCount == deletedTaskCount + deletedAuxiliaryCount`; final additionally requires `deletedTaskCount == scannedCount`, and reason/failure pairing is the closed mapping above. A row/auxiliary/fence validation failure commits the `failed_validation` operation, removes any active marker in the same transaction, and returns this typed terminal value rather than a Firebase callable error. Exact terminal replay changes only `replayed`. Reset rotation atomically removes an active marker and changes its operation to `failed_validation/FENCE_INVALID` with `PACKING_CLEAR_FENCE_INVALID`; that response can never authorize a later-generation delete. Other no-commit reasons are `RESET_ACTIVE`, `STALE_TASK_GENERATION`, `REQUEST_INVALID`, and `OPERATION_REUSED`. Fixtures kill before/after every scan/delete/auxiliary/failure/final transaction and prove exact marker-operation equality, one transition per invocation, concurrent retry serialization, lost response, active adoption, reset fencing, all failure codes, typed first return and replay, terminal cursor/index/count equality, final replay after marker removal, and every surplus member.

Confirmation-snapshot record IDs use reserved `pcs1_`; canonical per-epoch reset records use reserved `rso1_`; every other record ID is the validated operation ID. Cross-kind reuse, hash mismatch, missing/surplus members, altered response/snapshot/count/alias, malformed timestamp/lease, and a preoccupied deterministic ID fail with zero task/intent/reset mutation. Constructive worst-case tests combine a 196,608-byte state, 16,384-byte handoff, maximum valid contract/wake/resolution within the 262,144-byte semantic cap, one- and two-task operation arrays, 32 evidence-rooting history rows, 128 evidence rows, and H54 links. Exact/equal cap values must remain snapshotable and executable; one byte above a component, semantic-snapshot, operation, confirmation-record, task-state, history, or whole-task cap rejects atomically while exact replay remains available.

Notification intent storage and local route storage are:

```text
users/{uid}/notificationIntents/{intentId} {
  schema_version:1, audience:"user", kind:"task_resume",
  state:pending|consumed|cancelled,
  task_document_id, task_instance_id, interaction_epoch, interaction_revision,
  policy_fingerprint, route:{kind:row|outcome,session_id?},
  cause:{wake_evidence_id}, created_at, expires_at,
  consumed_at?, claim_operation_id?
}

AppRouteInbox store = {
  schemaVersion:1,
  records:[
    {intentId,claimOperationId,receivedAtEpochMilliseconds,
     expiresAtEpochMilliseconds,phase:"RECEIVED",
     lastForbiddenAccountUid?,lastForbiddenAuthEpochUUID?,
     refreshLease?:{attempt_id,started_at,expires_at},
     refreshNamespace?:{accountUid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision}}
  | {intentId,claimOperationId,receivedAtEpochMilliseconds,
     expiresAtEpochMilliseconds,phase:"CLAIM_DISPATCHED",
     dispatchAccountUid,dispatchAuthEpochUUID,dispatchCredentialRevision,
     dispatchedAtEpochMilliseconds}
  | {intentId,claimOperationId,receivedAtEpochMilliseconds,
     expiresAtEpochMilliseconds,phase:"CLAIMED",
     claimedAccountUid,claimedAuthEpochUUID,claimedRoute:PersistedTaskRouteV1}
  | {intentId,claimOperationId,receivedAtEpochMilliseconds,
     expiresAtEpochMilliseconds,phase:"PRESENTING",
     claimedAccountUid,claimedAuthEpochUUID,claimedRoute:PersistedTaskRouteV1,
     presentationStartedAtEpochMilliseconds}
  ]
}

PersistedTaskRouteV1 = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"task_route",claimOperationId,replayed,
  accountUid,intentId,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,
  interactionEpoch,policyFingerprint,
  route:{kind:"row"|"outcome",sessionId?},expiresAtEpochMilliseconds}
```

For this Phase 2 `task_resume` kind, cause is never operation-based: `intent.cause` is exactly `{wake_evidence_id:wakeEvidence.wake_id}`, and `wakeEvidence.intent_id` equals the intent document ID. Create, exact retry, fresh-command cancellation, claim consumption, and reset cancellation preserve or remove the pair atomically; validators reject a missing, dangling, crossed-task, crossed-instance, or unequal pointer. No other intent producer is named in this phase.

Every local aggregate whose phase is authority before an await/opener uses the same crash-durable file protocol, never UserDefaults. Each target is one self-verifying file—there is no two-file checksum/sidecar race—with exact outer `DurableFileEnvelopeV1 = {schemaVersion:1,fileKind,generationId,payload,sha256}`. `generationId` is a new lowercase RFC 4122 UUID for each committed replacement. `sha256` is 64 lowercase hex over the exact canonical UTF-8 preimage `{fileKind,generationId,payload,schemaVersion:1}`; the top-level `sha256` member is the sole excluded member. The complete envelope is canonical JSON with no trailing newline. Its closed file/payload mapping is:

| Target | `fileKind` | Exact `payload` root |
|---|---|---|
| `PeezyTaskRouteInbox-v1.json` | `TASK_ROUTE_INBOX_V1` | `{records:[AppRouteInbox record]}` |
| `PeezyHandoffAuthority-v1.json` | `HANDOFF_AUTHORITY_V1` | `{installation:{installationId},auth:signedAuth,sessions:{records:[handoff record]},reconciliationCancels:[reconciliation record]}` |
| `PeezyTaskPlanReset-v2.json` | `TASK_PLAN_RESET_V2` | `{records:[ResetOperationRegistryRecordV2]}` |
| `PeezyWorkflowRequests-v2.json` | `WORKFLOW_REQUESTS_V2` | `{records:[workflow record],retiredDiagnostics:[diagnostic]}` |

`signedAuth` is exactly `{state:"SIGNED_OUT",installationId,epochUUID}` or `{state:"SIGNED_IN",uid,installationId,epochUUID,credentialRevision}`; all IDs are lowercase UUIDs except opaque UID, both installation IDs equal `payload.installation.installationId`, and `credentialRevision` is a safe nonnegative integer advanced durably after each successful same-UID forced token refresh. The signed-out shape forbids `uid/credentialRevision`; the signed-in shape requires both. The inline `schemaVersion` roots stated elsewhere are payload-schema descriptions and do not create another file root; their value is normalized into the corresponding payload above. Every per-store cap applies to the complete outer envelope.

The one owning process-wide actor for each target serializes and validates the complete envelope, writes a same-directory unique temporary file with a complete POSIX write loop, applies `fsync` to that file, atomically `rename`s it over the target, then `fsync`s the Application Support directory before returning success. Ownership is exact: `AppRouteInbox` owns the complete route envelope; `HandoffSessionStore` owns the complete handoff envelope including installation, auth, sessions, and reconciliation cancels; `ResetOperationRegistry` inside `TaskPlanService.swift` owns the complete reset envelope; and `WorkflowService` owns the complete workflow envelope. No façade or consumer performs a direct file read/modify/write; it invokes its owner actor and receives an immutable validated snapshot/result. Files use protection `completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`. Startup accepts only exact outer/member sets, matching kind, canonical bytes, valid generation UUID, valid payload, and recomputed hash. A malformed/noncanonical/hash-mismatched target is never reset empty: before any dispatch, it is atomically renamed to the target's fixed sibling `PeezyTaskRouteInbox-v1.quarantine-v1.json`, `PeezyHandoffAuthority-v1.quarantine-v1.json`, `PeezyTaskPlanReset-v2.quarantine-v1.json`, or `PeezyWorkflowRequests-v2.quarantine-v1.json`, followed by directory `fsync`, and the actor enters `CORRUPT_BLOCKED`. If that quarantine already exists, neither file is overwritten/removed and the actor remains blocked. No callable, opener, presentation, callback, or synthesized empty store may begin until an explicit local recovery either validates/item-recovers the quarantined envelope into a new generation or the user explicitly discards it; discard removes only the exact named target/quarantine after showing its pending-record count when decodable. Injected tests kill after every write/fsync/rename/directory-sync/quarantine boundary and prove complete old or new value, tamper of every outer/payload/hash byte, signed-in/out transitions, existing quarantine, zero dispatch while blocked, item recovery, and explicit discard—never a torn or silently acknowledged-lost phase.

Intent expiry and local opaque-route TTL are both exactly 24 hours from server creation/local receipt respectively. At ingress the actor captures one safe epoch-millisecond integer `receivedAtEpochMilliseconds` and stores `expiresAtEpochMilliseconds = receivedAtEpochMilliseconds + 86,400,000`; overflow rejects. A successful claim replaces the record expiry with the lesser of that local value and the strictly parsed server receipt expiry. Notification payload key is exactly `peezy_task_intent_id`; URL grammar is scheme `peezy`, host `task`, path `/v1`, and one query pair named `intentId` whose value is the RFC 3986 percent-encoding of the opaque ID. Fragment, userinfo, alternate host/path, duplicate, or unknown keys fail. The route payload is ordered by `receivedAtEpochMilliseconds` and capped at eight records. Route receipt expiry is never persisted as a date string or floating-point `Date`: `PersistedTaskRouteV1` replaces only wire `expiresAt` with its safe epoch-millisecond integer, and all ordering/expiry/minimum/boundary comparisons use those integers. RECEIVED forbids dispatch/claimed/route/presentation fields; its two last-forbidden fields are both present or both absent. `refreshLease` and `refreshNamespace` are both present or both absent and are independent of last-forbidden markers. Immediately before awaiting the claim callable, the actor clears any expired/superseded refresh pair and durably writes CLAIM_DISPATCHED with the current signed-in UID/auth epoch/credential revision and epoch-millisecond time; this phase forbids last-forbidden, claimed, presentation, and refresh members. Relaunch or ambiguous transport retries the exact claim operation/bytes. CLAIMED requires account UID, claim-time auth epoch, and `PersistedTaskRouteV1` but forbids dispatch/presentation/last-forbidden/refresh fields; PRESENTING requires all four claimed/presentation values and has the same forbiddances. A successful claim durably replaces CLAIM_DISPATCHED with CLAIMED; immediately before automatic UI presentation it durably stores PRESENTING. A crash in CLAIMED replays/presents automatically only in the same UID+auth epoch; a crash in PRESENTING never auto-presents and exposes “Open task” only in that namespace. Thus server claim is consume-once, automatic UI is at-most-once, and ambiguous presentation remains recoverable without claiming exactly-once delivery.

The server authors `task_route.expiresAt` as the exact UTC `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.SSSZ` projection of intent `expires_at`; it obeys `CallableTimestampWireV1` including literal uppercase `Z` and exactly three fractional digits.
