It never returns a Firestore Timestamp/map, offset form, seconds-only string, or additional precision. Swift validates the exact grammar and Gregorian value before parsing once to a safe epoch-millisecond integer, then independently round-trips that integer to the identical wire string before constructing `PersistedTaskRouteV1`. JS and Swift parity fixtures cover leap-day and epoch boundaries, offsets, lowercase `z`, missing/extra fractional digits, invalid calendar values, safe-integer overflow, server/local minimum selection, and expiry at exactly equal/one-millisecond-before/one-millisecond-after boundaries. No route-expiry decision uses floating-point `Date` comparison.

Presentation acknowledgement is route-specific. A row route is removed only when the matching task row/detail `onAppear` acknowledges the same account, task instance, task ID, epoch, and fingerprint. An outcome route additionally matches the optional returned session. Sheet dismissal after acknowledgement has no inbox work. Manual “Open task” recovery from PRESENTING reuses the stored receipt in its exact namespace and waits for the same acknowledgement; it never calls claim again. A conclusively malformed phase/receipt pair is removed with a local diagnostic and never presented or reclaimed; there is deliberately no zero-loss claim.

The same intent reuses its first claim operation/receipt time. A ninth distinct receipt first removes expired records that are not in dispatch/presentation processing; if still full, it may evict only the oldest plain RECEIVED record with no live refresh lease and records a local diagnostic. A RECEIVED record with a live lease, plus every CLAIM_DISPATCHED, CLAIMED, and PRESENTING record, is protected from eviction. If all eight are protected, it rejects the new receipt and records a diagnostic; it never discards refresh/in-flight/ambiguous or server-consumed authority. No zero-loss claim is made. `AppRouteInbox` is one process-wide Swift `actor` and the sole reader/modify/replacer of this aggregate; URL, push, auth, scene, presentation, acknowledgement, overflow, and purge events call actor-isolated methods. It serializes one mutation at a time and atomically replaces the complete Codable value, so concurrent ingress cannot lose a record. Tests cover overflow during live refresh/in-flight claim, lost response, crash immediately after CLAIM_DISPATCHED, and exact relaunch replay.

Claim handling is exact over the complete callable union. The callable reads only `users/{auth.uid}/notificationIntents/{intentId}`; it performs no collection-group/global lookup and the opaque route exposes no account identifier. To make the adopted wrong-account retry requirement implementable without weakening that opacity, an absent document at that exact authenticated path deliberately returns `permission-denied/AUTH_FORBIDDEN` with zero operation or intent write. This result is nondisclosing: it means “not claimable in this account,” whether the ID belongs to another account or does not exist anywhere. A globally nonexistent opaque ID therefore remains fail-closed and locally retained only until its 24-hour TTL; it can never produce a route.

An initially unauthenticated RECEIVED record pauses. On deterministic `AUTH_REQUIRED`, the actor performs one envelope CAS requiring the record still be CLAIM_DISPATCHED with the same intent, claim operation, dispatch UID, dispatch auth epoch, and dispatch credential revision; it atomically returns it to RECEIVED, clears every dispatch/forbidden field, and preserves the operation. If a different currently durable valid UID/epoch or a higher credential revision already exists, the actor immediately invokes its queue again and dispatches under that namespace. Otherwise it atomically adds exact `refreshNamespace:{accountUid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision}` for that captured dispatch tuple and `refreshLease:{attempt_id,started_at,expires_at}` before invoking forced refresh. `attempt_id` is a fresh lowercase RFC 4122 UUID, `started_at` and `expires_at` are safe epoch-millisecond integers from one actor clock read, and `expires_at == started_at + 60,000`. A lease is live exactly while current durable auth still equals its namespace/revision and `now < expires_at`. A live lease survives process death and forbids every second forced refresh for that record. At `now >= expires_at`, or when a durable different UID/epoch or higher credential revision appears, whichever occurs first, the actor CAS-clears that lease/context; expiry may acquire a fresh attempt, while namespace/revision change immediately redispatches under the newer authority. A missing half, invalid UUID/time/relation, lower same-namespace credential revision, or lease on another phase is corrupt and dispatches nothing.

The refresh invocation captures the lease attempt ID and namespace. `HandoffSessionStore` performs its auth-envelope CAS only if signed auth still equals the captured signed-in UID/epoch/revision; matching refresh-API success then writes `credentialRevision = captured + 1`, and mismatch/failure writes nothing. Completion reenters `AppRouteInbox` and may mutate the route only if intent/claim, RECEIVED phase, lease `attempt_id`, and refresh namespace still match. A matching persisted success observes the exact increment, clears the lease/context, and queues claim dispatch; a matching failure leaves the live lease until expiry. A late completion from an expired/replaced attempt is a no-op. If the process crashes after lease persistence but before refresh invocation, no revision changes and the 60-second expiry permits a new attempt. If the refresh API succeeds but the process crashes before `HandoffSessionStore` persists the credential increment, auth remains at the captured tuple and the still-live lease suppresses overlap only until that same expiry. If the credential increment persists but the process crashes before inbox cleanup, the durable revision increase is sufficient on relaunch/observer delivery to clear the old lease and redispatch immediately. Thus A→refresh→B→late-A cannot publish A authority or dispatch under A, and none of the three crash boundaries wedges the record. Fixtures freeze live-lease no-overlap, exact expiry reacquisition, crash before invocation, API success then crash before auth persistence, auth revision commit then crash before route persistence, same-UID success without a second observer callback, failure then expiry, A→B and B→A, late completion after replacement, and every lease/member/CAS boundary.

`AUTH_FORBIDDEN` atomically returns a dispatched record to RECEIVED, copies its persisted `dispatchAccountUid/dispatchAuthEpochUUID` into the two last-forbidden fields, clears dispatch/refresh fields, skips only that dispatch namespace, and continues to the next record; the namespace current when the response arrives is irrelevant, and a different current namespace may retry. `INTENT_NOT_FOUND` is not emitted by this v1 claim path; receiving it is a protocol-version violation and is handled like `AUTH_FORBIDDEN`, not as conclusive absence. `INTENT_EXPIRED`, `INTENT_CANCELLED`, `INTENT_STALE`, `INTENT_ALREADY_CLAIMED`, `INTENT_INVALID`, or server-returned `REQUEST_INVALID` is conclusive and removes the record with a diagnostic, allowing the queue to continue; local request validation prevents REQUEST_INVALID dispatch. `OPERATION_REUSED` proves that the claim-operation document already belongs to different request bytes and that this intent was not mutated by that attempt; the actor generates one new lowercase UUID, atomically returns the record to RECEIVED with only `claimOperationId` replaced, records a collision diagnostic, and retries. `unavailable`, `deadline-exceeded`, `internal`, `unknown`, transport loss, and decode failure retain CLAIM_DISPATCHED and retry the identical operation/bytes. Task-only reasons `TASK_NOT_FOUND`, `RESET_ACTIVE`, `POLICY_INVALID`, `POLICY_MISMATCH`, `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`, `STALE_STATE`, `TRIGGER_INVALID`, `EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN`, `COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, `HANDOFF_MISMATCH`, `AUTH_EPOCH_MISMATCH`, and `WORKFLOW_BINDING_INVALID` are unreachable because claim normalizes all stale task/session/cause cases to INTENT_STALE; receiving one is a protocol-version violation, so the record is retained-but-skipped for this app build, a diagnostic is emitted, and the queue continues without retry storm. A success or exact claim replay atomically advances CLAIM_DISPATCHED to CLAIMED. A CLAIMED/PRESENTING record whose UID or auth epoch no longer matches is retained but blocked until expiry; it is never presented against another account or a regenerated task. After `TasksStore` is loaded in the matching namespace, a conclusively missing/stale instance removes the record with a diagnostic. Immediately before automatic presentation and again before any route opener, the actor rechecks current UID/auth epoch, receipt account/task instance, TasksStore namespace/identity/epoch/fingerprint, optional session, and active handoff namespace. Mandatory races include A-dispatch→B-current AUTH_REQUIRED immediate redispatch, same-UID forced-refresh completion with no second observer callback, repeated AUTH_REQUIRED under a live lease with no overlap, retry only after exact expiry/failure, and all URL/push/account/scene/reset cases previously named.

`claimedRoute` is exactly `PersistedTaskRouteV1`, derived from the validated wire `task_route` receipt with only `expiresAt` replaced by `expiresAtEpochMilliseconds`; it contains account UID and task instance. `row` forbids `sessionId`; `outcome` requires it exactly for a returned handoff and forbids it for a direct waiting transition. Before presenting a returned-session outcome, the inbox compares the task's active handoff installation/auth epoch with the current installation/auth record. Exact namespace match presents the outcome. Same installation with rotated auth epoch presents the handoff-reconciliation surface and lets `HandoffSessionStore` perform the exact AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED cancellation. A different installation presents “Continue on the other device” plus explicit “Restart here”; only the latter can perform the takeover cancel. Both mismatch branches retain the claimed receipt until that reconciliation surface appears, then acknowledge/remove the local route, but they send no handoff open acknowledgement, return, or outcome resolution and never display the outcome question. A direct-WAIT outcome has no handoff namespace and follows the ordinary outcome route. Tests claim the same returned-session intent from the opening installation, a second installation of the same UID, and the same installation after auth rotation.

The handoff authority payload stores one installation-level lowercase RFC 4122 UUID, generated once per app installation, plus §3.3's exact signed-in/signed-out auth union, session aggregate, and reconciliation-cancel array. `HandoffSessionStore` is the sole actor that reads or replaces any of those four members; auth observers, `AppRouteInbox`, scenes, adapters, and UI call actor methods only. Every begin stores both namespace IDs. The auth epoch persists across uninterrupted same-UID relaunch. Any observed signed-out state or UID change durably removes ordinary handoff presentation/session bytes for the old namespace and rotates only that installation's auth epoch before another begin/open; signing back into the same UID after observed sign-out gets a new epoch. It **retains every reconciliation-cancel record** until its exact terminal rule below, because purging a pre-await cancel would recreate the lost-request race. Route records follow their separate retain-but-block rule above.

`HandoffSessionStore` is one process-wide Swift `actor`, the sole mutator of the complete crash-durable `PeezyHandoffAuthority-v1.json` envelope. The session API below is one actor-isolated member projection; installation/auth/reconciliation methods use the same serialized whole-envelope read-modify-replace path. Auth, route refresh, scene, adapter callbacks, receipts, queued returns, reconstruction, and UI commands all enter actor-isolated methods:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,records:[{
  uid,installationId,authEpochUUID,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
  sessionId,interactionEpoch,policyFingerprint,actionId,adapterOperationId,
  origin:"LOCAL_BEGIN"|"SERVER_SNAPSHOT",
  phase:"PREPARED"|"BEGIN_DISPATCHED"|"OPEN_READY"|
        "OPEN_DISPATCHED"|"OPENED"|"AWAITING_CANCEL_TRIGGER"|"RETURNED",
  presentationSnapshot,flowAttemptProof?,createdAt,updatedAt,
  beginOperationId?,beginRequestCanonicalJSON?,beginRequestSHA256?,
  beginReceipt?,openedEvidence?,ackReceipt?,returnEvidence?,returnReceipt?,
  serverSnapshotCanonicalJSON?,serverSnapshotSHA256?,restoredByOperationId?,
  pending?:{kind:"BEGIN"|"CONTINUE"|"ACK"|"RETURN"|"CANCEL"|"RESOLVE",
            operationId,requestCanonicalJSON,requestSHA256,dispatchedAt},
  queuedReturnEvidence?,queuedCancellationEvidence?,restorationDescriptor?
}]}
```

The payload's disjoint `reconciliationCancels` member contains only exact records:

```text
{
  recordKind:"RECONCILIATION_CANCEL", uid,
  taskDocumentId, taskInstanceId, policyFingerprint, interactionEpoch,
  sessionId,
  storedNamespace:{installationId,authEpochUUID},
  requesterNamespace:{installationId,authEpochUUID},
  reasonCode:"AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED"|"USER_CONFIRMED_OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER",
  evidence,
  phase:"PREFLIGHT"|"AWAITING_TRIGGER"|"PREPARED"|"DISPATCHED"|
        "RECEIPT"|"APPLYING",
  restorationDescriptor?,
  operationId?, requestCanonicalJSON?, requestSHA256?,
  receipt?, applicationId?, createdAt, updatedAt
}
```

`evidence` is exactly the reason-paired cancellation evidence in §4.2. Same-epoch reconciliation is forbidden. AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED requires equal stored/requester installation IDs and different auth epochs; takeover requires different installation IDs. Before operation/request bytes exist, the record supplies that immutable branch to read-only cancel preflight. When preflight says no trigger, the actor creates a fresh operation and exact request with `confirmedTrigger` absent; when it requires one, only a user-confirmed trigger sourced from the descriptor policy may be included. `requestCanonicalJSON` is then the canonical complete accepted wire `cancelHandoff` request using the requester namespace and immutable stored task/session/policy tuple; every request timestamp remains `CallableTimestampWireV1`. Its SHA is lowercase SHA-256, and neither bytes nor operation ID changes after PREPARED. Records are unique by UID+task instance+session+reason+requester namespace, ordered by creation time then operation ID when present, capped at 16 and included in the 524,288-byte envelope cap. They are not part of ordinary session-record capacity.

Phase presence is total. PREFLIGHT forbids descriptor, operation/request, receipt, and application fields. AWAITING_TRIGGER requires an exact `CANCEL_PRIOR` descriptor with `requires_trigger:true` and forbids operation/request/receipt/application. PREPARED and DISPATCHED require operation ID plus canonical request/hash and forbid descriptor/receipt/application. RECEIPT requires those immutable request fields plus the exact typed matching cancel receipt and forbids descriptor/application; APPLYING adds `applicationId`. `createdAt` is immutable, `updatedAt` changes at each durable phase transition, and all are UTC RFC 3339 milliseconds. No phase may retain a field from a later phase, and no receipt/application ID may appear in the ordinary `sessions.records` member.

The actor first reconstructs/validates the server snapshot and durably writes PREFLIGHT before the read-only await. Preflight loss/relaunch repeats that read with no mutation. A no-trigger descriptor causes one atomic PREPARED write containing freshly generated immutable operation/request bytes. A required descriptor causes AWAITING_TRIGGER before the selector; cancellation leaves it recoverable, and confirmation alone creates PREPARED bytes. Immediately before the mutating await the actor writes DISPATCHED; ambiguous transport/relaunch retries identical bytes. Typed `TRIGGER_INVALID` atomically discards the denied operation/request, stores only the returned required descriptor, and returns to AWAITING_TRIGGER; it never infers from the snapshot. A matching success writes RECEIPT; before removing presentation/session bytes or invoking a local completion callback, it writes APPLYING with `applicationId = "hca1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,task_instance_id,session_id,operation_id}))`. In APPLYING it rereads TasksStore/server state: exact cancelled/resolved/absent-old-instance authority performs only idempotent local cleanup and then removes the reconciliation record; an active matching old session retains it and retries/reconciles without another callback. Deterministic no-commit AUTH/STALE errors retain the applicable PREFLIGHT/PREPARED phase and block until the exact requester namespace is durably current or a server read proves the session already terminal; changed requester namespace requires a **new** immutable record after the prior one is conclusively no-commit. Receipt account/task/session/operation mismatch enters `CORRUPT_BLOCKED` and is never applied. Auth purge, UID switch, and ordinary session cleanup cannot delete any reconciliation phase. Tests kill/lose response before and after all six phases for same-install epoch rotation and other-install takeover, and cover no-trigger, fired/expired date/event, selector cancellation/relaunch, preflight→mutation drift, sign-out, switch-back, second epoch rotation, exact retry, deterministic no-commit, receipt mismatch, callback crash, server already terminal, and terminal removal.

Records are unique by UID+installation+auth epoch+task instance+session, ordered by creation time/session ID, capped at 16, and the complete canonical store is capped at 524,288 UTF-8 bytes. Every date is UTC RFC 3339 milliseconds; every request/snapshot JSON is canonical and its companion hash is lowercase SHA-256. `LOCAL_BEGIN` requires the three begin fields and forbids server-snapshot fields. `SERVER_SNAPSHOT` requires the two snapshot fields, forbids all three begin fields, and is constructed only after the actor validates a current server `activeHandoff`; `restoredByOperationId` is required exactly for H54 `prepared` restoration. A reconstructed `prepared/open_requested/opened/returned` maps respectively to PREPARED/OPEN_READY/OPENED/RETURNED, copying only lifecycle evidence actually present. Thus local loss never fabricates a begin receipt.

PREPARED forbids lifecycle receipts/evidence. Local BEGIN_DISPATCHED requires pending BEGIN and is persisted before dispatch. Local OPEN_READY requires its begin receipt; reconstructed OPEN_READY instead requires a byte-valid current `open_requested` server snapshot. OPEN_DISPATCHED is durable before invoking an opener; a crash never auto-invokes again and exposes Continue/Cancel. OPENED/RETURNED require matching server lifecycle evidence; local-origin records additionally require their ack/return receipts, while reconstructed records use the validated server snapshot. H54 PREPARED persists pending CONTINUE before `continueHandoff`; its ambiguous result retries the exact operation/bytes and its exact receipt advances to OPEN_READY. ACK/RETURN/CANCEL/RESOLVE similarly persist before dispatch. Only one pending command exists. `queuedReturnEvidence` and `queuedCancellationEvidence` are mutually exclusive and compatible with the pinned adapter. They are permitted while ACK is pending/ambiguous; AWAITING_CANCEL_TRIGGER requires queued cancellation, no pending command, and the exact current `CANCEL_PRIOR` `restorationDescriptor`; every other phase forbids that descriptor. A quick return or SHARE/MAIL cancellation during ACK is stored once. After ACK exact replay/commit advances the server to opened, the actor persists a no-trigger RETURN directly. Before every RESTORE_PRIOR or MAIL-failure CANCEL mutation, however, it sends the read-only `cancelHandoff` preflight with the immutable session/evidence branch and no operation ID. A descriptor with `requires_trigger:false` causes the actor to persist and dispatch a new no-trigger CANCEL operation. A descriptor with `requires_trigger:true` causes it first to durably enter AWAITING_CANCEL_TRIGGER with that exact descriptor, then open the shared selector using only its policy ID. User confirmation persists a new CANCEL operation containing that trigger before dispatch. If state drifts and the mutating call returns typed `TRIGGER_INVALID`, the actor discards that failed request/operation ID, atomically replaces the stored descriptor with the returned one, and repeats the selector path; it never reads or guesses from the denied prior snapshot. Selector cancellation leaves AWAITING_CANCEL_TRIGGER recoverable with Retry/Keep task open and preserves the typed cancellation evidence. Relaunch resumes ACK first or restores the descriptor-bound trigger selector. A scheduler wake during ACK is therefore resolved by preflight plus the cancel transaction's current server snapshot, not stale client inference. Immediately before any opener invocation, the actor rechecks current UID/auth epoch, task instance, epoch, policy, session, action, adapter ID, share-payload digest when applicable, and state from TasksStore; mismatch never opens. Tests cover return/cancel/failure before ACK response, wake before and during ACK, preflight no-trigger mutation, preflight-required descriptor→durable selector, preflight/mutation drift, selector cancellation, lost preflight/ACK/cancel responses, and relaunch at every boundary.

Before a new preparation, the actor removes only records with a conclusively applied terminal cancel/resolve or a server-confirmed absent/stale session in its exact namespace. It never evicts PREPARED, any pending/ambiguous dispatch, OPEN_READY/OPEN_DISPATCHED/OPENED/AWAITING_CANCEL_TRIGGER/RETURNED, a reconciliation-cancel record, or a receipt awaiting local application. If 16 session records or 524,288 complete-envelope bytes remain, it rejects preparation before any server call/opener and shows a recoverable pending-actions diagnostic. Account switch follows the explicit purge/reconciliation rule below; any malformed/hash mismatch enters the common `CORRUPT_BLOCKED` quarantine protocol and never resets empty. Deterministic failure removes/rebases exactly as §5.4; ambiguous failure retries stored bytes. Tests cover the 17th record, all-pending capacity, concurrent callback RMW, every reconstructed server state, H54 CONTINUE ambiguity/relaunch/cancel, stale old adapter evidence, store corruption, and receipt-before-application.

A server handoff from the same installation but a different auth epoch is never reconstructed or opened; the newly authenticated same UID sends `cancelHandoff` with reason `AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED` and exact evidence `{kind:"AUTH_EPOCH_ROTATED",installationId,newAuthEpochUUID}`. A different installation neither reconstructs nor auto-cancels. It shows “Continue on the other device” and an explicit “Restart here”; only the latter sends reason `USER_CONFIRMED_OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER` with `{kind:"OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER_CONFIRMED",newInstallationId,newAuthEpochUUID,observedAt}`. Both narrow fail-closed cancels are legal only from `prepared`, `open_requested`, `opened`, or `returned`; resolved/cancelled history accepts exact operation replay only. A fresh valid cancel restores the prior snapshot without asserting an outcome and advances task epoch/revision.

## 4. Semantic commands and producers

### 4.1 Operation and concurrency envelope

Fresh semantic task-mutating `changeTaskPlan` commands accept only common keys `{action,operationId,taskDocumentId,expectedTaskInstanceId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedInteractionEpoch,expectedInteractionRevision,policyVersion,policyFingerprint,flowAttemptProof?}` plus keys named below. The task snapshot requires the proof exactly when it has a live complete attempt and forbids it otherwise, so every flow-exiting semantic command clears atomically rather than relying on a later client clear. Handoff follow-ups accept the exact base `{action,operationId,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,sessionId,interactionEpoch,policyVersion,policyFingerprint,installationId,authEpochUUID}` plus their named evidence/input fields; they do not carry expected revision and read the current revision transactionally. Ordinary acknowledge/return/continue/resolve/cancel requires both namespace IDs equal the stored session. `AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED` cancel alone permits the same stored installation with a new auth epoch matching its evidence; `USER_CONFIRMED_OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER` alone permits a new installation/auth epoch matching its evidence. H54 keeps its existing semantic members and adds the exact generation/instance expectations below. Reset/finalize use exactly `{action,operationId,reason:"retake_assessment",expectedTaskGenerationEpoch}`; the safe nonnegative expected epoch is captured before the first reset await and retained unchanged through recovery/finalize, and `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch + 1` must be safe. The five policy flow/content actions, packing clear, and legacy actions use their exact alternative bases in §§4.2/4.7. `claimTaskIntent` is the other non-task-envelope exception and accepts exactly `{action:"claimTaskIntent",intentId,claimOperationId}` because task/policy/instance authority is resolved from the server-only intent. No action may combine members from two bases.

`cancelHandoff`, `undoConfirmation`, and `reopen` each also have one read-only preflight variant. It uses the same action and all identity/namespace/policy/reason/evidence/expected-replacement members required to select that mutating branch, replaces the mutating `operationId` with literal `preflight:true`, and forbids `confirmedTrigger`, `restorationTriggers`, `flowAttemptProof`, and `flowAttemptProofs`. No other action accepts `preflight`; a request containing both preflight and mutation-only members is `REQUEST_INVALID`. The server performs the same current root/task/session/cycle/snapshot/policy reads in a read-only transaction, creates no operation/evidence/history/receipt record, clears no flow, and mutates no task, session, intent, or local/server state. The response is the exact restoration-preflight receipt in §4.2. Preflight is advisory current authority, not a reservation: the later mutating transaction rereads and validates everything.

Every callable first performs the recursive `CallableTimestampWireV1`/sentinel rejection in §2.1 and fingerprints the exact accepted wire map. During each Firestore transaction callback attempt, after its reads and fresh validations, the server obtains exactly one injected-clock value `server_write_time = Timestamp.fromMillis(clock.nowMillis())`; a transaction retry obtains a new value, while every server-authored time in the eventually committed attempt is byte-identical to that one concrete millisecond Timestamp. `FieldValue.serverTimestamp()` is forbidden even in server prospective maps. Operation/evidence/history times, `waiting_state.entered_at`, `pending_handoff.selected_at`, legacy completion/update times, booking `savedAt`, readiness `updatedAt`, and fresh synthetic-task `createdAt` are server-authored and forbidden in callable requests. The server parses semantic wire timestamps separately and never turns a client value into commit authority. Injected-clock tests assert concrete Timestamp type, within-commit equality, retry replacement, and exact replay without rewriting time.

For a valid policy-bearing H54 original, the additional common keys are exactly `expectedTaskInstanceId`, `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch`, `expectedInteractionEpoch`, `expectedInteractionRevision`, `policyVersion`, `policyFingerprint`, and optional `expectedReplacement`, `flowAttemptProofs`, `replacement`, `confirmationSource`, or `restorationTriggers` under the action rules below; the first six are required. `expectedReplacement` is exact `{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,interactionEpoch,interactionRevision,policyVersion,policyFingerprint}` and is required for confirm/undo and for reopen of a cycle that has a replacement, forbidden for supersede/internal-retired reopen/reset/finalize. It must match the exact cycle replacement before any evidence/trigger validation. `flowAttemptProofs` is exact `{original?,replacement?}` and each value is §4.2's proof; a member is required exactly when that task has a live complete tail the H54 transaction exits, forbidden otherwise, and both tasks are checked for a two-task action. A policy-absent H54 request instead requires `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch` and `expectedTaskInstanceId` as the exact stored nonblank string or explicit JSON null when absent. Whenever the named lifecycle reads or mutates a replacement it also requires exact `expectedReplacement:{taskDocumentId,taskGenerationEpoch,taskInstanceId}`, with that replacement instance likewise the stored nonblank string or explicit null. It rejects policy-only keys and otherwise retains Phase-1 semantic members and result shape. `reason` is trimmed 1–1,000 UTF-8 bytes.

Supersede alone accepts `replacement`, exact `{taskId,subject,institutionId,institution,amendmentAction,verification}` where both descriptors are `{confirmedTrigger,resumeDestination}` and are validated against the plan-change binding; `amendmentAction.resumeDestination` is literal `row`, `verification.resumeDestination` is literal semantic-WAIT intent `outcome`, and trusted display resolution alone determines whether the committed original projection is actual WAIT/`outcome` or fallback USER_ACTION/`row`. Replacement task/action/visible copy still derive from the catalog and named handoff, never descriptor labels. Confirm alone requires `confirmationSource`, exactly `{kind:"HANDOFF_OPERATION"|"WAITING_OPERATION",replacementTaskDocumentId,evidenceId,sourceOperationId}` or `{kind:"WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION",replacementTaskDocumentId,evidenceId,submissionToken}`. It must equal the server-stored PC cycle source byte-for-byte, names the already-written current replacement `POLICY_OUTCOME` evidence and direct receipt/submission identity, and carries no outcome code or input. `undoConfirmation` and `reopen` alone may accept `restorationTriggers`: undo's exact map is `{original?,replacement?}`, reopen's is `{original?}`; each member is a §4.2 `confirmedTrigger`, required exactly when that named snapshot would otherwise restore a fired/expired date/event and forbidden when its stored trigger remains future. The whole map is omitted when no member is required. Confirm rejects `replacement`/`restorationTriggers`; supersede rejects confirmation/restoration members; undo/reopen reject replacement/confirmation; reset/finalize reject all optional H54 members. Operation fingerprint/replay includes every applicable replacement tuple, evidence reference, trigger, and proof byte.

Before constructing either restoration-trigger map, `TaskPlanService` calls the read-only preflight and consumes the descriptors in fixed order. A no-trigger or future descriptor contributes no map member; a required descriptor opens the normal policy-bound selector and contributes exactly the confirmed result to its named original/replacement member. The mutating request still independently enforces the presence rule at commit time. Preflight cancellation, malformed response, or a stale descriptor produces zero mutating call; a later typed `TRIGGER_INVALID` returns the current descriptor and repeats this same path without reading `taskPlanOperations` from the client.

An authenticated operation record is read before mutable task state, time, trigger, or semantic validation. For policy operations, same operation ID and canonical request fingerprint returns the exact stored response, including its original task instance/generation, with `replayed:true`; same ID with different bytes fails with zero mutation, and the client still performs receipt-identity adoption. For §4.7 legacy/bulk operations, the operation read is followed by mandatory current root/target identity reads before any stored result is returned; mismatch is `STALE_TASK_GENERATION`. The only fingerprint-family exceptions are reset's two-action state machine and packing clear's paged state machine; each uses its named family fingerprint while rejecting cross-family/action reuse. A fresh request then checks reset fence, task ownership, exact task identity, valid pinned policy when applicable, exact fingerprint/version, and concurrency. Operation creation, task/intent/milestone/spawn mutation, exact flow clear when required, and response storage are one transaction.

Task document, operation, session, intent, adapter-operation, attempt, and claim IDs are trimmed NFC strings of 1–256 UTF-8 bytes, contain no `/`, are neither `.` nor `..`, and do not begin and end with `__`. Every client-supplied operation/claim ID is rejected before lookup when it begins with server-only prefix `pcs1_` or `rso1_`; only the server derives those namespaces for H54 confirmation snapshots and canonical reset epochs. Prefix `rsa1_` is client-reserved solely for a reset request's exact `ResetAliasV1`; every non-reset action rejects it, and reset rejects any other operation-ID shape. Installation/auth epoch values are lowercase RFC 4122 UUIDs. Raw envelope shape, unknown keys, reserved prefix, ID syntax, blank strings, wrong numeric types, and over-limit values are validated before operation lookup. For policy/reset/packing-clear operations, exact replay then precedes mutable task, time, trigger, and semantic-state validation subject to each family's rules. For every §4.7 legacy/bulk operation and policy-absent H54/workflow operation, live root plus every stored string-or-null task identity is revalidated before a stored result is returned, exactly as §§4.5/4.7 require. Fixtures preoccupy all three reserved ID families through every ineligible client operation surface, preoccupy a candidate alias path before reset, and prove rejection leaves each namespace unaliased; cross-kind ordinary operation reuse still returns `OPERATION_REUSED`.

State codes used below are `U` Upcoming, `A` USER_ACTION_TRACKED, `W` WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL, `D` DEFERRED, `C` Completed, `N` Not Applicable, `R` H54 retired, `PA` pending amendment, `PC` pending confirmation, `CF` confirmed, `RO` reopened, `X` reset active, `L` absent legacy, and `M` malformed-present. An ordinary/original task accepts fresh semantic commands only in `U/A/W/D/RO` with H54 state absent/null/reopened; `L/M/C/N/R/PA/PC/CF/X` reject with zero mutation. The exact replacement document linked by a live PA cycle is the narrow exception: while its task status is A/W/D it may execute the entry's named amendment handoff and follow-ups, its declared waiting resolution, a bound workflow producer, DEFER, or SELF_HANDLE. It rejects whole-case assertion, NOT_APPLICABLE, unrelated handoffs/outcomes/producers, and direct WAIT. Applying the entry's named terminal confirmation outcome first freezes the §3.3 snapshot/source and atomically changes both cycle markers PA→PC while leaving the replacement Completed; PC accepts only H54 `confirmAmendment`. `undoConfirmation` is legal only from CF and restores PA, so a second undo from PC or PA always rejects. The original PA/PC task never accepts ordinary semantic/handoff/workflow commands. End-to-end and unrelated-action tests freeze this exception.

Common fresh cleanup cancels a live handoff, archives/removes any live waiting or waiting-fallback state, clears stale wake pointers, cancels pending intents, clears obsolete resolution, preserves case/profile/milestone evidence and monotonic external-submission evidence, then increments epoch/revision. A command/outcome selecting WAIT writes either its exact waiting state or the trusted-display fallback after cleanup. Failed replacement-trigger validation performs no cleanup.

Every client sheet/selector is bound to exact local `DispositionDraftKey = {accountUid,installationId,authEpochUUID,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,interactionEpoch,interactionRevision,policyVersion,policyFingerprint,commandKind,capability,transitionCode?,actionId?,sessionId?}`. `commandKind` is one named command/effect from §§4.2–4.3; `capability` is its exact policy capability. Transition/action/session appear exactly when that draft targets one and otherwise are forbidden. The coordinator also stores `observedTasksStoreRevision` as a UI-observation counter, but that counter is never sent or treated as server authority. On every TasksStore/auth update and immediately before local preparation or dispatch, it reconstructs the key from the current mapped task/session and requires byte equality. Any account/auth namespace, task instance, interaction epoch/revision, policy version/fingerprint, capability, command, transition, action, or session change invalidates/dismisses the draft and performs zero call/opener; a changed observation counter with an identical authoritative key merely re-renders. Tests mutate every member while the disposition sheet and trigger selector are open.

WAIT has one anti-bypass exception to the general admissible-state set. Direct `markWaitingOnExternal` from `W` always rejects with zero mutation, whether it names the same or another transition. A WAIT_EXTERNAL effect from `W` is legal only through `resolveWaiting` against the exact live transition: cycle 0 renewal increments to 1 with a fresh trigger; cycle 1 returns the different-channel resolver with zero mutation. `beginHandoff` from W is legal only for the exact receipt-bound WAITING_CHANNEL pending selection in §3.1; that path changes to a different channel and carries the old entry/count into history. No workflow, generic handoff begin, direct disposition, or generic outcome may archive a waiting state and recreate cycle 0. A user leaves W through another fresh non-WAIT disposition, or follows the waiting resolver; tests cover direct same/different WAIT, outcome WAIT, workflow WAIT, W(count 0/1)→generic begin denial, receipt-bound different-channel begin, later matching new-channel WAIT, 0→1, and attempted third-cycle reset.

### 4.2 Command-specific wire values

User-visible/action strings are trimmed NFC. `nextAction` is 1–500 UTF-8 bytes; `reportedFacts` and `bounded_facts` use the exact same 0–16 member bounded-facts map with ASCII keys and null/Boolean/safe-integer/NFC-string values, each string at most 512 bytes and canonical map at most 4,096 bytes. `derivation_inputs` is not free-form: it is exactly `{deadline_evidence_id}` for SAFE_THRESHOLD and literal `{}` for LABELED_ESTIMATE. The former ID follows §4.1 and must already be visible in valid task state; the latter tells the server to create/recheck pacing-anchor evidence from its policy-declared source in the command transaction. Every callable timestamp uses `CallableTimestampWireV1`. Only observation/report/confirmation members (`observedAt`, `becameInactiveAt`, `becameActiveAt`, `reported_at`, `user_confirmed_at`) have the general no-more-than-five-minutes-in-the-future bound; trigger `at`, return/snooze, move, schedule, delivery, plan, message, and display-deadline timestamps use only their field-specific bounds.

`confirmedTrigger` is exactly one of:

```text
{ kind:"date", source:"PROMISED_DATE"|"ACCEPTANCE_DATE", at,
  user_report:{operation_id,reported_at,bounded_facts}, trigger_confirmed:true }
{ kind:"date", source:"SAFE_THRESHOLD", at,
  derivation_id:"trigger.deadline_offset.v1",
  derivation_inputs:{deadline_evidence_id},user_confirmed_at,trigger_confirmed:true }
{ kind:"date", source:"USER_PICKED", at,
  threshold_id,user_confirmed_at,trigger_confirmed:true }
{ kind:"date", source:"LABELED_ESTIMATE", at,
  derivation_id:"trigger.labeled_move_anchor_offset.v1",
  derivation_inputs:{},user_confirmed_at,trigger_confirmed:true }
{ kind:"event", eligible_event_id, user_confirmed_at, trigger_confirmed:true }
```

The server creates evidence and a canonical stored trigger. It rejects caller authority fields such as source evidence ID, publisher, canonical key, bound, or protected-outcome label. All date triggers must be strictly future at fresh validation. Promised/acceptance facts bind to the request identity: `operationId` for `changeTaskPlan`/H54 and `submissionToken` for a workflow producer, and `user_report.operation_id` must equal it. SAFE_THRESHOLD must exactly equal the recomputed current safe threshold. USER_PICKED names that safe threshold and is at or before its recomputed time. A labeled estimate is recomputed from the pinned move derivation and is never safe/legal authority. Event IDs resolve publisher/key/high-water transactionally. There is no server default, generic interval, universal move date, or trigger retention across a scheduler wake.

`RestorationTriggerDescriptorV1` is the exact `{task_instance_id,branch,requires_trigger,trigger_policy_id,prior_trigger_summary}` map. `branch` is `CANCEL_PRIOR`, `UNDO_ORIGINAL`, `UNDO_REPLACEMENT`, or `REOPEN_ORIGINAL`. `trigger_policy_id` is the exact pinned policy ID when a prior date/event trigger exists and JSON null only for the no-trigger summary. `prior_trigger_summary` is exactly `{kind:"none",at_or_event:null,fired:false}`, `{kind:"date",at_or_event:"YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.SSSZ",fired}`, or `{kind:"event",at_or_event:eligible_event_id,fired}`. The date text is the exact UTC-millisecond wire projection of the stored Timestamp; the event value is the stored eligible-event ID string. ATTENTION_NOW is not summarized directly: the server resolves its same-task wake evidence and summarizes the retained original date/event authority. Using the preflight transaction's one captured `preflight_time` and current trusted high-water, `fired` is true exactly when wake evidence proves firing, a prior date is not strictly later than that time, or the event high-water has passed its bound; otherwise it is false. `requires_trigger == fired` for date/event, while the none form always has false/null. Unknown/malformed prior trigger, ATTENTION_NOW linkage, event authority, or policy fails preflight closed rather than producing a descriptor.

The read-only response is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"restoration_preflight",action,accountUid,taskDocumentId,descriptors}`. `taskDocumentId` is the request's original/target task path member. Cancel returns exactly one `CANCEL_PRIOR` descriptor; reopen returns exactly one `REOPEN_ORIGINAL`; undo returns exactly two in fixed order `UNDO_ORIGINAL`, `UNDO_REPLACEMENT`, including a no-trigger descriptor for either branch. Action is `cancelHandoff`, `undoConfirmation`, or `reopen` and must match every branch. The client requires the account, task document, task-instance descriptor(s), branch order, and current draft/session/cycle binding before use. It consumes only descriptors with `requires_trigger:true`, sourcing a newly confirmed trigger from each descriptor's `trigger_policy_id` through the same shared selector and fresh-command rules above. It never guesses from a denied confirmation snapshot, prior contract, status, date label, or a failed request. A preflight→mutation drift is resolved only by the mutating transaction and its typed error below.

The shared client trigger selector filters the pinned policy and presents candidates in this exact order: future institution-promised date; future recipient-acceptance date; current server-authored safe threshold; a user-picked future date bounded by that safe threshold; then a labeled estimate only when the policy declares its move-anchor derivation. A date/event appears only when its exact source is allowed. The first available authoritative/default candidate is preselected, never silently accepted; USER_PICKED is an explicit editor choice rather than an automatic default. The user must confirm the complete value. Cancellation, a stale draft, missing/malformed deadline evidence, or absence of a valid candidate performs zero callable requests. Every command or workflow effect that requires a trigger uses this selector at disposition/submission time. A scheduler-fired value is evidence/history only and cannot be reused.

Command-specific keys in addition to the applicable §4.1 base are:

| Command | Exact extra keys |
|---|---|
| `assertAlreadyHandled` | `confirmation:true` |
| `markNotApplicable` | `reasonCode` |
| `deferTask` | `confirmedTrigger` |
| `markSelfHandling` | `nextAction,confirmedTrigger` |
| `markWaitingOnExternal` | `transitionCode,reportedFacts,confirmedTrigger` |
| `beginHandoff` | `sessionId,installationId,authEpochUUID,adapterOperationId,actionId,confirmedTrigger,presentationSnapshot` |
| `acknowledgeHandoffOpened` | `adapterEvidence` |
| `recordHandoffReturned` | `returnEvidence` |
| `continueHandoff` | `confirmedTrigger?` |
| `cancelHandoff` | `reasonCode,confirmedTrigger?,adapterCancellationEvidence?` |
| `resolveHandoff` | `outcomeCode,inputs,confirmedTrigger?,resolverChoice?,resolverOperationId?` |
| `resolveWaiting` | `transitionCode,outcomeCode,inputs,confirmedTrigger?,resolverChoice?,resolverOperationId?` |
| `claimTaskIntent` | envelope exception is exactly `action,intentId,claimOperationId` as §4.1 |
| `initializeFlowProgress` | alternative base and `flowProgress` exactly below |
| `continueFlowProgress` | alternative base and `flowProgress` exactly below |
| `clearFlowProgress` | alternative base and `flowAttemptProof` exactly below |
| `replaceTaskNotes` | alternative base and `notes` exactly below |
| `replaceTaskQuotes` | alternative base and `quotes` exactly below |
| `clearPackingPlan` | exact `{action,operationId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch}` |

The policy flow/content alternative base is exact `{action,operationId,taskDocumentId,expectedTaskInstanceId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedInteractionEpoch,expectedInteractionRevision,policyVersion,policyFingerprint}`. Every request transaction requires the immutable task instance/generation/policy tuple and exact current interaction epoch/revision before inspecting mutable content. `initializeFlowProgress` and `continueFlowProgress` additionally require `{expectedFlowWriteRevision,flowProgress}`; `clearFlowProgress` requires `{expectedFlowWriteRevision,flowAttemptProof}`. `flowProgress` is the complete exact `{taskInstanceId,stage,flowPath,flowAnswers,flowAnswerIdentities,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration}` map under §5.5 and carries no client-supplied post revision/fingerprint. Initialization requires no live attempt, `expectedFlowWriteRevision == current`, and generation `(lastClearedFlowAttemptGeneration ?? 0)+1`; continuation requires exact live attempt/generation and current revision. Both server-write revision `expected+1`. Clear validates proof/current revision, performs §5.5's five-delete/tombstone relation, and writes revision `expected+1`.

`replaceTaskNotes` requires exactly `{expectedNotesWriteRevision,notes}` and `replaceTaskQuotes` requires exactly `{expectedQuotesWriteRevision,quotes}` in addition to the alternative base; each forbids the other content member/revision and every flow member. Every policy task has required server-validated root `notesWriteRevision` and `quotesWriteRevision`, each a safe nonnegative integer initialized to `0`; absence on an already-present policy task is malformed and never decoded as zero. The action-specific expected value must equal its selected field's current revision. Success replaces exactly that content member, increments only that revision, and leaves the other content/flow/server fields byte-identical. Two requests from the same captured revision serialize: exactly one succeeds; the loser receives deterministic no-write `CONTENT_REVISION_CONFLICT` with `{field,currentRevision}`. After reload, a later request using that current revision is the explicit later-write winner. This is the complete last-write-wins-by-monotonic-revision rule; arrival time, device clock, operation ID, and labels never choose a winner.

All five use the ordinary operation record and `request_fingerprint = "op1_" + SHA-256(canonical complete accepted request)`; notes/quotes use `TaskCanonicalV1` for the complete accepted request, while the three flow actions use the strict request domain. Exact operation replay precedes task/revision checks. The response union is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"flow_state",action:"initializeFlowProgress"|"continueFlowProgress"|"clearFlowProgress",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,flowWriteRevision,flowAttemptId?,flowAttemptGeneration?,flowClearedAttemptId?}` or `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"task_content",action:"replaceTaskNotes"|"replaceTaskQuotes",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,field:"notes"|"quotes",contentRevision,contentSHA256}`. Initialize/continue require the two live attempt members and forbid `flowClearedAttemptId`; clear requires only `flowClearedAttemptId` and forbids live attempt members. Content action/field pairs are one-to-one and `contentSHA256` hashes canonical `{field,value}`. Replay changes only `replayed`; after an ambiguous response the client retries the same callable bytes, the server reads/validates the operation, and the client adopts the typed receipt only after current live instance/revision validation. Closed deterministic errors are `REQUEST_INVALID`, `TASK_NOT_FOUND`, `RESET_ACTIVE`, `POLICY_INVALID`, `POLICY_MISMATCH`, `STALE_STATE`, `FLOW_REVISION_CONFLICT`, `CONTENT_REVISION_CONFLICT`, `COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, and `OPERATION_REUSED`; none writes an ordinary operation except an already-committed exact replay. RED covers every action's exact/surplus maps, revision winner/loser, replay before state drift, lost response at commit/application, initialization/continuation/clear relations, task reset/instance drift, cross-field independence, fractional quote parity, size caps, and direct-rules denial.

`clearPackingPlan` has no task/policy/interaction members. It requires a safe nonnegative captured `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch`, validates the exact three-key-plus-operation envelope above, and uses the fingerprint/operation/receipt/error state machine in §3.3. A missing/extra task ID, policy value, reason, or caller-supplied cursor/count/marker is `REQUEST_INVALID`.

`flowAttemptProof` is exact `{taskInstanceId,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration,flowWriteRevision,persistedTailFingerprint}`. The fingerprint is `fp2_` plus SHA-256 over the exact canonical eight-member map `{task_instance_id,stage,flowPath,flowAnswers,flowAnswerIdentities,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration,flowWriteRevision}` read after the local queue drains. The command/producer transaction derives proof requirement from its task snapshot, recomputes all members before changing state, and rejects omission, surplus proof, task-instance mismatch, or any intervening write.

Adapter evidence maps are exact:

- opened: `{kind:"EXTERNAL_OPEN_ACCEPTED"|"CONTROLLER_PRESENTED"|"SHARE_PRESENTER_PRESENTED"|"MAIL_COMPOSER_PRESENTED",adapterOperationId,observedAt}`;
- returned: `{kind:"EXTERNAL_SCENE_ROUND_TRIP",adapterOperationId,becameInactiveAt,becameActiveAt}`, `{kind:"CONTROLLER_DISMISSED"|"SHARE_COMPLETED"|"MAIL_SENT"|"MAIL_DRAFT_SAVED"|"SHARE_PRESENTATION_CANCELLED"|"MAIL_COMPOSER_CANCELLED",adapterOperationId,observedAt}`, or `{kind:"USER_CONFIRMED_RETURN",sessionId,adapterOperationId,observedAt}`; the two cancellation kinds are legal here only under matching `RETURN_FOR_OUTCOME` policy and remain cancellation-only under `RESTORE_PRIOR`;
- cancellation: `{kind:"EXTERNAL_OPEN_REJECTED"|"CONTROLLER_PRESENTATION_FAILED"|"SHARE_PRESENTATION_FAILED"|"SHARE_PRESENTATION_CANCELLED"|"MAIL_PRESENTATION_FAILED"|"MAIL_COMPOSER_CANCELLED"|"MAIL_COMPOSER_FAILED",adapterOperationId,observedAt}`, same-install auth reconciliation `{kind:"AUTH_EPOCH_ROTATED",installationId,newAuthEpochUUID}`, or explicit different-install takeover `{kind:"OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER_CONFIRMED",newInstallationId,newAuthEpochUUID,observedAt}`.

Operation IDs must match the prepared local adapter operation. Round-trip inactive time precedes active time and both follow opener acceptance. `USER_CONFIRMED_RETURN` is available for any matching persisted `opened` session when the adapter has not produced a qualifying return; the user must tap the explicit “I’m back” action after opener acceptance. It covers relaunch, system-mediated URL confirmation, multi-scene behavior, and other no-cycle returns. Ordinary activation alone remains invalid. These records prove lifecycle/presentation, not institutional success.

The pinned handoff kind/mode closes the adapter evidence cross-product:

| Pinned kind/mode | Open evidence | Adapter return evidence | Pre-ack failure | Post-ack cancellation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CALL/WEB/MAP + EXTERNAL_APP | `EXTERNAL_OPEN_ACCEPTED` | `EXTERNAL_SCENE_ROUND_TRIP` | `EXTERNAL_OPEN_REJECTED` | forbidden |
| PORTAL/WEB + IN_APP_SHEET | `CONTROLLER_PRESENTED` | `CONTROLLER_DISMISSED` | `CONTROLLER_PRESENTATION_FAILED` | forbidden |
| SHARE + SHARE_SHEET | `SHARE_PRESENTER_PRESENTED` | `SHARE_COMPLETED` | `SHARE_PRESENTATION_FAILED` | `SHARE_PRESENTATION_CANCELLED` |
| MAIL + IN_APP_SHEET | `MAIL_COMPOSER_PRESENTED` | `MAIL_SENT` or `MAIL_DRAFT_SAVED` | `MAIL_PRESENTATION_FAILED` | `MAIL_COMPOSER_CANCELLED` or `MAIL_COMPOSER_FAILED` |

`USER_CONFIRMED_RETURN` is the only return alternative common to all four rows and requires the matching stored opened session. SHARE/MAIL cancellation evidence follows the pinned cancellation policy: RESTORE_PRIOR dispatches `cancelHandoff(PRESENTER_CANCELLED)`; RETURN_FOR_OUTCOME dispatches `recordHandoffReturned` with that same typed evidence and then shows the outcome surface. MAIL technical failure always dispatches `cancelHandoff(PRESENTER_FAILED)` and never asks an outcome. Every evidence `adapterOperationId` equals the immutable ID stored by begin. A kind/mode pair absent from this table, an evidence kind from another row, controller/external evidence crossing modes, evidence before its predecessor, or a second fresh lifecycle event rejects with zero mutation. The seven §2.2 CTA bindings further restrict which rows can be mounted.

`cancelHandoff.reasonCode` and evidence form a closed pair:

| Reason | Legal state | Required/forbidden cancellation evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `USER_CANCELLED` | `prepared` or `open_requested` | `adapterCancellationEvidence` forbidden |
| `OPENER_REJECTED` | `open_requested` | exact `EXTERNAL_OPEN_REJECTED` evidence required |
| `PRESENTATION_FAILED` | `open_requested` | exact `CONTROLLER_PRESENTATION_FAILED`, `SHARE_PRESENTATION_FAILED`, or `MAIL_PRESENTATION_FAILED` evidence required and compatible with the pinned handoff kind/mode |
| `PRESENTER_CANCELLED` | `opened`, pinned SHARE or MAIL | exact compatible `SHARE_PRESENTATION_CANCELLED` or `MAIL_COMPOSER_CANCELLED` evidence required |
| `PRESENTER_FAILED` | `opened`, only pinned MAIL | exact `MAIL_COMPOSER_FAILED` evidence required |
| `AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED` | `prepared`, `open_requested`, `opened`, or `returned` | exact `AUTH_EPOCH_ROTATED` evidence required and its installation must equal the session installation |
| `USER_CONFIRMED_OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER` | `prepared`, `open_requested`, `opened`, or `returned` | exact `OTHER_DEVICE_TAKEOVER_CONFIRMED` evidence required and its installation must differ from the session installation |

Any other reason/evidence/state combination rejects with zero mutation. The opened lifecycle evidence must precede SHARE/MAIL post-ack cancellation or failure. Opener rejection or presentation failure occurs before acknowledgement and therefore cannot carry opened evidence. H54's internal cancellation is reducer-owned cleanup and never fabricates or submits this command. `confirmedTrigger` is required exactly when the restored prior snapshot's date/event trigger is fired or no longer future, and forbidden otherwise; its validation completes before any restore/cleanup mutation.

The MAIL driver checks `MFMailComposeViewController.canSendMail()` before local preparation; false renders the policy action passive with a labeled “Mail isn’t configured on this device” fallback and sends no begin or implicit `mailto:` open. `ProviderDirectoryService` may resolve only that the `provider_mail` action is available and its policy-owned subject/body copy; it has no recipient field and never synthesizes one from phone, URL, provider name, identity, or a website. `ProviderActionCard` prompts for one recipient, validates the exact ASCII shape/bounds above, displays the complete address, and requires explicit confirmation. Only then does `TaskExternalActionButton` build origin `USER_ENTERED_MAIL` plus `mail_recipient_source:"USER_ENTERED"`, persist/begin, and present. Cancellation before recipient confirmation sends no callable. Its MessageUI wrapper and injectable fake driver live entirely in `TaskExternalActionButton.swift`. Recipient/subject/body come only from the validated presentation snapshot; there is no cc, bcc, attachment, raw identity interpolation, implicit directory lookup, or test send. `.sent` and `.saved` record returned lifecycle; `.cancelled` follows the pinned cancellation policy; `.failed` restores prior state. Offline tests simulate every driver result and invalid/changed/double-confirmed recipient; no email is sent.

Resolver choice maps are exact. Handoff failure accepts `{code:"TRY_ALTERNATE",actionId}`, `{code:"DO_LATER",confirmedTrigger}`, or `{code:"OPEN_CHAT"}`. TRY_ALTERNATE is offered only when a different declared handoff exists; it resolves the old session into history, writes `pending_handoff` with zero trigger consumption, and preserves the current contract until the required new begin atomically writes the replacement USER_ACTION trigger/session. DO_LATER resolves the old session and writes DEFERRED using its handoff trigger policy. OPEN_CHAT returns navigation while leaving task and returned handoff unchanged. Waiting second-cycle resolution accepts only the receipt's exact `{code:"TRY_CHANNEL",actionId,transitionCode,nudgeTemplateId}` descriptor; all three values must equal the live `waiting_state` derivation. It writes only the pending selection/nudge digest and retains WAIT until a new begin supplies the single confirmed trigger and atomically exits WAIT. Chat is the fixed visible `It's stuck` navigation before this resolver and is never a second-cycle resolver choice. Supplying `confirmedTrigger` to either TRY choice, omitting it from DO_LATER, altering/omitting a waiting descriptor member, or sending a trigger both outside and inside a choice rejects.

`resolver.not_applicable_conflict.v1` is structurally legal only when SELF_HANDLE and its complete disposition branch are also declared. Its receipt contains the exact ordered choices `EDIT_UNDERLYING_FACT`, `SELF_HANDLE`, and `RETURN_TO_TASK` and never authorizes NOT_APPLICABLE. EDIT navigates locally to the existing Settings source-management surface with zero server mutation. RETURN dismisses locally. SELF_HANDLE opens the shared trigger selector and, only after confirmation, sends a new independent `markSelfHandling` operation with freshly read instance/epoch/revision/fingerprint, bounded action, and trigger. The resolver operation/receipt cannot be submitted as a choice or reused after source-version change. A later NOT_APPLICABLE attempt always reruns the evaluator; stale receipt and changed fact-source cases are mandatory tests.

Resolver receipts contain ordered typed choice descriptors, never bare ambiguous codes. A no-parameter choice is exact `{code}`. Handoff-failure TRY_ALTERNATE is exact `{code:"TRY_ALTERNATE",actions:[{actionId,actionLabel}]}`; actions are all eligible handoffs in pinned declaration order, unique by action ID, and label copies expected action. Handoff-failure choices order TRY_ALTERNATE, DO_LATER, OPEN_CHAT; TRY_ALTERNATE is omitted when empty. A waiting second-cycle receipt contains exactly one choice `{code:"TRY_CHANNEL",actionId,actionLabel,transitionCode,nudgeTemplateId}` derived from `second_cycle_channel`, the one compatible handoff, and registry; it is never empty/multiple and contains no OPEN_CHAT. A first REQUIRES_RESOLVER request forbids `resolverChoice` and `resolverOperationId`; its zero-mutation receipt is stored at the direct operation path. A later choice requires both, transactionally reads that exact operation, and requires receipt account/task instance/session-or-transition/epoch/policy/resolver/outcome plus the submitted descriptor/action to match and remain eligible in current unchanged state. It never queries for a receipt. No default/first/label matching exists. Handoff zero/one/multiple actions and waiting exact-one action; missing/changed/reused/stale receipt; dangling ID; altered template/transition; same original ID; and same-kind waiting choices are mandatory tests.

Outcome `inputs` reject unknown keys and are exact by effect: `{}` for COMPLETE and DEFER; `{reportedFacts}` for WAIT_EXTERNAL; `{nextAction}` only when `next_action.bounded_user_text.v1` is named and `{}` for `next_action.handoff_expected_action.v1`; and `{}` on the first REQUIRES_RESOLVER request, with the later choice carried only in `resolverChoice`. An outcome that does not require `confirmedTrigger` rejects it; an effect that requires one rejects omission.

Successful policy-bearing callable receipts and reset/route receipts reject unknown keys and use one discriminator:

```text
task state = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"task_state",operationId,replayed,
              accountUid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,
              interactionEpoch,interactionRevision,
              policyFingerprint,status,disposition?,activeHandoffState?,
              pendingHandoff?:{actionId,selectedAt},flowClearedAttemptId?,
              spawnedTaskDocumentIds?}
resolver   = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"resolver",operationId,replayed,
              accountUid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,
              interactionEpoch,interactionRevision,
              policyFingerprint,resolverId,source,choices,
              evaluatorDigest?}
navigation = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"navigation",operationId,replayed,
              accountUid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,
              interactionEpoch,policyFingerprint,destination:"chat"}
preflight  = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"restoration_preflight",action,
              accountUid,taskDocumentId,
              descriptors:[RestorationTriggerDescriptorV1]}
task route = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"task_route",claimOperationId,replayed,
              accountUid,intentId,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,
              interactionEpoch,policyFingerprint,
              route:{kind:"row"|"outcome",sessionId?},expiresAt}
h54       = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"plan_change",operationId,replayed,action,
              accountUid,taskId,lifecycleState,planChangeRevision,
              original:{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,
                        interactionEpoch,interactionRevision,status},
              replacement?:{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,interactionEpoch,
                             interactionRevision,status},
              flowClears?:{original?:{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
                                      flowClearedAttemptId},
                           replacement?:{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
                                         flowClearedAttemptId}},
              restoredHandoff?:{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,sessionId,
                                 adapterOperationId,interactionEpoch,
                                 policyFingerprint},
              spawnedTaskDocumentIds?}
reset      = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"reset_progress"|"reset_final",
              operationId,replayed,accountUid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,
              taskGenerationEpoch,
              activeMoveEventId,deletedCount,
              deletedCounts:{tasks,notificationIntents,
                             taskDeadlineEvidence,confirmationSnapshots},state}
workflow   = {schemaVersion:1,kind:"workflow_outcome",submissionToken,replayed,
              accountUid,workflowSubmissionId,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
              taskGenerationEpoch,
              interactionEpoch,
              interactionRevision,policyFingerprint,status,
              disposition?,flowClearedAttemptId?,spawnedTaskDocumentIds?}
```

`choices` is the exact ordered array of typed descriptors above. `evaluatorDigest` is required exactly for `resolver.not_applicable_conflict.v1` and forbidden otherwise. `lifecycleState` is `retired`, `pending_amendment`, `pending_confirmation`, `confirmed`, or `reopened`; `replacement` is present exactly when that cycle has one. `pendingHandoff` appears exactly after a TRY selection; `actionId` equals `pending_handoff.action_id`, and receipt `selectedAt` is the exact millisecond wire projection of stored `pending_handoff.selected_at`. A task/workflow `flowClearedAttemptId` appears only when that transaction cleared one tail. H54 `flowClears` is present iff at least one named task tail was cleared and contains exactly those members; `restoredHandoff` is present only for undo that recreated it; H54 `spawnedTaskDocumentIds` is present only for confirmation/reconfirmation with a nonempty frozen closure. Other task/workflow `spawnedTaskDocumentIds` follows the same nonempty rule. The restoration-preflight receipt has no operation/replay member because it is never stored; its descriptor count/order and action pairing are exactly the rules above. Reset `state` is `deleting`, `awaiting_local_reset`, or `finalized` consistent with kind; its expected/result generations are `e/r`, and `operationId` is the deterministic `rso1_` canonical record ID rather than the caller alias. A reset client recomputes that ID from authenticated UID and receipt result epoch before adoption. A task route with `row` forbids `sessionId`; `outcome` requires it exactly for a returned handoff and otherwise forbids it. Its wire `expiresAt` is required `CallableTimestampWireV1`, and exact replay preserves that string byte-for-byte; it is not a Firestore Timestamp/map or local integer until the inbox projection. Every policy client compares account, task instance, and generation before applying even a replay. Reset application additionally requires the current authenticated UID equal `accountUid`; an auth switch during any reset response/replay retains that receipt for its namespace and performs no local clear, finalize, or state advance in the new account. Tests cover switch before progress/final responses, switch-back replay, and the same caller alias under two UIDs. Exact replay returns all stored semantic fields, including selection time and route expiry, and changes only `replayed:false` to `true`.

The exact `DispositionReceiptV1` value union is `COMPLETED|NOT_APPLICABLE|USER_ACTION_TRACKED|WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL|DEFERRED|SUPPORT_ACTIVE`, the six §A disposition strings and no aliases. A `task_state` or `workflow_outcome` receipt contains `disposition` iff that receipt's committed transaction wrote or restored a `dispositionContract.disposition`; when present it is string-equal to the persisted post-write value read in that same transaction. It is never inferred from `status`, outcome/effect, trusted-display availability, response kind, or a client label. A pure handoff lifecycle/state transition such as ACK, RETURN, or CONTINUE omits it. `beginHandoff` writes USER_ACTION and therefore returns `USER_ACTION_TRACKED`; a cancellation/restoration includes the restored value only when it actually writes a disposition contract; direct and outcome/workflow WAIT return `WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL` only for trusted WAIT and return `USER_ACTION_TRACKED` for the Amendment-2 waiting-display fallback. COMPLETE, NOT_APPLICABLE, and DEFER writers return their exact values. `SUPPORT_ACTIVE` has no Phase-2 writer but remains a valid closed decoder member for exact existing persisted authority. A missing member when required, surplus member on a pure transition, unknown string, or receipt/task inequality is a protocol violation and is never applied. The policy workflow consumer keys its COMPLETE versus trusted-WAIT versus fallback-USER_ACTION behavior on this member alone; it does not branch on `status` or the requested effect. Fixtures cover all six decoder values, every presence/absence branch, direct/handoff/outcome/workflow writers, pure ACK/RETURN/CONTINUE, restoration with and without a contract, and WAIT-ineligible workflow fallback returning `USER_ACTION_TRACKED`.

The union above does not rewrite compatibility paths. A policy-absent H54 request retains the Phase-1 exact success response `{taskId,status,replacementTaskId,lifecycleState,revision,replayed}`; `replacementTaskId` is string or null, `revision` is a safe nonnegative integer, and replay changes only the Boolean. Its deterministic validation/precondition/not-found errors retain the existing Firebase code/message envelope. A policy-absent no-token workflow retains exactly one existing response variant: guidance `{success:true,status:"completed"}`, mini-assessment `{success:true,tasksCreated}`, or vendor `{success:true,status:"matching_in_progress"}`. Those legacy branches never emit/decode the discriminator union, policy task identity, or v2 error details; their current payload/callback bytes are frozen by existing tests. A present/malformed policy may never fall into either compatibility branch.

Callable errors use Firebase code `invalid-argument`, `unauthenticated`, `permission-denied`, `not-found`, `failed-precondition`, `aborted`, or `resource-exhausted`, and exact details `{schemaVersion:1,reason,operationId?,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch?,submissionToken?,field?,currentRevision?,activeOperationId?,estimatedBytes?,limitBytes?,documentCount?,restorationDescriptor?}`; optional detail members are present only for the exact reason that names them. These codes guarantee the requested fresh task/effect transaction did not commit. `reason` is exactly one of `REQUEST_INVALID`, `AUTH_REQUIRED`, `AUTH_FORBIDDEN`, `TASK_NOT_FOUND`, `INTENT_NOT_FOUND`, `INTENT_EXPIRED`, `INTENT_CANCELLED`, `INTENT_STALE`, `INTENT_ALREADY_CLAIMED`, `RESET_ACTIVE`, `POLICY_INVALID`, `POLICY_MISMATCH`, `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`, `STALE_STATE`, `STALE_TASK_GENERATION`, `TRIGGER_INVALID`, `EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN`, `COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, `HANDOFF_MISMATCH`, `AUTH_EPOCH_MISMATCH`, `INTENT_INVALID`, `OPERATION_REUSED`, `WORKFLOW_BINDING_INVALID`, `FLOW_REVISION_CONFLICT`, `CONTENT_REVISION_CONFLICT`, `PACKING_CLEAR_ACTIVE`, or `TERMINAL_WRITE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`. `RESET_ACTIVE` alone requires canonical `operationId` plus the active reset's safe `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch`; `CONTENT_REVISION_CONFLICT` alone requires `field/currentRevision`; `PACKING_CLEAR_ACTIVE` alone requires `activeOperationId`. A mutating cancel/undo/reopen `TRIGGER_INVALID` requires `restorationDescriptor` equal to the exact `RestorationTriggerDescriptorV1` recomputed from that transaction's current reads; the validation order is CANCEL_PRIOR, or UNDO_ORIGINAL then UNDO_REPLACEMENT, or REOPEN_ORIGINAL, so at most the first invalid branch is returned. If state did not drift, it is byte-equal to the corresponding preflight descriptor. Every other `TRIGGER_INVALID` forbids that detail. `PACKING_CLEAR_INVALID` and `PACKING_CLEAR_FENCE_INVALID` are not callable-error reasons: they are committed typed terminal results under §3.3, including on exact replay. `TERMINAL_WRITE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` alone uses `failed-precondition`, requires exactly one of operation ID/submission token plus safe integers `estimatedBytes`, literal `limitBytes:8388608`, and `documentCount`; it forbids every other optional detail. `unavailable`, `deadline-exceeded`, `internal`, `unknown`, connection loss, or response decode failure after dispatch are ambiguous: the client retains the immutable request and retries the same operation ID/bytes before opening or editing. A deterministic stale/auth/policy/not-found result discards prepared presentation after refresh; an editable input/trigger/evidence/reset result preserves only the draft and creates a new operation after correction. On a restoration-trigger error the client discards the denied trigger/request, uses only the returned descriptor's policy through the shared selector, preflights again when another undo branch remains, and never reconstructs a trigger from a denied snapshot. No error path opens an external target. Second-installation and rotated-epoch forged ACK/RETURN/RESOLVE/ordinary-cancel cases must return `AUTH_EPOCH_MISMATCH` with zero mutation.

Resolver `source` is a required disjoint union: `{kind:"HANDOFF",sessionId,outcomePolicyId,outcomeId,returnEvidenceId}` for `resolver.handoff_failure.v1`; `{kind:"WAITING",transitionCode,entryEvidenceId,sameChannelCycleCount:1,outcomePolicyId,outcomeId}` for `resolver.waiting_change_channel.v1`; or `{kind:"CONTRADICTION",reasonCode,sourceRef,sourceVersion,selectionDigest}` for `resolver.not_applicable_conflict.v1`. The earlier optional `sessionId` shorthand is superseded by this exact member. `evaluatorDigest` is required exactly for CONTRADICTION, equals §2.2's `ced1_` value, and is forbidden otherwise. The later choice transaction matches the complete source map against the retained session/transition/evidence/evaluation and stored operation response; cross-outcome, cross-entry, cross-cycle, or cross-evaluation reuse fails. Fixtures mutate every source member independently.

### 4.3 Complete command × prior state × result table

| Command | Admissible prior | Result and cleanup |
|---|---|---|
| `assertAlreadyHandled` | `U/A/W/D/RO` | Whole-case confirmation closes only required milestones currently OPEN; preserves every existing COMPLETED and NOT_REQUIRED evidence/reason/source byte-for-byte; companions unchanged; `Completed + COMPLETED`; common cleanup; terminal evidence archived. The explicit-confirmation and direct wrapper `closed_milestone_ids` contain only the newly closed OPEN IDs in profile order. |
| `markNotApplicable` | same and evaluator=`none` | `Dismissed + NOT_APPLICABLE`; resolution/evidence recorded. `present` returns resolver with zero task mutation; `unknown` fails closed. |
| `deferTask` | same | Milestones preserved; `Snoozed + DEFERRED`; owner authenticated user; action `Return to this task`; destination `row`; disposition-policy trigger. |
| `markSelfHandling` | same | Milestones preserved; `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED`; authenticated owner; policy-valid concrete action and fresh trigger. |
| `markWaitingOnExternal` | `U/A/D/RO` only | Milestones preserved; with trusted display, `matching_in_progress + WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL` and owner/expectation derived from transition plus bounded facts; without it, exact `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED` waiting fallback. Both use the same fresh transition trigger. Prior `W` rejects and must use `resolveWaiting`. |
| `beginHandoff` | `U/A/D/RO`, or W only with the exact count-1 WAITING_CHANNEL pending selection; no live nonterminal handoff | Common cleanup; store bounded prior snapshot; write USER_ACTION fallback and `open_requested`; a W selection archives its exact prior transition/entry/count and must change channel; external opener is still forbidden. |
| `acknowledgeHandoffOpened` | matching `open_requested` | `opened`; revision only. Exact replay succeeds; any other state rejects. |
| `recordHandoffReturned` | matching `opened` | `returned`; revision only; accepts adapter cycle/dismissal or explicit user-confirmed return, never ordinary activation alone. |
| `continueHandoff` | matching `prepared` | `open_requested`; same epoch, revision+1. Fired/expired fallback requires a fresh trigger before mutation. |
| `cancelHandoff` | state/reason/evidence pair from the closed §4.2 table | Restore exact prior dynamic snapshot, retain cancelled history, epoch/revision+1; never restore a fired trigger; auth/takeover exceptions record no action outcome. |
| `resolveHandoff` | matching `returned` | Apply policy effect. A WAIT effect uses trusted WAIT or the exact display fallback. Missing `resolverChoice` for `REQUIRES_RESOLVER` returns receipt-backed descriptor, leaves handoff `returned`, and mutates no task; a new operation with a registry-valid choice resolves it. |
| `resolveWaiting` | current `W`, matching `waiting_state` | “It happened” applies the transition-bound outcome; any new WAIT transition uses trusted WAIT or the exact display fallback. The first same-transition stuck cycle copies the already-trusted name/source, writes count `1` and new entry time with a fresh trigger; at count `1`, another same-channel failure returns the resolver, whose sole later fresh choice selects the policy-declared different handoff/transition/template. Chat is not a resolver choice. It never reuses a resolved session. |
| `claimTaskIntent` | pending, unexpired, intended UID, live epoch/session/cause | Intent `pending→consumed`, typed route returned; task unchanged; same claim op replays, another claimant fails. |
| `initializeFlowProgress` | exact §5.5 eligible policy task, no live attempt/handoff, matching instance/generation/interaction/flow revision | Write complete attempt at next generation and flow revision+1; no status/evidence/content mutation. |
| `continueFlowProgress` | same lifecycle with matching live attempt/generation/revision | Replace complete flow snapshot and increment only flow revision; stale writer conflicts. |
| `clearFlowProgress` | same lifecycle with matching full proof | Delete five live fields, retain stage, write exact tombstone ID/generation, increment flow revision. |
| `replaceTaskNotes` / `replaceTaskQuotes` | exact §5.5 eligible policy task with required content revisions present | Replace one bounded field, increment only its named revision, return content hash; concurrent loser gets typed conflict. |
| `clearPackingPlan` | authenticated root generation current, reset inactive; fresh no marker or exact active/terminal operation | Advance exactly one §3.1/§3.3 transition; terminal success proves all scanned tasks deleted and both auxiliary steps done; validation/fence terminal never resumes deletion. |
| `mutateLegacyTask` (all 16 kinds) | exact §4.7 policy-absent PLAIN or coherent H54_REOPENED task expectation and each kind's target/status predicate | Apply only the closed field relation for that kind, write one operation/receipt, and preserve every unmentioned byte including retained reopened H54 audit; stale generation never replays/adopts. A migration already-current/completed no-op writes only its operation/receipt. |
| `persistLegacyPackingPlan` | exact root/marker/auxiliary/set/delete expectations; write equation/caps satisfied | Atomically set/delete the declared full maps, retain compact bulk operation/digests, and return ordered paths/counts; any expectation mismatch writes nothing. |
| `beginLegacyExpertReview` | exact generic-legacy task identity and absent message/marker | Atomically create user support message plus false task marker; no admin-queued claim. |
| `markLegacyExpertReviewQueued` | same live task identity, matching false marker and persisted message | Change only marker `adminQueued:false→true`; exact replay is current-identity-bound. |

### 4.4 H54 matrix

`planChangeRevision` remains the H54 cycle ID and changes only on a new supersede cycle; `interaction_revision` changes on every semantic mutation.

| H54 action | Legal prior | Exact result |
|---|---|---|
