| internal `supersede` | H54 absent/null/reopened; no external-submission evidence; no replacement | Snapshot bounded dynamic state; write original `Dismissed + RETIRED + PLAN_SUPERSEDED_INTERNAL` with no `superseded_by`; cancel handoff/intents; clear wake; epoch/revision+1. |
| external `supersede` | any `U/A/W/D/RO` with retained qualifying external evidence and one exact policy replacement entry | Archive the exact current original state, then move original into the entry's original-policy verification WAIT transition while the accepted artifact remains in force. Create replacement in `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED` for the entry's named handoff/action using the amendment-action trigger, selected amendment profile, epoch/revision `1/1`, current generation epoch, root flow revision 0, and deterministic instance below. Cancel/clear current handoff/wake/intents; original epoch/revision+1. |
| named replacement confirmation outcome | replacement in PA and an exact reachable handoff/wait/workflow COMPLETE producer | Before mutation freeze the exact §3.3 original/replacement non-flow snapshot pair and typed source; then apply the replacement-policy outcome/evidence, atomically clear its flow tail if present, require the amendment profile completion policy closed, and move both cycle markers PA→PC. Original stays in verification WAIT; replacement is Completed. |
| `confirmAmendment` | `PC`, exact expected replacement tuple and `confirmationSource` | Consume—never reapply—the named current-instance replacement `POLICY_OUTCOME` evidence/receipt, verify and copy its bounded tuple by value into a new original-instance amendment-confirmation record, write original `Dismissed + SUPERSEDED` with `superseded_by` equal to the replacement instance and `AMENDMENT_CONFIRMED` resolution, and move both cycle markers to CF. |
| `undoConfirmation` | `CF`, first use, within five minutes, unchanged bounded snapshots | Validate exact per-snapshot restoration triggers/proofs before mutation; restore both frozen pre-outcome non-flow snapshots, set both markers to PA, and increment both epochs/revisions; an opened handoff becomes a deterministic flowless new-session/new-epoch `prepared` record as below; old intent/lifecycle/flow evidence never returns. |
| `reopen` | `R` or untouched `PA` | Validate the exact original restoration trigger when required, then restore the original snapshot. An untouched proposed replacement is internally retired as `Dismissed + RETIRED + AMENDMENT_CANCELLED_UNTOUCHED`, keeps its retired link as audit history, forbids `superseded_by`, and is never physically deleted; epoch/revision+1. |
| `resetAllTasks` | no reset active at captured expected epoch, or matching per-result-epoch record/tombstone | Derive the canonical `rso1_` record, adopt/append the caller alias, and either create/resume the lease/pages that delete task docs, notification intents, deadline-authority seam docs, and H54 confirmation-snapshot records, or return the verified permanent final receipt; checkpoint exact unique total and per-collection counts. |
| `finalizeTaskReset` | matching canonical `awaiting_local_reset` record, or its tombstone | Atomically delete the marker and replace the active record with the digest-bound terminal tombstone before the first final receipt; exact alias/tombstone replay returns the same count without another reset. |

`resetAllTasks` and `finalizeTaskReset` are two messages to one per-epoch state machine. Both carry literal `reason:"retake_assessment"`, the same retained caller `operationId` alias, and the same captured `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch:e`. Their shared fingerprint is exactly `"reset1_" + SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({kind:"reset",reason:"retake_assessment",expected_task_generation_epoch:e}))`, intentionally omitting action and alias. Dispatch validates the complete request and computes safe result epoch `r`, canonical ID/path, and fingerprint before consulting mutable root state, then reads that deterministic record before any marker/task/time validation. A matching active record appends the alias subject to the bound and resumes/returns its exact progress. A matching tombstone appends the alias subject to the bound, verifies the final digest/projection, and returns the reconstructed final receipt with `replayed:true` for either action. Neither branch can create epoch `r+1`.

If the canonical path is absent, `finalizeTaskReset` returns no-write `STALE_STATE`. If absent for `resetAllTasks`, one transaction may create it only when root epoch equals `e`, result epoch is `r`, and no marker exists; that transaction stores the first alias, creates the canonical marker, rotates `taskGenerationEpoch e→r` and `activeMoveEventId`, and returns progress. A live marker for a different computed record returns `RESET_ACTIVE` with its canonical ID and expected epoch; a conflicting/malformed record at the deterministic path fails closed. A later intentional reset captures then-current root epoch `r` and therefore addresses the different `r+1` record. A losing scene retaining `e` always addresses/adopts the old active record or tombstone, regardless of the root now being `r`; it never starts the later reset. A different reason, overflow epoch, reserved/malformed alias, non-reset use of the canonical ID, mismatched marker/record, finalize-before-ready, or deterministic-path collision fails with zero destructive mutation. Tests cover page loss/replay, competing scenes before/after rotation, alias order/deduplication/cap/17th resolution, intentional next epoch, finalize loss, crash after the first final commit/receipt, digest/collision/surplus mismatch, and action-independent fingerprint equality.

`TaskPlanService.swift` owns one process-wide `ResetOperationRegistry` actor. `ResetOperationRegistryRecordV2` is exact `{uid,suggestedOperationId,canonicalOperationId?,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,phase,progressReceipt?,finalReceipt?,applicationId?,createdAt,updatedAt}` inside §3.3's envelope. Records are unique by UID, ordered by creation/UID, capped at eight active account records, and local dates are UTC RFC 3339 milliseconds. Phase is exactly lowercase `prepared|reset_dispatched|reset_receipt|finalize_dispatched|final_receipt|applying`. PREPARED forbids canonical ID/receipts/application. The first RESET_DISPATCHED may lack canonical ID/progress; after a validated progress response, canonical ID and latest progress remain durable and RESET_RECEIPT requires both. FINALIZE_DISPATCHED requires the canonical ID and an awaiting-local-reset progress receipt. FINAL_RECEIPT requires the validated terminal receipt and forbids application ID. APPLYING adds `applicationId = "ra1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,task_generation_epoch:finalReceipt.taskGenerationEpoch,operation_id:canonicalOperationId})))`. Other phase/member combinations are corrupt.

Before first reset dispatch, `beginOrResume(uid,suggestedOperationId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch)` serially returns the existing UID record or durably stores a generated-once `ResetAliasV1` suggestion and captured epoch as PREPARED. All instances retain that alias/epoch through both actions. The actor writes a dispatched phase before each await and validates every response account, `e/r`, recomputed canonical ID, counts, state, and alias adoption before advancing. It writes FINAL_RECEIPT before acknowledging the first server final response, then APPLYING before any nontransactional local completion/reload callback; application is idempotent by `applicationId`, and the record is removed only after all state-derived local effects finish. A crash before final-receipt durability leaves FINALIZE_DISPATCHED and retries the same alias/epoch against the tombstone. A crash after FINAL_RECEIPT makes no new server reset call. A crash in APPLYING repeats only idempotent local reconciliation and never a generation rotation.

If the registry is absent while an authenticated server marker is live, a new expected-epoch request receives `RESET_ACTIVE`. The actor validates the returned canonical formula and safe prior expected epoch, retains its own exact `ResetAliasV1` suggestion as the new alias, atomically adopts that expected epoch/canonical ID as RESET_DISPATCHED, and retries; it never sends `rso1_` as a caller operation ID. No other error permits adoption. Lowercase phase decoding and uppercase `RESET_DISPATCHED` rejection are mandatory alongside competing scenes/process abstractions, crashes before/after each six-phase boundary, server-marker recovery, stale legacy coordinator suggestion, alias/canonical distinction, account switch, eight-record capacity, corrupt aggregate quarantine/no-dispatch, final replay, and idempotent APPLYING in `TaskPlanDispositionTests`. No edit to `RetakeAssessmentCoordinator.swift` or protected assessment/knowledge/dose cleanup paths is required.

Reset uses four ordered targets, each queried by document ID with literal `limit(1)`: `tasks`; `notificationIntents`; `taskDeadlineEvidence`; and only `taskPlanOperations` records with exact `kind == "CONFIRMATION_SNAPSHOT"` (ordinary operations, active reset records, permanent reset tombstones, and malformed/lowercase wrong-kind records remain for op-first review rather than being misclassified). One leased transaction rereads and deletes at most that single result, then advances its document-path cursor/count; using one document is the conservative service-size proof for a legacy near-1-MiB task and the exact removable index-entry multiset under §7.4, avoiding the prior 400-document/10-MiB contradiction. Missing-after-query is counted zero and still advances past that path; a target becomes done only after a transactional empty requery from the beginning, which also catches concurrent inserts. The marker and active reset operation store exact `target_index`, `page_after_path`, `deleted_counts:{tasks,notification_intents,task_deadline_evidence,confirmation_snapshots}`, and `deleted_count` equal to their checked sum. Retry cannot double-count. `awaiting_local_reset` is legal only after all four empty checks, and finalize rechecks the marker/count/epoch/canonical-ID tuple while retaining generation epoch and active move-event ID in the tombstone. Tests use schema-maximum and emulator-maximum-size documents, actual indexed/exempted maps/arrays, deletion/insertion races, response loss/replay, old deadline removal, uppercase confirmation-snapshot removal while lowercase/wrong-kind/ordinary/reset/tombstone records remain outside the target, regenerated same document/new instance non-alias, exact per-target/unique totals, finalization refusal with one record in each target, and rules denial for active/tombstone records.

Each `plan_change_policy.replacements` entry is resolved transactionally by catalog task ID, exact replacement policy fingerprint, amendment profile, named amendment handoff, its action trigger policy, the original-policy verification transition, the replacement confirmation outcome, and amendment-anchor kind. The replacement policy's ordinary profile resolver runs against the newly derived immutable replacement task/case facts and must select exactly `amendment_profile_id`; the caller cannot set/bypass it, and zero/multiple/different-profile resolution aborts the whole supersede. A caller replacement descriptor that differs is rejected. External supersede requires newly confirmed `amendmentAction.confirmedTrigger` under the named replacement handoff/action trigger and a separate `verification.confirmedTrigger` under only the original verification transition; each creates its own evidence and contract. The replacement USER_ACTION owner is authenticated user, its action is the pinned handoff `expected_action`, and its destination is row. With trusted display, original verification owner/expectation/destination derive only from its transition and produce WAIT; without it, the original remains in the same PA cycle but carries the exact waiting-fallback USER_ACTION contract and row route. There is no replacement verification transition or shared-trigger coupling. Tests cover exact/different/zero/multiple amendment-profile resolution, both display branches, and immutable-fact drift.

The original verification entry is a closed DIRECT transition written by `taskPlan.js` with `producer.task_plan.v1`. With trusted display, its WAIT_ENTRY `source_ref` is the verification CONFIRMED_TRIGGER evidence ID and facts contain selected transition/channel, `entry_kind:"DIRECT"`, that trigger ID, `reported_facts_digest = SHA-256(canonical {})`, exact display name/source, and no `source_evidence_id`; state starts at cycle `0`. Without trusted display, it instead creates the exact DIRECT WAIT_DISPLAY_FALLBACK evidence/state and USER_ACTION projection with the same trigger and no WAIT_ENTRY. Either branch preserves already-established external-submission evidence. Structural plan-change validation rejects a verification transition with `external_submission_effect:"FORBID"`; PRESERVE or ESTABLISH_FROM_OUTCOME_EVIDENCE may admit direct verification, but it never newly establishes the fact. Supersede replay reproduces the originally committed branch, IDs, and records. Fixtures cover both branches, full dependency chains/rollup roots, both admissible transition effects, FORBID rejection, changed trigger bytes, directory appearance after replay, and op-first replay.

When the exact named replacement outcome is applied by `resolveHandoff`, `resolveWaiting`, or `submitWorkflowAnswers`, that transaction reads the linked original/cycle, requires both expected current instances/policies/cycle revision, freezes the §3.3 snapshot record and typed pending source, writes the replacement policy-outcome evidence, completes the amendment profile, and moves both markers PA→PC. Any other outcome leaves the cycle PA. `confirmAmendment` then requires the exact expected replacement tuple and byte-equal stored `confirmationSource`; in one transaction it reads both tasks plus the referenced direct operation/workflow submission and snapshot record, requires the complete replacement evidence record's source identity, policy/outcome, current instance, completion state, digest, and observed time to match, and creates the new original-instance `evidence.amendment_confirmation.v1` with only the copied tuple before writing original SUPERSEDED terminal state. Missing/wrong/stale/other-outcome/source evidence, a snapshot mismatch, a reset/recreated or concurrently changed replacement, or an already-mutated cycle rejects atomically with no cross-task evidence resolver. Undo/reopen accepts restoration triggers only under exact source snapshot trigger policies. No label or free replacement ID selects policy. Tests cover U/W/SELF_HANDLE/DEFER→external supersede, differing original/replacement dates/policies, amendment handoff→confirmation outcome→confirm, workflow confirmation, PC recovery after every local-loss boundary, prior-state archival, unrelated/partial outcome, wrong source evidence, copied digest/time mismatch, replacement mutation/reset race, transition mismatch, and monotonic external evidence.

For a valid-policy external supersede, the server first normalizes the exact replacement subject/institution fields under §4.1, then derives `replacementTaskDocumentId = "a2_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,originalTaskId:original_task_document_id,revision:plan_change_revision,subject:normalized_replacement_subject,institutionId:normalized_replacement_institution_id,taskId:replacement_catalog_task_id}))`. This is the existing `canonicalAmendmentId` identity and is never caller-supplied. It then derives `replacementInstancePreimage = canonical {uid,original_task_document_id,original_task_instance_id,plan_change_revision,replacement_task_document_id,replacement_catalog_task_id}` and `task_instance_id = "ti1_" + first40(SHA-256(replacementInstancePreimage))`.

The transaction reads persistent root generation and active move-event authority. It creates the replacement at that absent path with exact current `task_generation_epoch`, `flowWriteRevision:0`, `notesWriteRevision:0`, `quotesWriteRevision:0`, no tail/fences, exact copied catalog policy, normalized `subject:{kind,id}` and `institutionId` from the validated replacement descriptor, current-code display `institution`, `supersedes:originalTaskDocumentId`, and base `spawnedFrom:{kind:"conversation",id:originalTaskDocumentId}`. Those four identity/provenance members are unconditional on every a2 row; only the selected §2.2 move-event alias is conditional, and `spawnedFrom.move_event_id` augments that base map only for that selected path. The replacement validator requires descriptor subject kind equal policy kind, exact normalized ID/display bounds, and all stored members byte-equal; omission, surplus provenance, or mismatch rejects. The complete initial replacement includes its PA link/state/dynamic projection, and its canonical hash becomes the cycle's `creation_document_sha256`. A fresh policy supersede requires the derived document absent; any existing document without the matching supersede operation receipt is `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED` and is never adopted as a partial/reusable create. Operation-first replay alone returns the already committed same document/instance. Policy-absent legacy H54 uses §4.7's captured generation/string-or-null-instance expectation while preserving its canonical ID/result behavior. Reset/new original instance changes the replacement instance even if the legacy document formula recurs; a stale preexisting path then fails rather than aliasing. Tests cover exact ID/hash fixtures, unconditional subject/institution/supersedes/spawnedFrom fields with every selector combination, omitted/surplus/mismatch rejection, every move-event path, path/instance collision, response-loss replay, generation mismatch, reset/recreate, and stale old-instance receipt/route/proof.

When undo restores a previously opened handoff, the server clears old lifecycle evidence and computes `session_id = "hs1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,task_instance_id,undo_operation_id,old_session_id,new_interaction_epoch}))` and `adapter_operation_id = "ao1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,task_instance_id,session_id,undo_operation_id}))`. It writes `state:prepared`, the preserved redacted presentation/non-flow prior snapshot, `restoration:{kind:"H54_UNDO",operation_id:undoOperationId}`, and no `flow_attempt_proof`. A previously cleared tail is intentionally not rollback data; the old lease is released, Cancel returns to the task row, and choosing the flow again creates the next generation. The H54 receipt adds exact `restoredHandoff?:{taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,sessionId,adapterOperationId,interactionEpoch,policyFingerprint}`. `HandoffSessionStore` reconstructs its SERVER_SNAPSHOT PREPARED variant from those bytes and must durably dispatch `continueHandoff`; any old session/adapter/flow evidence is stale. Undo replay returns the same IDs. Tests cover confirm-clears-flow→undo→Continue/Cancel/reconfirm, relaunch, ambiguous Continue, old evidence, and next-generation flow restart.

### 4.5 Workflow producers

`submitWorkflowAnswers` is an authoritative producer event, not a `changeTaskPlan` command. A policy-bearing request accepts exactly `{workflowId,answers,submissionToken,taskDocumentId,expectedTaskInstanceId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedInteractionEpoch,expectedInteractionRevision,policyVersion,policyFingerprint,flowAttemptProof,outcomeInputs,confirmedTrigger?}`. Caller `userId`, owner/action/expectation/status, legacy `waiting_*`, and every other key are forbidden. `answers` is exact `{workflowId,answers}`: the inner workflow ID equals the outer value; the answer map has at most 256 question IDs following §4.1 ID syntax, each value is an ordered array of at most 64 NFC payload strings of 1–32,768 UTF-8 bytes, and the complete canonical map is at most 131,072 bytes. Existing eligible workflow logic remains responsible for its typed values, but raw answer strings never enter task evidence, history, policy selection, or copy. Tests include exact 32,768-byte acceptance, 32,769-byte rejection, malformed typed JSON where an eligible workflow declares JSON, and total-map overflow.

`submissionToken = WorkflowSubmissionToken.make(auth.uid,taskDocumentId,workflowId,taskInstanceId,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration)`: for those six UTF-8 components in that order, encode `decimalByteCount + "|" + component`, join the six encoded components with `|`, hash with SHA-256, and prefix `workflow-v2-`. The server recomputes it from auth plus the exact proof and rejects mismatch. `workflowSubmissionId = "ws2_" + first40(sha256(canonical {owner:auth.uid,submissionToken}))`. Task generation is separately fingerprinted/validated; task instance plus attempt identity survive server clear in the submission record and last-cleared ID/generation. Exact request bytes survive process death in the local store below. No-token behavior remains byte-compatible legacy only and may not mutate a policy-bearing task.

`task_selector.explicit_task_document.v1` reads user root plus that exact owner task, requires root/task generation and expected task instance, recomputes the §4.2 proof from the transaction snapshot, and requires its stored `workflowId`, valid pinned policy, and unique producer/workflow/outcome binding to match. The proof instance/attempt/generation are the token components. Mini-assessment and `rowGeneration` workflows are policy-ineligible. A guidance binding is valid only for COMPLETE_MILESTONES; its `outcomeInputs` is `{}` and `confirmedTrigger` is forbidden. A vendor binding is valid only for COMPLETE_MILESTONES or WAIT_EXTERNAL. Vendor COMPLETE has the same empty/forbidden shape. Vendor WAIT requires exact `outcomeInputs:{reportedFacts}` under §4.2 plus a newly user-confirmed trigger valid for the bound transition; omission prevents submission and the server has no default. Its transaction resolves the trusted display source and commits either actual WAIT or the exact USER_ACTION fallback while retaining that same confirmed trigger. CONTINUE_USER_ACTION, DEFER, and REQUIRES_RESOLVER producer bindings are structurally invalid.

The workflow request fingerprint is SHA-256 over the canonical exact map `{workflowId,answers,taskDocumentId,expectedTaskInstanceId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedInteractionEpoch,expectedInteractionRevision,policyVersion,policyFingerprint,flowAttemptProof,outcomeInputs,confirmedTrigger?}`; token and authenticated UID are stored alongside it. The exact op path is server-owned `workflowSubmissions/{workflowSubmissionId}`; the existing user response path remains `users/{uid}/workflowResponses/{workflowId}`. Exact token+fingerprint replay returns the stored `workflow_outcome` before task/time/state/flow checks; changed bytes fail. A fresh request enforces the expected tuple, writes both records and bounded workflow evidence, applies only the bound outcome, performs the exact server-atomic flow clear, and materializes any terminal spawn children in one transaction. It never trusts answer labels to choose a task, outcome, contract field, spawn, or trigger. Guidance completion and vendor submission use their distinct registry producer IDs.

`WorkflowService` is one process-wide Swift `actor` and sole mutator of atomic Application Support file `PeezyWorkflowRequests-v2.json`; its exact payload inside §3.3's envelope is `{records:[record],retiredDiagnostics:[diagnostic]}`. A record is exact `{uid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,workflowId,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration,submissionToken,requestCanonicalJSON,requestSHA256,createdAt,lastDispatchAt?,phase:"PREPARED"|"DISPATCHED"|"RECEIPT"|"APPLYING",receipt?,applicationId?}`. `requestCanonicalJSON` is the complete accepted wire JSON with `CallableTimestampWireV1` strings retained, and `requestSHA256` hashes those exact UTF-8 bytes; neither may be regenerated from parsed Firestore values. `applicationId = "wa1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonical {uid,task_instance_id,task_generation_epoch,submission_token}))` is required only for APPLYING. A diagnostic is exact `{applicationId?,submissionTokenHash,oldTaskInstanceId,oldTaskGenerationEpoch,reason:"TARGET_INSTANCE_RETIRED",observedAt}`; the newest 64 are retained. Local durable dates are UTC RFC 3339 milliseconds. The record is unique by UID+task instance+task generation+workflow+attempt generation, ordered by creation time/token, capped at 16 per UID and 64 total; each request is at most 262,144 bytes and the complete envelope at most 16,777,216 bytes.

PREPARED is atomically durable before dispatch; DISPATCHED is durable immediately before the callable; RECEIPT contains the fully decoded typed receipt after account/instance/token validation. Ambiguous transport retains DISPATCHED and retries identical JSON/token. A deterministic no-commit error removes it after preserving only editable draft data outside this authority store. Before invoking any nontransactional local navigation/accounting callback, the actor atomically writes APPLYING with the deterministic application ID. That callback is therefore at-most-once: normal return removes the record; a crash in APPLYING never invokes it again, instead reloads TasksStore, verifies the receipt's server state/account/instance, performs only state-derived navigation if still needed, and removes the record without replaying haptics, dose/session counters, or completion accounting. Losing those ephemeral effects across a crash is permitted; duplicating them is not. Wrong-account/instance records remain blocked in their namespace.

Before preparing a new record, the actor removes only fully reconciled APPLYING records and deterministic-no-commit records; it never evicts PREPARED, DISPATCHED, ambiguous, blocked, RECEIPT, or unreconciled APPLYING merely for capacity. A reset receipt or loaded TasksStore may, however, conclusively show that the exact old task instance/generation is absent or replaced. A DISPATCHED record is first replayed with identical bytes: an old-instance/generation receipt, or a deterministic no-commit response, closes ambiguity. The actor then appends the exact bounded diagnostic `{applicationId?,submissionTokenHash,oldTaskInstanceId,oldTaskGenerationEpoch,reason:"TARGET_INSTANCE_RETIRED",observedAt}` to a 64-entry local archive and removes the active record without applying/retrying it. It never treats a same-document new instance or generation as the target. If the UID cap, global cap, request cap, or envelope cap would otherwise be exceeded, it rejects before dispatch and exposes the pending-request recovery surface. That surface permits Retry for DISPATCHED, Apply for RECEIPT, Reconcile for APPLYING, and Discard only for never-dispatched PREPARED, exact no-submission proof, or the conclusive retired-instance path. It never guesses expiry for an ambiguous request. Malformed/hash mismatch follows the common fixed-quarantine `CORRUPT_BLOCKED` rule; no request is synthesized. Tests cover receipt-before-reset, DISPATCHED replay after reset, regenerated same document, cap recovery, callback→crash→relaunch with zero duplicate accounting, 17th same-UID, 65th global, oversized request/file, multiple accounts, auth purge vs receipt, simultaneous prepare/dispatch, corrupted file, ambiguous full store, and receipt-before-application.

Policy receipt consumption is closed at every eligible workflow caller and branches only on the required `workflow.disposition`. `COMPLETED` means the server has already completed the task, atomically cleared the attempt, and materialized declared children; after APPLYING is durable, the client reloads `TasksStore`, performs at most one existing `.completedAlreadyPersisted` local navigation/accounting callback, and issues no SpawnService, TaskActionService status, or flow-clear write. For a WAIT-bound producer, `WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL` means the server committed trusted `matching_in_progress` WAIT, while `USER_ACTION_TRACKED` means it committed the no-name `InProgress` waiting-display fallback. After APPLYING is durable, either branch releases the local barrier, closes the flow through an at-most-once already-persisted/no-write callback, reloads, and presents only that matching stored surface without completion accounting. A missing or other disposition for the binding, or receipt/task inequality, is invalid and remains unapplied. No policy success invokes a legacy `onComplete`/complete/snooze/dismiss writer. Receipt account/task instance/generation mismatch is never applied. The two named eligible callers RentTruck/SetupInternet and six authorized generic/insurance callers each test COMPLETE, both WAIT-result branches including WAIT-ineligible→`USER_ACTION_TRACKED`, replay, lost response, crash in APPLYING, malformed/missing disposition, and zero second writes/accounting.

`PACKING_SUPPLIES_KIT` is a client-materialized synthetic task with no catalog row and is policy-ineligible. `SuppliesKitView` therefore has only these branches: `absentLegacy` runs the existing `supplies_kit` customization/submission, `updateSuppliesKit`, `BOX_RETURN` generation, payload bytes, callbacks, and no-token WorkflowService overload byte-for-byte; any present `taskInteraction`—whether otherwise decodable or malformed—renders the embedded control passive and sends no workflow, task, generation, or direct Firestore write. Its synthetic writer must never copy/preserve/add `taskInteraction`, `taskInteractionState`, `dispositionContract`, or H56 policy fields from an existing row. There is no valid-policy Supplies producer, no WAIT selector, and no v2 receipt path. Tests freeze the legacy `SuppliesKitOrderPayload` JSON at its existing bounds and callbacks, present-policy passive behavior, and no policy-field preservation during regeneration.

Mandatory producer fixtures cover guidance COMPLETE and the full-policy vendor WAIT: empty vs bounded reported facts, confirmed-trigger presence/source, exact replay after clear/reset, token reuse with changed answers/trigger/expected tuple, no token, wrong account/task instance/task/workflow, stale tuple, proof generation/revision mismatch, forbidden effect, child-spawn failure, and lost-response retry. The Swift payload tests assert `userId` and every legacy `waiting_*` key are absent on the policy path and prove the exact local request-store lifecycle. Legacy policy-absent guidance/vendor response bytes and callbacks remain unchanged.

The exact vendor-WAIT request fixture uses authenticated UID `fixture-user`, task/workflow `fixture_notice`, task generation `0`, task instance `11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555`, attempt `attempt-fixture-1`, attempt generation `1`, write revision `7`, and this 243-byte persisted tail with fingerprint `fp2_dc5ee51f8897b51b9c4b55d371755d25fec9af88ffa25c80d9e6ef20fa3bdb1f`:

```json
{"flowAnswerIdentities":{},"flowAnswers":{"submitted":["true"]},"flowAttemptGeneration":1,"flowAttemptId":"attempt-fixture-1","flowPath":["submit"],"flowWriteRevision":7,"stage":"book","task_instance_id":"11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555"}
```

The six-component token preimage is `12|fixture-user|14|fixture_notice|14|fixture_notice|36|11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555|17|attempt-fixture-1|1|1`. The token is `workflow-v2-8b8736bc4258eedb2eb93c87bfba13415e156f6771179cd27fd5098986342f28`. The complete exact request is 1,087 canonical bytes:

```json
{"answers":{"answers":{"submitted":["true"]},"workflowId":"fixture_notice"},"confirmedTrigger":{"at":"2026-09-24T12:00:00.000Z","kind":"date","source":"PROMISED_DATE","trigger_confirmed":true,"user_report":{"bounded_facts":{},"operation_id":"workflow-v2-8b8736bc4258eedb2eb93c87bfba13415e156f6771179cd27fd5098986342f28","reported_at":"2026-09-01T12:00:00.000Z"}},"expectedInteractionEpoch":0,"expectedInteractionRevision":0,"expectedTaskGenerationEpoch":0,"expectedTaskInstanceId":"11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555","flowAttemptProof":{"flowAttemptGeneration":1,"flowAttemptId":"attempt-fixture-1","flowWriteRevision":7,"persistedTailFingerprint":"fp2_dc5ee51f8897b51b9c4b55d371755d25fec9af88ffa25c80d9e6ef20fa3bdb1f","taskInstanceId":"11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555"},"outcomeInputs":{"reportedFacts":{}},"policyFingerprint":"p2i1_fa424ac0b92b59d4e7ebd821cba4b2b191b30503845172a2faf6f1b3aed63613","policyVersion":1,"submissionToken":"workflow-v2-8b8736bc4258eedb2eb93c87bfba13415e156f6771179cd27fd5098986342f28","taskDocumentId":"fixture_notice","workflowId":"fixture_notice"}
```

Omitting only `submissionToken` gives the 990-byte canonical request-fingerprint map; its SHA-256 is `4708b4d34486775a64d0336f7d7758d9ef0d77e73a3b5fc4f1fdc95fe1ffae1d` (the complete 1,087-byte request SHA-256 is `5bf0b6946fec121834087228c8c969302b25c5266d42b9d4be6d5cd3038178d7`).

### 4.6 Generic and atomic terminal spawn closure

The deployed generic `spawnTasks` callable remains the sole conversation/nudge/onComplete request producer and keeps the t1/t2 document-ID formulas. Its exact request is the existing closed `{token,source:{kind:"conversation"|"nudge"|"onComplete",id},spawns,answers:null|map,expectedUserId?}` map plus required safe nonnegative `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch` and optional `sourceTaskExpectation`. When present, that expectation is exact `{taskDocumentId,taskGenerationEpoch,taskInstanceId}`; its generation must equal the top-level expectation, `source.id` must equal its task document ID, and `taskInstanceId` is the exact captured stored nonblank string or explicit JSON null when absent. It is required for `onComplete` and for any conversation/nudge caller launched from a mapped task; it is forbidden when no task row is the source. The authorized client captures these bytes from the same TasksStore snapshot before any asynchronous preparation and retains them unchanged through retry. Unknown keys and a caller that omits a required expectation or its explicit instance member fail before reads.

`spawnRequestFingerprint = SHA-256(canonical complete accepted request with token omitted)`; the stored token record is exact `{schema_version:2,token,request_fingerprint,task_generation_epoch,source_task_expectation?,result,result_sha256,created_at}` and the token document ID equals `token`. `result` is exact `{created:[{id,taskId,title,dueDateISO}],taskGenerationEpoch,sourceTaskInstanceId?}`; `sourceTaskInstanceId` is present exactly when `sourceTaskExpectation` is present and then is the same string-or-null value. `result_sha256` hashes the complete map. Every fresh or replay transaction reads token, user root, and source task when named before any writes and requires reset inactive, root generation equal the request/token generation, and source path/generation/string-or-null instance byte-equal. Then exactly four results exist: (1) a never-committed pre-reset/stale-source request returns `failed-precondition/STALE_STATE` and writes no token/task; (2) an exact token+fingerprint replay while generation/source remain current returns the stored result; (3) a committed token replay after reset/source retirement returns `STALE_STATE`, leaves the old token/receipt retained, and exposes no old IDs for application to the new generation; (4) the same token with changed fingerprint returns `failed-precondition/OPERATION_REUSED`, while a new token with changed/stale generation or source identity returns `STALE_STATE`, all with zero writes. The client also requires response generation/source string-or-null instance equal its captured expectation before applying. Catalog validation follows those replay/fence reads; all catalog reads precede any write.

A fresh request validates all targets with the shared canonical policy validator and the dynamic producer row in §2.2. Policy-absent target task bytes remain legacy-equivalent except every fresh create also carries the ignored captured/current `task_generation_epoch`; an already-stored policy-absent subject-aware task may retain an absent epoch only if it predates v5 and any title/institution enrichment runs through §4.7's generation-fenced transaction, never a policy/fence backfill.

For a policy-bearing generic target, `genericSpawnInstancePreimage = canonical {uid,task_document_id,catalog_task_id,source_kind,source_id,task_generation_epoch,source_task_instance_id?,move_event_id?}`. `source_task_instance_id` is present exactly when `sourceTaskExpectation` is present and copies its string-or-null value; `move_event_id` is present exactly when the target policy declares it. `task_instance_id = "ti1_" + first40(SHA-256(genericSpawnInstancePreimage))`. The fresh task copies the exact catalog policy, those captured/current root generation/move values, literal `flowWriteRevision:0`, `notesWriteRevision:0`, and `quotesWriteRevision:0`, status Upcoming, existing catalog-derived projection/due-date/provenance, and the exact subject/institution/move fields allowed by its request mode; it forbids state, contract, tail, and fences. A move-requiring generic request uses the shared server `ensureActiveMoveEventId`: it pre-generates one lowercase UUID before the retryable transaction, atomically writes it only when the root field is absent and reset/generation are unchanged, otherwise requires the existing valid union member, then copies it at the one selected path. The spawn token/result and root/task creates commit together. Reset rotation therefore changes both generation and deterministic instance even for the same t2 path/source; old evidence/receipt/intent/proof namespaces cannot recur.

Non-subject t1 creation requires its path absent. Subject-aware t2 may reuse an existing policy task only without writing it and only when document ID, authenticated UID, catalog task, normalized subject/institution, exact spawnedFrom including conditional move member, current generation epoch, deterministic instance, and pinned policy equal the immutable fresh expectation. It does not require live `flowWriteRevision` equal fresh-create zero: any current revision/tail/state must instead be structurally valid under §5.5 and is preserved byte-for-byte. Any immutable mismatch, invalid live progress, attempted policy-task display enrichment, malformed policy/state, or existing non-subject path is `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`. The existing response item remains exact `{id,taskId,title,dueDateISO}`; policy identity arrives through the task listener, not an expanded wire. Tests cover both generic modes and source kinds; task-source string and explicit-null instances; missing, surplus, or mismatched instance members; no-task-source expectation omission; policy copy/omission/malformed; every case-path mode; absent-root establishment; token loss/replay; changed token bytes; t1 collision; t2 new-token reuse after H56 progress; catalog-policy drift; reset epoch; and zero policy-task enrichment.

Only a successful policy COMPLETE transition—whole-case assertion, handoff outcome, waiting outcome, or workflow producer—evaluates catalog `onCompleteSpawns`; WAIT/USER/DEFER/NOT_APPLICABLE/resolver/navigation never spawn. Before any transaction write, the reducer reads the source catalog row named by the owner's immutable `taskId`, requires identity with that task, and validates `onCompleteSpawns` as an ordered unique array of at most 20 exact `{id,dateRule?}` maps. `dateRule` is exact `{anchor:"moveDate"|"spawn",offsetDays}` with integer `-365...365`. It then reads every target catalog row, requires `spawnedOnly:true`, valid task identity, valid/absent policy, and the fixed terminal-producer path closure in §2.2. Missing, duplicate, malformed, self-referential, reset-active, missing source-case authority, kind/path mismatch, or policy-invalid input aborts completion with no task, evidence, child, or op write.

For ordinal `i` and target catalog ID, let `targetMoveEventId` be absent when the target policy omits `move_event_id_field`, otherwise the transactionally validated current user-root value. `spawnIdentity` is exact canonical `{uid,source_task_document_id,source_task_instance_id,target_catalog_id,ordinal,move_event_id?}`, with `move_event_id` present exactly when `targetMoveEventId` is present. Child document ID is `t3_ + first40(sha256(spawnIdentity))`. A policy-bearing target gets stable `task_instance_id = "ti1_" + first40(sha256(spawnIdentity))`, the transaction's current user-root `task_generation_epoch`, root `flowWriteRevision:0`, `notesWriteRevision:0`, and `quotesWriteRevision:0`, exact copied policy, and base `spawnedFrom:{kind:"onComplete",id:sourceTaskDocumentId,source_task_instance_id:sourceTaskInstanceId,target_catalog_id:targetCatalogId,ordinal:i}`. The producer writes `targetMoveEventId` at exactly the target policy's §2.2 path: root, nested subject, or conditional `spawnedFrom.move_event_id`; every other alias is absent. Those values deliberately do not depend on the terminal operation, so H54 undo→reconfirm reuses the same immutable child. A policy-absent target retains the byte-compatible existing `spawnTasks.buildTaskDoc` projection plus only the ignored current `task_generation_epoch`, omits policy identity/revisions, and keeps exact legacy `spawnedFrom:{kind:"onComplete",id:sourceTaskDocumentId}`. The terminal operation remains only in source history/operation receipt. The entry's dateRule overrides the target row solely for the existing UTC `resolveDueDate` pacing calculation on first creation; it never creates deadline evidence. Target title/provenance/policy and initial due-date bytes are server-derived.

All child reads precede writes in the same completion transaction. An absent child is created. An existing policy child must match stable identity/provenance/policy/task instance/generation epoch/all three root revisions; an existing legacy child must match its legacy immutable identity/provenance and policy absence. Either match is reused without rewriting status, due date, or user progress; any collision aborts atomically. The ordered child IDs appear in `spawnedTaskDocumentIds`. Operation replay returns them without reevaluation.

Before **any** terminal-completion transaction write, and before the atomic bulk packing write in §4.7, the reducer materializes every concrete pre/post document map and computes `TerminalTransactionBudgetV1 = FirestoreWriteBudgetV1(changed_paths)`. The changed set includes, as applicable, source/original/replacement task, every child, user root, ordinary/bulk operation, confirmation snapshot, workflow submission/response, spawn token, notification intent, deadline-source mutation, packing plan/readiness, and every other transaction create/update/delete; a byte-identical reused read-only document contributes zero. All server-authored values are concrete first and no `FieldValue` sentinel can reach this stage.

`FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` is a closed reimplementation of Firestore's documented storage and index-entry size formulas. UTF-8 collection ID, string document ID, field-name/path, and string-value size is byte count plus one. Document-name size is the sum of every collection/document path component plus 16. Boolean/null are one byte; integer, double, and Timestamp are eight; an array value is the sum of its actual element value sizes; a map value is the sum of each nested field-name and value size plus 32. Document size is document-name size plus all top-level field-name/value sizes plus 32. Unsupported schema values reject. Indexed values are capped at 1,500 bytes for entry sizing.

Index entries are enumerated from the exact normalized §7.4 registry, never estimated by a flat allowance. Unless exempted, each non-array/non-map leaf produces collection-scope ascending and descending automatic entries; each map produces ascending/descending entries for the whole value and recursively indexes its descendants; each array produces ascending/descending entries for its whole value and one array-contains entry for every actual element. A parent-map or wildcard exemption suppresses every inherited automatic entry, while an explicit child re-enable overrides it. Each collection-scope automatic entry costs indexed-document name + parent-document name (zero for no parent document) + full escaped field-path string size + truncated indexed-value size + 32. Each configured collection-group manual composite produces one entry exactly when every field exists; the three §7.4 composites have no array field, and each entry costs indexed-document name + sum of truncated indexed values + 32. Manual indexes remain active when their fields are automatically exempted.

For each path, the estimator treats entries as a multiset keyed by scope/index identity/mode/field path/value bytes. Create charge is prospective document size plus all prospective entry sizes. Delete charge is current document size plus all current entry sizes. Update charge is prospective document size plus the byte-size sum of the exact current/prospective multiset symmetric difference: removed old and added new entries are both charged, while a byte-identical entry is zero. Before summing, each document must satisfy its named schema/service cap, no entry may exceed 7,680 bytes, entry count may not exceed 40,000, and the sum of that document's entry sizes may not exceed 8,388,608. Every intermediate/total is a safe integer. The transaction proceeds only when the total is `<= 8,388,608`; `8,388,609`, a per-document/index violation, or an unencodable map returns the exact no-write `failed-precondition/TERMINAL_WRITE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` details above. Completion+receipt+children remain atomic and cannot be paged to evade the check.

The first S1 RED fixture constructs the real current catalog's worst reachable 20-child closure plus maximum admitted source/operation/evidence/snapshot documents, prints every charged path split into document/add/remove index bytes, and must fit before reducer implementation continues; failure is a design contradiction and stops for owner redesign. `functions/taskInteraction.js` owns production arithmetic and a normalized in-source registry whose set and SHA must match exact root `firestore.indexes.json`. Existing `functions/rules-tests/firestoreRules.test.js` independently parses that file and reimplements the document/index walk without importing production arithmetic. Its demo-emulator fixture constructs deterministic valid prospective sets at exact 8,388,608 and 8,388,609: at-budget commits, over-budget returns the typed error and writes nothing. It covers create/update/delete, map recursion, every array element, exemptions and child re-enable, composite present/missing, 1,500-byte truncation, entry/count/per-document limits, every ancillary family, one-large versus twenty-child distributions, historical sub-millisecond timestamps, and production/oracle per-path parity. The 8 MiB cap preserves the amendment's 20% headroom below Firestore's documented 10 MiB transaction request limit; no production-connected measurement is permitted.

An H54 confirmation additionally freezes `terminal_spawn_closure` in the server-only plan-change cycle: exact `{source_catalog_update_time,entries}` in declared order, capped at 20. Each entry has base `{ordinal,target_catalog_id,child_document_id}` and exactly one disjoint identity branch: a policy child requires `{target_policy_fingerprint,move_event_id?}` and forbids `legacy_immutable_projection_hash`; a policy-absent child requires `{legacy_immutable_projection_hash}` and forbids target fingerprint and move event. In the policy branch, `move_event_id` is present exactly when the target policy declares a move path and equals the immutable value written there. The legacy hash is lowercase SHA-256 of the complete frozen legacy immutable identity/provenance projection named above. Undo retains this closure while not deleting/rolling back children. A later reconfirm does not reevaluate live `onCompleteSpawns`; it requires every frozen child to exist and match its exact branch, then returns the same ordered IDs. Missing/colliding/cross-branch child fails the reconfirm atomically with `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`; target/source catalog reorder/add/remove after first confirmation is irrelevant to that cycle. Reset and lost-response retries cannot duplicate. `functions/spawnTasks.js` exports this shared transaction helper and its existing callable remains byte-compatible. Policy callers never issue SpawnService. RED cases cover each COMPLETE producer, WAIT no-spawn, all three move-event paths and impossible producer/path combinations, both closure branches and every forbidden cross-branch member, valid-policy vs policy-absent epoch byte shape, exact replay, response loss, invalid child/edge, collision, reset, due-date override, exact policy copy, waiting-later COMPLETE, undo→reconfirm after untouched/already-progressed child, catalog graph drift, and missing frozen child.

### 4.7 Legacy generation-fenced mutation closure

`LegacyTaskExpectationV1` is exact `{taskDocumentId,taskGenerationEpoch,taskInstanceId}`. The generation is a safe nonnegative integer; an existing pre-v5 row with the stored member absent decodes only as `0`. `taskInstanceId` is always present in the request as either the exact stored nonblank instance string or JSON `null`; a missing stored instance matches only null. `LegacyDocumentExpectationV1` is exact `{exists:false}` or `{exists:true,taskGenerationEpoch,taskInstanceId}` with the same decoding. Every v5 client captures the expectation from the presented `TasksStore` snapshot before its first await and retains the exact request bytes and operation ID through ambiguous retry. A fresh transaction reads operation, user root, reset marker, and every named task/auxiliary document before writes. It requires reset inactive, root `taskGenerationEpoch` equal the expected value, document ID equality, each row's absent-as-0/stored generation equality, and absent/stored instance equality. No task path, response, local callback, or operation ID can substitute for this tuple.

An existing exact operation is not immediately adoptable merely because its request fingerprint matches. The server rereads current root and every receipt identity first. If generation or instance no longer matches, it returns deterministic no-write `STALE_TASK_GENERATION`, retains the old operation for audit, and does not expose its old result/task IDs to the caller. Only a still-current exact identity returns the stored response with `replayed:true`; changed bytes are `OPERATION_REUSED`. A never-committed delayed request after reset also returns `STALE_TASK_GENERATION` and creates no operation. Local adoption separately requires the current mapped identity to equal the captured expectation after the await; otherwise the response is ignored and the live listener owns reconciliation.

The authenticated `mutateLegacyTask` action accepts exactly `{action:"mutateLegacyTask",operationId,taskDocumentId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedTaskInstanceId,mutation}`. Common eligibility requires genuinely absent `taskInteraction` and a task document ID that does not start `a2_`. It then requires exactly one of these disjoint lifecycle branches:

- `PLAIN`: `dispositionContract` and every current plan-audit key (`planChangeRevision`, `planChangeState`, `planChangeHistory`, `pendingAmendmentTaskId`, `firstConfirmationUndoUntil`, `confirmationUndoUsed`, `confirmationOriginalFingerprint`, `confirmationAmendmentFingerprint`) are absent.
- `H54_REOPENED`: `dispositionContract` is absent; `planChangeState == "reopened"`; `planChangeRevision` is a safe positive integer; bounded `planChangeHistory` passes the shared H54 validator, contains the matching supersede cycle whose `priorDispositionContract` is absent, and ends in the matching reopen record at that same revision; `pendingAmendmentTaskId`, `firstConfirmationUndoUntil`, `confirmationOriginalFingerprint`, and `confirmationAmendmentFingerprint` are absent; and `confirmationUndoUsed` is absent or literal false. Every mutation preserves all H54 audit members byte-for-byte.

The second branch preserves the shipped contractless reopen path: current Home/focus/row/detail controls can reach it, so COMPLETE/SNOOZE/USER_IN_PROGRESS and the other applicable generic mutations remain active after regeneration while gaining the same generation/string-or-null-instance fence. Contract-bearing rows, live retired/pending/confirmed cycles, reopened cycles whose prior contract existed, malformed audit, and every other audit-bearing row remain under their named H54/workflow/spawn/scheduler reducer and never receive a generic Admin mutation. Their generic controls are not mutation surfaces: the shared mount exposes only the existing named contract/H54 lifecycle surface. A present or malformed policy may never enter this action. The exact closed mutation union is:

```text
{kind:"SET_STAGE",stage}
{kind:"WRITE_FLOW_PROGRESS",stage?,flowPath,flowAnswers,
 flowAnswerIdentities,flowAttemptId?}
{kind:"CLEAR_FLOW_PROGRESS"}
{kind:"CLAIM_NUDGE",choice:"Dismissed"|"Converted"}
{kind:"COMPLETE"}
{kind:"REOPEN"}
{kind:"USER_IN_PROGRESS",returnAt}
{kind:"SNOOZE",until}
{kind:"REPLACE_NOTES",notes}
{kind:"REPLACE_QUOTES",quotes}
{kind:"COMPLETE_BOOKING",bookingDetails}
{kind:"MIGRATE_BOOK_MOVERS_PRESENTATION",title,desc}
{kind:"UPDATE_SUPPLIES_KIT",suppliesKit,desc}
{kind:"DISMISS_SUPPLIES_KIT"}
{kind:"SAVE_READINESS",items,scheduledDate}
{kind:"COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION",packingPlan,
 consequenceLine,nextSessionDate?}
```

`stage`, the complete legacy flow members, notes, and quotes use exactly §5.5's enums, presence rules, and caps; legacy clear deletes exactly `flowPath`, `flowAnswers`, `flowAnswerIdentities`, and `flowAttemptId` and retains stage. `CLAIM_NUDGE` accepts only a current status in `Upcoming/InProgress/pending/matching_in_progress/UserInProgress/Snoozed`; success writes status equal choice and `answeredBy:{choice,opId:operationId}`, while an already-terminal row writes nothing and returns its stored choice/op ID. `COMPLETE` writes literal `status:"Completed"` and server `completedAt = server_write_time`; `REOPEN` writes literal `status:"Upcoming"` and deletes `completedAt`; `USER_IN_PROGRESS` writes literal `status:"UserInProgress"`, server `userInProgressDate = server_write_time`, and parsed request `userInProgressReturnDate`; `SNOOZE` writes literal `status:"Snoozed"`, parsed request `snoozedUntil`, and server `lastSnoozedAt = server_write_time`. `returnAt`/`until` are `CallableTimestampWireV1` and must be future when first validated.

Except for CLAIM_NUDGE's closed status set, migration's Completed/already-current no-op, and COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION's named prior-uncompleted relation, no legacy mutation kind adds a prior-`status` predicate beyond the common identity/lifecycle/target rules. It accepts an absent or any `TaskCanonicalV1`-encodable legacy status value and overwrites or preserves it only as that kind's field relation states, matching the baseline status-unconditional writers. Tests run every otherwise-applicable kind against each current `TaskStatus` literal, an unknown NFC status string, and absent status; they separately prove the three named exceptions and every target predicate.

`bookingDetails` is exact `{booked:true,company?,moveDate?,arrivalWindow?,crewSize?,crewHourlyRate?}`. COMPLETE_BOOKING is legal only when the stored immutable catalog `taskId == "BOOK_YOUR_MOVERS"`. Company and arrival window are trimmed NFC 1–256 bytes when present; move date is `CallableTimestampWireV1`; crew size is integer `1...100`; hourly rate is finite binary64 `0...1000000`. Success parses move date, adds server `savedAt = server_write_time` inside the map, and atomically writes Completed with `completedAt = server_write_time`; either time in the request is forbidden. Its complete accepted request and fingerprint use `TaskCanonicalV1`, preserving fractional hourly-rate bytes. MIGRATE_BOOK_MOVERS_PRESENTATION is legal only when stored `taskId == "BOOK_MOVERS"` and accepts only one of the two fixed pairs from `MoversTitleMigration`: `("Get Moving Quotes","Learn how mover pricing really works, then start collecting binding estimates from three USDOT-licensed movers with the same facts in every hand.")` or `("Compare your moving quotes","Finish comparing the quotes you already collected, then book the company you choose.")`. The server reruns the current legacy classifier and rejects a pair that is not its deterministic desired pair. When the row is noncompleted and does not already carry the desired title/description, it replaces only those two fields. When the row is Completed or both fields already match, it performs no task mutation but commits the current-identity-bound operation/receipt with `result:{state:"already_current"}`; the client marks its session guard complete for either `applied` or `already_current` and never retries/logs the no-op as failure.

UPDATE_SUPPLIES_KIT and DISMISS_SUPPLIES_KIT are legal only when stored `taskId == "PACKING_SUPPLIES_KIT"` and `generatedBy == "packingPlan"`. `suppliesKit` is exact `{small,medium,large,xl,wardrobe,dishPack,tape,paper,wrap,mattressBags,headroomPercent,totalPriceCents,supplyRatesCents,deliveryBy?}`. The ten counts, headroom, total, and every member of exact `supplyRatesCents:{smallBox,mediumBox,largeBox,xlBox,dishPack,wardrobe,pictureCarton,packingPaper10lb,bubbleRoll,tapeRoll,mattressBag,stretchWrap,marker}` are safe integers `0...100000000`; `deliveryBy` is `CallableTimestampWireV1`. Server recomputation requires nonnegative counts, literal `headroomPercent:12`, and exact total from submitted rates/counts. `desc` must equal the current deterministic itemized summary and be at most 8,192 UTF-8 bytes. UPDATE replaces only kit/desc, parses delivery time, and adds server `kitCustomizedAt = server_write_time`. DISMISS writes `UserInProgress`, server `kitDismissedAt = userInProgressDate = server_write_time`, and deletes `userInProgressReturnDate`.

Readiness `items` is the exact five-key Boolean map `{allSessionsComplete,furnitureDisassembled,accessReserved,pathClear,firstNightBagSetAside}` and request `scheduledDate` is `CallableTimestampWireV1`; request `completedAt`/`updatedAt` are forbidden. SAVE_READINESS is legal only for `taskDocumentId == "PACKING_READINESS_GATE"`. It parses the scheduled date and atomically replaces/merges `readiness/current` exact `{schema_version:1,task_generation_epoch,items,scheduledDate,updatedAt,completedAt?}` with `updatedAt = server_write_time`; when all five values are true it sets readiness and task `completedAt = server_write_time` and task Completed, otherwise it removes completion from both and writes task Upcoming. Both documents must match captured root/task identity and any existing readiness generation. Direct client auxiliary writes are forbidden.

`packingPlan` for COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION is the exact §3.1 current map `{schema_version:1,task_generation_epoch,moveDate,generatedAt,reflowedAt?,sessions}`. Sessions are ordered, unique, at most 128, and exact `{taskId,sessionKey,sourceKeys,rooms,roomLabel,estMinutes,scheduledDate,itemSummary,isFirstNightBag,isBehindPace,completedAt?}` with the §3.1 component/aggregate caps: identifiers/labels/items are NFC 1–512 bytes, string arrays contain at most 128 unique members, `estMinutes` is integer `1...1440`, and every request date is `CallableTimestampWireV1`. Historical `completedAt` values on other sessions are comparison-only wire projections of server reads. The common `taskDocumentId` must equal exactly one submitted/prior session `taskId`; that task must be `generatedBy:"packingPlan"` with workflow `packing_session`. Its prior and submitted target-session completion are absent, and every submitted plan byte matches prior after wire normalization. The server then parses the plan and inserts `server_write_time` only at that target session. `consequenceLine` is the deterministic engine result, trimmed NFC 1–1,024 bytes; optional `nextSessionDate` is `CallableTimestampWireV1` and must equal the engine result. Success atomically replaces the constructed plan and writes target task Completed with both root and nested-session `completedAt = server_write_time`. The complete accepted request/fingerprint uses `TaskCanonicalV1`; any target-session completion value and every sentinel reject, while historical non-target completion strings are admitted only as the comparison fields just defined. The response exposes only the validated result `{consequenceLine,nextSessionDate?}`.

The ordinary legacy receipt is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_task_mutation",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskDocumentId,taskGenerationEpoch,taskInstanceId,mutationKind,result}`. `taskInstanceId` is string or null. `result` is `{state:"applied"}` except CLAIM_NUDGE uses `{state:"won"}` or `{state:"already_terminal",choice,opId?}`, MIGRATE_BOOK_MOVERS_PRESENTATION may use `{state:"already_current"}`, and COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION uses `{state:"applied",consequenceLine,nextSessionDate?}`. Server timestamps are never returned as caller authority. Fingerprint is `"op1_" + SHA-256(complete accepted-request canonical bytes)`; REPLACE_QUOTES, COMPLETE_BOOKING, and COMPLETE_PACKING_SESSION use `TaskCanonicalV1` for the complete request and every other legacy mutation uses the strict request domain. Receipt and request hashes are retained in a `TASK_OPERATION`, and replay changes only the Boolean. A migration `already_current` operation has the target identity but an empty `prior_snapshots` array because it mutates no task.

Packing-plan regeneration uses the separate authenticated action `persistLegacyPackingPlan` with exact request `{action,operationId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedPackingPlan,expectedReadiness,planDocument,taskSets,staleTaskDeletes,deleteReadiness,deleteBuyPackingSupplies}`. The two auxiliary expectations are `LegacyDocumentExpectationV1`. `taskSets` is a document-path-ordered array of at most 130 exact `{taskDocumentId,expectedExisting,taskData}`; stale deletes are a document-path-ordered array of at most 128 exact `{taskDocumentId,expectedExisting}` whose expectation must have `exists:true`, disjoint from task sets. `deleteReadiness` and `deleteBuyPackingSupplies` are Booleans. When `deleteBuyPackingSupplies` is true, `staleTaskDeletes` contains exactly one entry with `taskDocumentId:"BUY_PACKING_SUPPLIES"`; when false, that ID is forbidden. `deleteReadiness` true requires `expectedReadiness.exists:true` and deletes it; false never deletes it. The exact data-write equation is `taskSets.count + staleTaskDeletes.count + 1 + (deleteReadiness ? 1 : 0) <= 259`; the `+1` is `packingPlan/current`, and deleting BUY is already counted in stale deletes. The transaction additionally creates exactly one `LEGACY_PACKING_PERSIST_OPERATION`, so total writes are `dataWriteCount + 1 <= 260`.

Every timestamp in `planDocument` and `taskData` is `CallableTimestampWireV1`; the server parses it before storage. `taskData.createdAt` is always forbidden: an overwrite preserves the current stored Timestamp byte-for-byte, including historical sub-millisecond precision, and a fresh row receives `server_write_time`. A supplies-kit row with a real `deliveryBy` carries matching semantic `dueDate`; without it the request omits `dueDate` and the server authors fallback `dueDate = server_write_time`. Server-authored update/completion fields are omitted; historical completion values are admitted only at the plan/session paths declared above and must match the transaction's prior plan/task or a deterministic server projection. Every constructed full task/plan document carries the request epoch; all task rows are exact policy/contract/audit-absent generated-packing shapes from §3.1, and `expectedExisting` binds absence or complete prior legacy identity. The transaction compares root, marker absence, both auxiliaries, every overwrite, and every delete, constructs the concrete prospective maps with no sentinel, enforces component and Firestore-write budgets, and only then performs all sets/deletes plus operation creation atomically. Its exact response is `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_packing_persist",operationId,replayed:false,accountUid,taskGenerationEpoch,setTaskDocumentIds,deletedTaskDocumentIds,packingPlanWritten:true,readinessDeleted,buyPackingSuppliesDeleted}`. Operation replay first revalidates current root; a stale generation never reveals or reapplies retained path arrays.

The exact fractional COMPLETE_BOOKING accepted-request fixture is this one-line, no-trailing-LF `TaskCanonicalV1` value:

```json
{"action":"mutateLegacyTask","expectedTaskGenerationEpoch":0,"expectedTaskInstanceId":null,"mutation":{"bookingDetails":{"arrivalWindow":"8:00 AM-10:00 AM","booked":true,"company":"Fixture Movers","crewHourlyRate":149.95,"crewSize":3,"moveDate":"2026-09-24T12:00:00.000Z"},"kind":"COMPLETE_BOOKING"},"operationId":"booking-fractional-fixture","taskDocumentId":"BOOK_YOUR_MOVERS"}
```

It is 379 UTF-8 bytes, `request_sha256 = 5ac643ce693d7695576636f530f82c65aed60ccccf9715c29a0932708c0905a8`, and `request_fingerprint = op1_5ac643ce693d7695576636f530f82c65aed60ccccf9715c29a0932708c0905a8`.

The exact fractional legacy REPLACE_QUOTES fixture is this one-line, no-trailing-LF `TaskCanonicalV1` value:

```json
{"action":"mutateLegacyTask","expectedTaskGenerationEpoch":0,"expectedTaskInstanceId":null,"mutation":{"kind":"REPLACE_QUOTES","quotes":[{"company":"Fixture Movers","crew":3,"hours":2.5,"notes":"Binding estimate","perManRate":49.95,"travelFee":125.5}]},"operationId":"quotes-fractional-fixture","taskDocumentId":"FIND_MOVERS"}
```

It is 326 UTF-8 bytes, `request_sha256 = 23f3209089c74b9c439b4a890c5c2ca10c3d7e5191b47d75c8c6e56b89cf99a1`, and `request_fingerprint = op1_23f3209089c74b9c439b4a890c5c2ca10c3d7e5191b47d75c8c6e56b89cf99a1`. JS and Swift independently reproduce those bytes. Replacing only `perManRate` with its next representable binary64 value emits `49.95000000000001`, changes the hash, and makes reuse of the fixture operation ID return `OPERATION_REUSED`; exact replay of the original bytes returns the original receipt. No integer-unit conversion is permitted.

The exact fractional `persistLegacyPackingPlan` accepted-request fixture is this one-line, no-trailing-LF `bulk_request_preimage`:

```json
{"action":"persistLegacyPackingPlan","deleteBuyPackingSupplies":false,"deleteReadiness":false,"expectedPackingPlan":{"exists":false},"expectedReadiness":{"exists":false},"expectedTaskGenerationEpoch":0,"operationId":"packing-fractional-fixture","planDocument":{"generatedAt":"2026-09-01T12:00:00.000Z","moveDate":"2026-09-24T12:00:00.000Z","schema_version":1,"sessions":[{"estMinutes":10,"isBehindPace":false,"isFirstNightBag":false,"itemSummary":["Dishes"],"roomLabel":"Kitchen","rooms":["room-1"],"scheduledDate":"2026-09-20T12:00:00.000Z","sessionKey":"session-1","sourceKeys":["room-1:item-1"],"taskId":"PACKING_SESSION_1"}],"task_generation_epoch":0},"staleTaskDeletes":[],"taskSets":[{"expectedExisting":{"exists":false},"taskData":{"actionCategory":"packing","actionType":"in-app","category":"packing","desc":"Here's what's in it: Dishes","dueDate":"2026-09-20T12:00:00.000Z","estHours":0.16666666666666666,"generatedBy":"packingPlan","id":"PACKING_SESSION_1","packingSession":{"estMinutes":10,"isBehindPace":false,"isFirstNightBag":false,"itemSummary":["Dishes"],"roomLabel":"Kitchen","rooms":["room-1"],"scheduledDate":"2026-09-20T12:00:00.000Z","sessionKey":"session-1","sourceKeys":["room-1:item-1"],"taskId":"PACKING_SESSION_1"},"selfServiceOnly":true,"status":"Upcoming","taskId":"PACKING_SESSION_1","taskType":"provide_info","task_generation_epoch":0,"tips":"Pack only this session. Peezy will reflow the rest if plans change.","title":"Today: Kitchen. About 10 minutes.","urgencyPercentage":80,"whyNeeded":"One focused packing session keeps moving day on pace.","workflowId":"packing_session"},"taskDocumentId":"PACKING_SESSION_1"}]}
```

It is 1,648 UTF-8 bytes, `request_sha256 = 34cae4d673e1a120bb3126a33ec5bfeda8317235688d6212fa4ad8d9baae22b8`, and `request_fingerprint = op1_34cae4d673e1a120bb3126a33ec5bfeda8317235688d6212fa4ad8d9baae22b8`. JS and Swift independently reproduce both byte strings, hashes, fresh receipts, exact replay, and `OPERATION_REUSED` after changing only each fractional binary64 value to its adjacent representable predecessor/successor. No integer-unit conversion is accepted.

Legacy expert review uses two fenced actions in `taskDisposition.js`. `beginLegacyExpertReview` accepts exact `{action,operationId,taskDocumentId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedTaskInstanceId,message:{messageId,text,timestamp,taskContext?}}`; context is exact `{userTaskId,catalogTaskId,title}`, every ID/title/text is trimmed NFC within 256/4,096 bytes, `userTaskId` equals the target document, and `timestamp` is `CallableTimestampWireV1`. Its transaction requires generic legacy eligibility, parses that semantic message time, and atomically creates absent `supportChat/{messageId}` exact `{text,sender:"user",timestamp,read:false,taskContext?}` plus task `expertReview:{messageId,adminQueued:false}`. It returns the ordinary legacy receipt; then the unchanged `submitSupportMessage` callable is awaited with the exact existing message payload. On confirmed admin-queue success, `markLegacyExpertReviewQueued` accepts exact common expectation plus `messageId`, requires the matching false marker and existing message, and changes only `adminQueued:true`. Both are idempotent operations. This flow is distinct from valid-policy WAIT Chat, which performs zero support/task write.

All other server writers apply the same fence without entering the generic action. A policy-absent H54 request adds required root/source expectation and, whenever applicable, exact replacement expectation; its named reducer validates them before replay or mutation and retains the Phase-1 response shape. Policy-absent guidance/vendor/mini workflow requests add `legacyTaskExpectation` wherever they name/mutate a task and validate it before submission-token replay; target task creates carry current generation and never overwrite a mismatched existing row. Generic spawn uses §4.6. Every date/event/threshold scheduler query captures `{taskDocumentId,taskGenerationEpoch,taskInstanceId}` from the candidate; its transaction rereads root/task and no-ops on mismatch. Reset remains the only authority that deletes arbitrary task generations.

Firestore rules make every task row carrying `task_generation_epoch` owner-read/server-write: direct owner update/delete is denied regardless of requested map. The sole owner-write exception is the §3.1 one-time addition of current epoch to an otherwise byte-identical policy/contract/audit-absent `generatedBy:"packingPlan"` pre-v5 row; no other field may change. Pre-v5 rows with the member absent retain the old contractless owner update/delete rule for already-installed clients, while every v5 caller uses these actions; once reset regenerates the same ID with an epoch, delayed old-client writes are denied. `packingPlan/**` and `readiness/**` become owner-read/server-write, including marker-era transitions. Tests deny owner/other/anonymous direct task, plan, and readiness writes for fenced rows and prove the one exact upgrade.

Client optimistic application/revert/removal/accounting uses one equality predicate over document ID, generation, and explicit optional instance. It governs `TasksStore.performWrite`, inventory-reset optimistic/revert lookup, `PeezyHomeViewModel.focusTask`, `dismissTaskFlow`, every completion/accounting/navigation callback, route acknowledgement, and every bespoke movers/packing callback. A stale response may not remove/reinsert/credit/advance a new task with the same ID; it is ignored and listener state wins. The unused `TaskActionService.setStatus` and `markTaskInProgress` methods are removed. The RED matrix runs every mutation kind and every special action through: epoch-0 success; reset to epoch 1 with the same document ID; delayed epoch-0 request rejection with no task/auxiliary mutation; committed epoch-0 replay returning `STALE_TASK_GENERATION` with no adoptable old result; fresh epoch-1 success; and a late client result that causes no epoch-1 UI, accounting, navigation, or store mutation. It additionally freezes contractless H54 supersede→reopen→COMPLETE/SNOOZE/USER_IN_PROGRESS parity, preserves every reopened audit byte, rejects malformed/live/contract-restoring H54 cycles, and treats both migration `applied` and `already_current` as a completed session guard.

## 5. Outcome, wake, and race semantics

### 5.1 Outcome → milestone → completion

| Visible semantic outcome | Policy effect | Exact milestone/completion result |
|---|---|---|
| Fully handled | `COMPLETE_MILESTONES` | Close all current-profile required milestones named by policy; `Completed` only when none remain open. |
| Got what I needed | policy-specific complete/user/wait | Never label-hardcoded; a receipt/acceptance tail remains when policy requires it. |
| They are handling/processing it | trusted-display `WAIT_EXTERNAL`; otherwise owned USER_ACTION fallback | Close the declared submission milestone and leave acceptance/result open. With trusted policy/provider-directory display, apply WAIT plus the fresh trigger; without it, preserve that same confirmed trigger/evidence in `waiting_fallback_state`, show “You’re handling this,” and never render an opaque owner identifier. |
| I need one more thing / submit something else / need proof | `CONTINUE_USER_ACTION` | Close only named prior milestone; persist a concrete next action and trigger. |
| Sent | policy-specific complete or wait | Complete only when send is final; otherwise preserve receipt/money/acceptance tail. |
| Ready to send later | `CONTINUE_USER_ACTION` | Preserve concrete send action and send-by trigger; never convert to generic defer. |
| Could not finish / did not work | `REQUIRES_RESOLVER` | Zero task/milestone mutation until a later operation submits a valid choice. |
| Try another route | resolver selection → handoff | Resolve/archive the old route and persist only the selected action ID; the later `beginHandoff` obtains the sole newly confirmed trigger and atomically enters USER_ACTION before any CTA. |
| Do this later | `DEFER` | Close no milestone; require a fresh confirmed trigger. |
| Get help / open Chat | navigation only | Preserve current task/handoff; create no support state or SLA claim. |
| WAIT “It happened” | transition-bound outcome | Complete only if every required milestone closes; otherwise enter the policy's next owned state. |
| WAIT first stuck cycle | bound renewal | Same transition may recur once with a fresh trigger and recorded cycle. |
| WAIT second stuck cycle | different channel resolver | The sole receipt choice is the declared `TRY_CHANNEL` handoff with its exact alternate transition and pre-scripted nudge template; preserve WAIT until that new begin, and never offer Chat here or a third identical cycle. |

### 5.2 Scheduler and wake contract

Scheduler state remains `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState`. It contains eight independent cursor maps with exact keys:

- `event_envelope_prepass`: `{afterPath}`;
- `date_snoozed_deferred`, `date_inprogress_user_action`, `date_matching_waiting`: `{afterAt,afterPath}`;
- `event_snoozed_deferred`, `event_inprogress_user_action`, `event_matching_waiting`: `{afterPath}`.
- `threshold_attention`: `{afterAt,afterPath}`.

Every `afterPath` is the complete Firestore document path, not a leaf ID. One immutable `runNow` is captured immediately after lease acquisition. Every run then executes this fixed order: phase 0 event-envelope ingestion; date Snoozed/DEFERRED; date InProgress/USER_ACTION; date matching/WAITING; event Snoozed/DEFERRED; event InProgress/USER_ACTION; event matching/WAITING; threshold attention. Phase 0 builds `collectionGroup("events").where("processingState","==","pending").orderBy(documentId(),"asc")`, applies `startAfter(event_envelope_prepass.afterPath)` when that cursor exists, and then applies `limit(101)`. It selects only the first 100, processes them at maximum concurrency 10, catches every candidate failure independently, and waits for all selected candidates to settle. Under the same live run lease and before scan 1, its checkpoint transaction writes the 100th selected document's full path iff the 101st result exists; otherwise it deletes only `event_envelope_prepass`, including on an empty/end page. End-of-range therefore wraps to the beginning on the next run and eventually sees documents inserted at or before an earlier cursor. A query or cursor-checkpoint failure aborts before scan 1 and releases the lease. A candidate transaction/logging failure leaves that event pending but suppresses neither another candidate nor any of the seven scans. A crash before checkpoint may replay candidates and is safe. All phase-0 writes and later scan transactions use the same `runNow`.

The phase-0 candidate transaction rereads and no-ops unless the source still exists with `processingState == "pending"`. Its reference must be exactly `users/{uid}/events/{event_id}` with nonblank UID. For a valid path it selects and validates the existing H55 semantic envelope: trimmed nonblank `event_id` equal to document ID; trimmed nonblank `event_name`, `canonical_key`, and `source_evidence_id`; safe nonnegative integer `source_version`; valid Firestore Timestamp/Date `observed_at`; literal `effect:"fire"|"retract"`; optional `payload` default `{}` whose recursive values are null/string/Boolean/finite number/Date/Firestore Timestamp/acyclic array/plain nonblank-key map; and `processed === false`. Unrecognized outer producer fields are retained on the source but excluded from the semantic envelope. Deterministic validation uses this closed order and reason-code union: `REFERENCE_INVALID`, `ENVELOPE_INVALID`, `EVENT_ID_INVALID`, `EVENT_NAME_INVALID`, `CANONICAL_KEY_INVALID`, `SOURCE_VERSION_INVALID`, `OBSERVED_AT_INVALID`, `SOURCE_EVIDENCE_ID_INVALID`, `EFFECT_INVALID`, `PAYLOAD_INVALID`, `PROCESSED_INVALID`. A wrong-depth/parent reference is `REFERENCE_INVALID`. Transport, query, transaction, logging, digest-encoder, cursor-checkpoint, and lease failures are infrastructure failures and never increment a deterministic validation count.

For quarantine identity only, `OriginalEventBytesV1` is the UTF-8 JSON bytes of a tagged recursive encoding of the complete decoded Firestore source map after omitting only scheduler-owned `phase0ValidationFailure`: null `['n']`; Boolean `['b',0|1]`; exact string `['s',value]`; number `['d',16 lowercase big-endian IEEE-754 hex digits]`; Date/Timestamp `['t',decimal seconds,nanoseconds]`; bytes `['y',base64]`; GeoPoint `['g',latitude-double-hex,longitude-double-hex]`; document reference `['r',fully-qualified database and document path]`; vector `['v',[double-hex...]]`; array `['a',[encoded values...]]`; and map `['m',[[exact key,encoded value]...]]` with keys sorted by unsigned UTF-8 bytes. JSON has no whitespace or trailing LF and performs no trim or Unicode normalization. This is the complete domain; an unrecognized runtime type, nonfinite number, invalid reference, or cycle is an infrastructure failure. `originalBytesDigest` is the full lowercase SHA-256 of these bytes; it is neither SDK/wire serialization nor `EventEnvelopeCanonicalV1`.

A deterministic failure maintains the exact source map `phase0ValidationFailure:{schemaVersion:1,originalBytesDigest,reasonCode,failureCount,firstFailedAt,lastFailedAt}`. `failureCount` is only `1` or `2`. An absent/malformed map or changed `(originalBytesDigest,reasonCode)` starts at `1`; an exact match increments. Counts one and two leave the source pending and never change high-water.
