On the third identical failure the transaction creates or requires byte-identical server-only `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/quarantinedEvents/{quarantineId}`, where `quarantineId = "qev1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({domain:"quarantined_event.v1",source_path,original_bytes_digest})))`. The exact record is `{schemaVersion:1,sourcePath,originalBytesDigest,reason:{code,message},failureCount:3,firstFailedAt,lastFailedAt:runNow,quarantinedAt:runNow}`. `message` is the fixed validator message, contains no raw value/payload, is UTF-8-safe truncated to 1,000 bytes, and the complete record is at most 16,384 `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes; no raw envelope bytes are copied. In the same transaction the source becomes `{processingState:"terminal",processed:true,processedAt:runNow,outcome:"quarantined",processingError:reason.message}` while retaining unrelated producer fields and deleting `phase0ValidationFailure`; high-water is untouched. An exact preexisting quarantine record is idempotent; an ID/content mismatch fails closed. A later-valid corrected source deletes any retry map in its ordinary terminal/high-water transaction. Phase 2 has no quarantine TTL, deletion, reader, alert, export, admin surface, cleanup, or named operational consumer; the post-Phase-2 regeneration records that consumer as H57 `UNKNOWN` without weakening the supported producer contract.

`EventEnvelopeCanonicalV1` preserves current H55 bytes rather than substituting policy canonicalization: construct exact ordered semantic map `{event_id,event_name,canonical_key,source_version,observed_at,source_evidence_id,effect,payload}`, recursively preserve array order, sort plain-map keys with JavaScript's default UTF-16 code-unit order, convert valid Date/Timestamp values to `toISOString()`, emit finite numbers with `JSON.stringify`, and emit no whitespace/LF. `fingerprint` is the full 64-character lowercase SHA-256 of those bytes. The event-state document ID is the full lowercase SHA-256 of the same canonicalization of `[event_name,canonical_key]`, with no prefix, at `users/{uid}/eventState/{id}`.

The transaction rereads that high-water row and classifies exactly: missing or malformed `source_version`, or a greater source version, is `advance`; a lower version is `stale`; an equal version plus identical fingerprint is `duplicate`; equal version plus different fingerprint is `version_conflict`. Only `advance` replaces the high-water map with exact `{event_name,canonical_key,source_version,effect,event_id,observed_at,source_evidence_id,payload,fingerprint,advancedAt:runNow}`. Every valid classification terminalizes the event with `{processingState:"terminal",processed:true,processedAt:runNow,outcome}` and deletes any `phase0ValidationFailure`; stale/duplicate/conflict leave high water unchanged. Thus all seven same-run event scans observe phase-0 advancement/retraction, and a version conflict cannot silently alter wake authority.

Each of the seven scan queries has literal `limit(1)`; a candidate transaction rereads only root/state plus that one potentially 655,360-byte task, so no page materializes hundreds of maximum-size documents. Each of the six firing scans is bound to one exact status/disposition pair. Its Firestore query filters the pair's status, trigger kind, and `fired == false`; the transaction rechecks the matching disposition because status already determines the only coherent Phase 2 disposition and the authorized index shape deliberately omits that redundant equality field. A date query additionally applies `where("dispositionContract.next_trigger.at", "<=", runNow)`, orders by trigger time ascending and then document ID ascending, and uses its `{afterAt,afterPath}` cursor with `startAfter`. An event query orders by document ID and uses only `{afterPath}`. The authorized composites are status + `dispositionContract.next_trigger.kind` + `dispositionContract.next_trigger.fired` + `dispositionContract.next_trigger.at`, and the same without `at` for event scans. Maximum-task, query-retry, cursor-loss, same-run high-water visibility, and complete phase-0-plus-seven-scan passes are mandatory fixtures. The mandatory poison fixture creates 100 lexically earlier deterministic-invalid pending sources and valid source 101. Run 1 counts each poison to one, checkpoints poison 100, and still runs all seven scans; run 2 starts after it, consumes source 101, deletes the prepass cursor at end, and exposes its high-water to that run's event scan; run 3 wraps and counts poisons to two; run 4 atomically writes 100 bounded quarantine records and terminalizes those sources. Every run executes all seven scans. The fixture also freezes full-path cursor/startAfter/checkpoint/delete/crash/wrap/insertion behavior, failure `1→2→quarantine`, changed bytes/reason reset, `OriginalEventBytesV1` scalar/type corpus, record ID/cap/collision, client denial, wrong-depth, duplicate/stale/conflict, and zero poison high-water.

The transaction first branches on immutable-policy presence. A genuinely absent `taskInteraction` is eligible only in the Snoozed/DEFERRED date or event scan and executes the byte-compatible H55 reducer: reread the current legacy trigger and due/high-water condition, write only `status:"Upcoming"`, `dispositionContract:buildUpcomingContract(previous,"Ready to continue")`, and delete `snoozedUntil`. It creates no policy state, epoch/revision, wake evidence, history, or intent. Policy-absent tasks in the USER_ACTION/WAITING scans no-op. A present malformed policy no-ops in every scan. A valid policy takes only the v5 branch below. This tri-state decision uses key presence, never truthiness or failed decoding.

Before a valid-policy mutation, every date-task transaction rereads the task and requires the exact status/disposition pair, structurally valid pinned policy, exact canonical date trigger with `fired == false`, and `at <= runNow`; a future, replaced, ATTENTION_NOW, malformed, or already-fired value is a no-op. Every valid-policy event transaction makes the analogous reread, then resolves the exact server-owned event tuple and requires high-water `source_version > after_source_version` with effect `fire`. It never trusts a query snapshot after the transaction begins. Only the scan's own cursor changes. If a page has overflow, it advances to the last processed query document; when that status/kind reaches its end—including an empty page—it deletes only that cursor so the next run restarts from the beginning. Thus a future date is not queried, cursor-skipped, or woken early, one status/kind cannot poison another cursor, and policy rollout cannot strand an existing legacy snooze.

The seventh scan is the independent first-producer and escalation producer for urgent recovery. Its indexed query is exactly `where("thresholdProjection.state","==","armed")`, `where("thresholdProjection.threshold_at","<=",runNow)`, `orderBy("thresholdProjection.threshold_at","asc")`, `orderBy(documentId(),"asc")`, `startAfter(afterAt,afterPath)` when a cursor exists, and `limit(1)`. The authorized composite is `thresholdProjection.state + thresholdProjection.threshold_at + document ID`; no `wakeEvidence` predicate is used. The transaction rereads the task/root/source and requires U/A/W/D, a valid current policy/instance, exact armed projection, matching single-outcome deadline evidence and client-denied authority source version, and `threshold_at <= runNow`. Any mismatch/no longer eligible task removes or refreshes the stale projection only through its owning server reducer; the scheduler itself no-ops on unverifiable authority.

At a valid threshold, the transaction behaves as one total branch. If the same current instance already has a coherent normal TRIGGER-cause wake, it changes that wake to `urgent_recovery`, writes the exact basis including optional class, retains cause/intent, marks the projection `attended`, increments revision, and appends the deterministic `URGENCY_ESCALATION` row; it does not recreate/consume/update the intent. If a coherent urgent wake already exists, exact retry only marks an identical projection attended and returns it. Otherwise it creates one threshold-cause urgent wake plus one intent: U remains Upcoming and routes `row`; D clears its active trigger, becomes Upcoming, and routes `row`; A/W retain their disposition, move any active unfired date/event trigger into `cause.displaced_trigger`, replace it with ATTENTION_NOW, and route by the ordinary returned-handoff/WAIT/row precedence. The new wake/intent and `thresholdProjection.state:"attended"` commit atomically. A malformed, different-instance, or incompatible existing wake is fail-closed and not overwritten.

The ordinary trigger-firing transaction participates in the same race rule. After its rereads, if the exact armed projection is already due at `runNow`, it emits the urgent TRIGGER-cause wake/basis and marks the projection attended in that transaction. If the threshold transaction commits first, an ordinary date/event scan sees ATTENTION_NOW/no active trigger or an Upcoming task and no-ops. If ordinary commits first as normal, the threshold transaction upgrades it. Therefore every serialization produces one wake, one intent, one attended projection, and the same urgent visible result; `cause.kind` records which authority won without changing semantics. Claim validation treats the scheduler-only revision as diagnostic and validates live instance/epoch/fingerprint/wake cause/state/session, so escalation does not stale a pending or already-claimed route. Cursor advance/reset follows the six-scan rule using threshold time/path. Fixtures cover first observation already past threshold with no prior wake; active trigger later than threshold; U/D/A/W; returned handoff and direct WAIT routes; ordinary-before-threshold and threshold-before-ordinary transactions; equal-time races; normal→urgent before/after claim with pending/consumed/cancelled intent; displaced-trigger retention; exact IDs/replay; class drift; and a fresh disposition clearing/rearming before threshold.

Wake ID is `w1_` plus the first 40 lowercase hex characters of SHA-256 over canonical `{uid,task_document_id,task_instance_id,interaction_epoch,policy_fingerprint,cause}`. Intent ID is `ni1_` plus the first 40 over canonical `{uid,task_document_id,task_instance_id,interaction_epoch,policy_fingerprint,route,cause:{wake_evidence_id:wake_id}}`. Transaction retry produces the same IDs; reset/regeneration cannot collide even when every other byte recurs.

- `Snoozed + DEFERRED`: move the entire fired trigger to wake evidence, clear active trigger, reactivate to `Upcoming`, and create one row intent.
- `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED` and `matching_in_progress + WAITING_ON_EXTERNAL`: retain disposition; move the fired trigger to wake evidence/history; replace active trigger with server-only `{schema_version:1,kind:"ATTENTION_NOW",wake_id}`; create one row or outcome intent. A display-fallback USER_ACTION retains its exact `waiting_fallback_state` and always chooses row.
- `ATTENTION_NOW` is valid only as a server-written pointer to existing wake evidence. A client cannot submit it. The next fresh disposition requires a new date/event trigger.
- At the firing transaction, compare the exact current `thresholdProjection`. If it is armed/due, write `urgent_recovery` plus its exact basis/class and mark it attended; otherwise write `normal` with no basis. The seventh scan independently creates or upgrades attention. Missing/malformed/stale evidence always yields normal trigger attention and is never inferred as urgent.
- A fresh successful command cancels obsolete pending intents and clears wake pointers. Failed trigger replacement preserves both.

Intent route selection is deterministic at the wake transaction and applies this precedence. First, an exact external H54 `planChangeState == "pending_confirmation"` task with a coherent PC cycle, replacement link, current confirmation ordinal/source, and retained confirmation snapshot stores `row`; claim revalidates those same PC identities and the row opens only the “Finish plan update” recovery surface. It never opens a waiting outcome, even if the task status is WAITING or a returned handoff snapshot remains. Otherwise DEFERRED stores `row`; a USER_ACTION carrying `waiting_fallback_state` stores `row` even if stale handoff history remains; USER_ACTION/WAITING with a matching `activeHandoff.state == returned` stores `outcome` plus that session ID; WAITING without a returned handoff stores `outcome` without a session and opens the current transition's outcome surface; every other USER_ACTION case stores `row`. The intent copies task instance/epoch/revision/fingerprint after the wake. Claim rejects if that instance/epoch/fingerprint, optional returned session, wake cause, state, expiry, or PC linkage no longer matches; it never silently downgrades an invalid outcome route to another surface. Tests cover fallback row precedence, PC precedence from WAITING with and without returned handoff, claim after PC→CF/undo/reset, and exact recovery presentation after relaunch.

`TasksStore` derives one shared urgent-recovery projection consumed byte-identically by Home and Tasks. A line is eligible only when the mapped policy is valid, `wakeEvidence.urgency == "urgent_recovery"`, its `urgency_basis` resolves byte-for-byte to retained current deadline evidence, policy threshold, optional consequence class/rank, and attended threshold projection, the task instance matches, and the live state is either A/W with ATTENTION_NOW pointing at that wake or Upcoming with the exact threshold/DEFER wake history. The exact comparison key is classified `(0,consequence_rank,threshold_at,threshold_id,task_document_id)` or unclassified `(1,threshold_at,threshold_id,task_document_id)`; unsigned UTF-8 order breaks string ties. Because the Phase 2 production registry is `{}`, all production lines use deadline order until one reviewed Phase 3 source+policy change activates classes. No catalog percentage/label invents a rank. Zero lines hides the group; one line uses the same group header; multiple lines render one “Needs attention now” group with one line per task. Each line shows the policy threshold label/protected outcome and current owner/action, and routes independently using the same PC/returned-WAIT/row precedence above. There is no group-level dismiss, completion, claim, or write; opening/acknowledging one line changes only that task/intent, and the listener recomputes the group. Malformed/stale evidence/class/rank is excluded rather than downgraded. Tests cover empty production registry, injected two-rank mixed classified/unclassified ordering, equal ranks/times/IDs, zero/one/many, Home/Tasks parity, PC precedence, U/A/W/D line routes, one-line mutation leaving siblings, and stale evidence removal.

Rules deny every client read/write of notification intents. `claimTaskIntent` is the only client path. Rules separately deny owner/other/anonymous `get`, `list`, `create`, `update`, and `delete` at `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/quarantinedEvents/{quarantineId}`; the recursive system deny may implement it, but rules tests must name this literal path and all five operations. There is no sender, FCM call, collection query from Swift, delivery state, production delivery claim, or Phase-2 quarantine consumer.

### 5.3 Mandatory server concurrency results

| Race | Required result |
|---|---|
| two fresh commands/begins/resolutions | one commits; the other sees stale revision/session and writes nothing |
| scheduler first vs fresh command | fresh request rejects stale revision; caller refreshes |
| fresh command first vs scheduler | scheduler transaction rechecks and no-ops or evaluates the replacement future trigger |
| scheduler vs handoff acknowledge/return | scheduler may advance revision but not epoch; matching session follow-up succeeds and preserves wake evidence |
| fresh/H54 first vs return | session is cancelled/rebased; stale return rejects |
| return first vs stale fresh request | return commits; stale expected revision request rejects |
| cancel vs acknowledge | an admissible pre-open cancel first invalidates ack; ack first reaches `opened`, where only matching SHARE presenter cancellation or the two auth/takeover exceptions can cancel |
| resolve vs scheduler | scheduler first cannot strand session; resolve cleans obsolete wake; resolve first makes scheduler recheck new state |
| reset marker vs mutation | reset first makes semantic/scheduler write reject/no-op; mutation first is subsequently deleted by reset |
| transaction retry after concurrent write | validator reruns; op record and task mutation remain atomic |
| policy replay after time passes/new revision | exact matching policy operation receipt returns before mutable-state/time validation; client still requires receipt account/instance/generation |
| legacy/bulk-packing replay after reset/root or target identity change | retained operation remains audit-only; server returns `STALE_TASK_GENERATION` without exposing the old result/path arrays |

### 5.4 Complete handoff and local reconciliation table

The normative sequence is local immutable preparation → exact flow-tail lease when applicable → awaited `beginHandoff` → server `open_requested` → opener/presenter result → acknowledge or cancel → kind-specific return → outcome question → awaited resolution.

| Case | Exact behavior |
|---|---|
| invalid/malformed policy or stale draft epoch/revision/fingerprint | no local preparation, callable, or opener |
| deterministic begin failure | no open; stale/auth/policy/not-found discards prepared operation after refresh; editable input/trigger/reset retains only draft data and a retry uses a new operation ID |
| ambiguous begin transport | retry identical operation/payload; changed payload fails; a user cancel first resolves the begin receipt, then cancels the committed session or discards a deterministic no-commit |
| concurrent begin | same op replays and opener runs once locally; different live op/session rejects |
| `open_requested`, crash before opener/local lost | reconstruct redacted snapshot and show Continue/Cancel; explicit Continue revalidates the same server session then invokes the opener without another state mutation, while Cancel calls `cancelHandoff`; never auto-open or infer action |
| local prepared/server absent | discard local |
| server active/local absent | reconstruct only after UID/auth epoch/policy/session checks |
| mismatch or already resolved/cancelled | fail closed, clear presentation/local state, never re-present |
| external CALL/WEB/PORTAL/MAP accepted | acknowledge; observed inactive/background→active after acceptance records return, and an explicit user-confirmed return remains available when no qualifying cycle arrives |
| external opener rejects | send `OPENER_REJECTED` plus matching `EXTERNAL_OPEN_REJECTED`, await cancel and exact restore; ambiguous cancel retries identical bytes |
| in-app WEB/PORTAL | presentation success acknowledges; explicit dismissal returns; pre-ack presentation failure sends `PRESENTATION_FAILED` plus matching controller evidence |
| SHARE | explicit presenter success acknowledges; completion/dismissal returns; pre-ack failure uses `PRESENTATION_FAILED`; a user cancellation after acknowledgement uses `PRESENTER_CANCELLED` and the narrow opened-state exception |
| any persisted `opened` without adapter return | show explicit “I’m back from [action]”; its tap creates `USER_CONFIRMED_RETURN`; ordinary activation remains invalid and the tap asserts no outcome |
| scheduler revision drift | keep matching epoch/session/fingerprint; epoch/session/fingerprint drift reconciles or cancels, never opens |
| wake fires during session | session survives; cancellation with fired fallback requires a new trigger and preserves attention until success |
| H54 | cancel session/intents; undo restores only a fresh-session/new-epoch `prepared` state |
| sign-out/account switch | clear presentation and purge namespace; observed sign-out→same UID rotates auth epoch; same-install mismatch auto-cancels before reconstruction; another installation requires explicit takeover and never auto-opens/cancels |
| `REQUIRES_RESOLVER` | receipt-backed resolver, zero mutation, handoff remains returned; a new op with valid choice resolves |
| flow-origin handoff | no begin/open until exact attempt tail is drained and persisted; failure means no begin |

### 5.5 H56 persistence, full clear, and scene lease

The H56 replacement prevents ABA after `clear A → clear B → delayed initialization A`. A policy-bearing task has immutable root `task_instance_id`; live flow fields are `stage`, `flowPath`, `flowAnswers`, `flowAnswerIdentities`, `flowAttemptId`, `flowAttemptGeneration`, and `flowWriteRevision`; retained fence fields are `lastClearedFlowAttemptId`, `lastClearedFlowAttemptGeneration`, and `flowWriteRevision`. `stage` is one of `notStarted/capture/measure/scope/price/compare/book/verify/complete`. `flowWriteRevision` is a safe nonnegative integer that never resets during a task instance.

Mode-neutral UI constructs only `FlowProgressDraft = {stage?,path,answers,answerIdentities}`. The task-bound coordinator combines a draft with mapped policy state and produces the exact persisted union `FlowProgressSnapshot = legacy({stage?,path,answers,answerIdentities,flowAttemptId?}) | policy({taskInstanceId,stage,path,answers,answerIdentities,flowAttemptId?,flowAttemptGeneration?,flowWriteRevision,persistedTailFingerprint?})`. `legacy` is constructible only for `absentLegacy` and serializes the current five field names/omissions byte-equivalently; `policy` is constructible only for valid policy; malformed-present has no writable case. In the policy case, absent stage at revision 0/no attempt maps only to `notStarted`; first initialization stores it. Attempt ID and generation are both present or both absent. Path has at most 256 step IDs of 1–128 UTF-8 bytes. Answers has at most 256 step-ID keys, each mapping to at most 32 NFC strings of at most 1,024 bytes. Identities has at most 256 matching keys and exact values `{id,label,source}` with ID 1–256 bytes, label 1–512 bytes, and source `mapkit` or `manual`. Attempt ID follows §4.1; generation is a safe positive integer. The canonical live tail is the exact eight-member map `{task_instance_id,stage,flowPath,flowAnswers,flowAnswerIdentities,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration,flowWriteRevision}`, capped at 131,072 bytes. Its local/command fingerprint is `fp2_` plus lowercase SHA-256 and is never stored inside answers.

Each presented task attempt has exactly one `FlowExitCoordinator` writer shared by that task's flow view/model/coordinator; transactions arbitrate globally across scenes/devices. It serializes stage, path, answers, and identities in one ordered persistence queue; no valid-policy caller performs a detached `setStage` or direct flow-field update. Attempt initialization is a transaction requiring the expected task instance, no live attempt/tail, no locked handoff, and captured current revision `r` (0 initially and retained above 0 after prior clears); it writes a fresh attempt ID, generation `(lastClearedFlowAttemptGeneration ?? 0) + 1`, the complete queued snapshot including stage, and revision `r+1`. Continuation requires exact task instance, attempt ID, generation, and current expected revision and writes the complete queued snapshot with revision+1. Each queued write binds its expected revision only when its predecessor has conclusively committed or reconciled. A transaction retry reruns all comparisons.

The exact awaited stage API is `persistStageAndWait(_ stage: TaskStage, merging draft: FlowProgressDraft) async throws`. It replaces only the draft stage, preserves/merges the coordinator's current full path/answers/identities, enqueues that complete snapshot, and returns only after commit or exact ambiguous reconciliation. Generic FlowEngine enqueues stage with its navigation draft. Movers policy mode injects this same API into `MoversFlowViewModel`; its quote/matrix UI advances only after success. The view model owns `pendingStageRetry: TaskStage?` and exact `retryPendingStage() async`: after quote deletion commits, a fallback-stage failure stores `.compare`, leaves local `stage`/`persistedResumeStage` at matrix, and FindMoversFlow renders Retry; retry sends only the current complete stage draft and clears pending state only on success. It never uses `try?` or repeats quote deletion. The legacy movers persister remains callable only under `absentLegacy`.

On ambiguous initialization/continuation transport, the coordinator reads once. It accepts commit only when task instance, attempt ID, generation, complete submitted stage/path/answers/identities, and revision `r+1` all match; it then advances the queue. Any other state preserves the conflict/error and blocks later writes until reload—never blind retry with a new revision. Policy-absent flows retain their current five-field writer bytes; malformed-present policy is passive. RED covers commit/lost-response for init and continuation, mismatch after ambiguity, and two queued writes whose second binds only after the first reconciles.

The five literal policy actions in §4.2 are routed through the existing authenticated `changeTaskPlan` export and implemented in `taskDisposition.js`; Firestore rules deny every direct client mutation of those fields when `taskInteraction` is present. The callable uses the exact closed requests/responses/revisions there, the bounds above, one shared JS validator, and an Admin transaction. Initialization/continuation require a complete valid post-state, exact revision+1, immutable task instance, no server-owned field change, and no `activeHandoff` in `prepared/open_requested/opened/returned`. They are legal only for `Upcoming` or coherent `InProgress + USER_ACTION_TRACKED` with H54 absent/null/reopened, plus the exact linked PA replacement exception; Completed, Dismissed, WAITING, DEFERRED, original PA/PC, replacement PC, CF, retired, reset-active, and malformed projections deny. Initialization enforces the generation formula, valid nonblank ID, and ID inequality to the immediately last-cleared ID; the client generates its UUID once before dispatch and durably retains identical request bytes on ambiguity. The server does not claim global UUID freshness: `(task_instance_id,generation,flowAttemptId)` is the authority, so even a reused older ID at a later valid generation cannot revive old proof. Continuation requires exact live ID/generation retention. An old client omitting revision/generation, a partial/invalid state, mixed content request, terminal/H54-state request, or cross-device request against a stale revision is denied. Reset/regeneration changes task instance, so every old policy writer is denied even if document ID and flow bytes recur.

`TaskActionService.clearFlowState(expected:)` sends the exact drained snapshot to that callable, whose transaction requires task instance, attempt ID, generation, revision, stage, and recomputed fingerprint. It retains stage byte-equivalent; deletes exactly `flowPath`, `flowAnswers`, `flowAnswerIdentities`, `flowAttemptId`, and `flowAttemptGeneration`; writes `lastClearedFlowAttemptId` and `lastClearedFlowAttemptGeneration` from the cleared attempt; and increments `flowWriteRevision` exactly once. The server clear action requires precisely that pre/post relation and the same lifecycle predicate as initialization/continuation: no `activeHandoff` in `prepared/open_requested/opened/returned`, only Upcoming or coherent USER_ACTION with H54 absent/null/reopened or the exact PA replacement exception, and denial for terminal, WAITING, DEFERRED, original PA/PC, replacement PC, CF, retired, reset-active, or malformed state. Thus an owner client cannot clear a tail held by a live handoff; only the server outcome/cancel reducer may clear/release it atomically. On ambiguous transport, one read treats the clear as committed only when the five live members are absent, stage is byte-equivalent, task instance is unchanged, the two last-cleared values match, and revision is expected+1. Otherwise the original error and terminal lock remain. Because a new attempt's generation must be the retained generation+1, delayed A initialization/writes/clears remain stale after any B clear; generation and task instance close both same-document and reset ABA. Callable/rules tests include direct-write denial, begin/open_requested→clear denial, returned→clear denial, cancel→eligible clear, and server resolve+clear.

Task-detail policy content uses the two disjoint actions/revisions in §4.2. `notes` is at most 20,000 UTF-8 bytes. `quotes` has at most 50 exact maps `{company,notes,crew?,hours?,perManRate?,travelFee?}`: company 1–256 bytes, notes 0–2,000, crew integer 1–100, hours 0–1,000, per-man rate/travel fee 0–1,000,000. The server validates every member, `TaskCanonicalV1` content hash, revision CAS, and complete prospective task size; mixed content+flow or content+server-state requests are denied, and rules deny direct policy-content writes. Policy-absent legacy content uses §4.7's generation-fenced legacy mutation callable rather than an unfenced direct write, while preserving the legacy visible result/callback semantics.

The handoff predrain uses a transient exact-attempt lease, separate from the terminal barrier. Acquisition synchronously locks submission/exit before tail capture, rejects new local progress, drains and proves the exact persisted attempt, releases on predrain/begin failure, remains held through a live handoff, and releases exactly once after cancel or a nonterminal resolution. The begin transaction stores the proof in `activeHandoff` and creates the rules-visible cross-device lock above. Only the matching lease token/session can release. A held-and-drained lease is not scene-flush work.

All non-handoff mutating exits use `FlowExitCoordinator.acquireTerminalLease(kind:operationId:)`. A restoration preflight is read-only, has no operation ID, never acquires/consumes either flow lease, never drains or clears a tail, and cannot block progress; only after every required descriptor has been resolved does the client acquire the terminal lease and build the mutating request/proofs from the then-current task snapshot. Mutation-lease acquisition is synchronous before its first await, blocks new queue entries, drains predecessors, captures the exact persisted proof, and returns one token plus immutable request builder. Direct dispositions, applicable H54 mutations, and workflow dispatch hold it through deterministic or ambiguous completion. Ambiguous transport retains token/request and retries identical bytes; a semantic commit consumes the token after receipt reconciliation because the server cleared the tail. Every deterministic no-commit/error releases without clear, including NOT_APPLICABLE evaluator `present` resolver and `unknown` failure. Cancellation before dispatch releases. Handoff begin uses its distinct longer-lived lease; a REQUIRES_RESOLVER/OPEN_CHAT handoff result retains that handoff lease only. No token may unlock another task/attempt. Tests cover preflight concurrent progress and zero lease/clear, present/unknown, validation error, cancellation, ambiguous retry, concurrent queued progress, semantic commit, and wrong-token release for direct/H54/workflow paths.

Every semantic command or policy-bearing producer whose transaction exits a live flow derives proof requirement from the stored tail, validates the exact eight-field proof, and performs the same five-deletion/last-cleared/revision+1 operation atomically with its effect. This includes direct COMPLETE/NOT_APPLICABLE/DEFER/SELF_HANDLE/WAIT, `resolveHandoff`, `resolveWaiting`, applicable H54 supersede/confirm/undo/reopen task transitions, and workflow COMPLETE/WAIT. Reset/finalize delete tasks through their existing lease/page protocol and neither accept a proof nor perform a per-tail clear. A clearing receipt includes `flowClearedAttemptId`; the client never sends a second clear. `beginHandoff` validates and stores the proof but deliberately retains the locked tail until resolution/cancel. REQUIRES_RESOLVER and OPEN_CHAT zero-mutation responses neither exit nor clear and keep the lease/session recoverable. There is no Phase-2 production valid-policy caller of client-owned `clearFlowState(expected:)`; it remains only as the rules-tested policy clear seam and for the byte-compatible absent-legacy paths already using it.

The scene background path captures only genuine queue-backed pending tail while both submission and terminal locks are clear, starts an injected background lease before its first await, and ends it exactly once on completion or expiry. No lease is requested for no work, a held handoff lease, barriers, or an external-pending marker. Expiry cancels only the waiter/lease, never claims OS-level zero loss; the queue remains owned by the next barrier.

RED cases are: legacy byte compatibility; policy initialization; complete queued stage/path/answer write; legal stage+revision transition over a complete retained tail; same-attempt continuation; concurrent revision loss; stage without revision, partial-invalid post-state, and old-client denial; terminal/original-PA/PC/replacement-PC/retired/reset lifecycle denial; exact tail drain/fingerprint; exact full clear; partial/extra/mismatch denial; same-attempt cross-device write after drain causes mismatch and no clear; clear retry and commit-then-lost-response reconciliation; clear A→init/clear B→delayed A initialization/write/clear denial; reset/regenerate same document→old-instance denial; content bounds and mixed mutation denial; predrain/begin failure release; cross-device write denied after live handoff begin; proof mismatch on concurrent pre-begin write; wrong-token unlock; cancel/nonterminal release; atomic clear for every exiting command/producer; process death after effect commit before receipt; resolver zero-mutation retention; and every scene no-work/barrier/external/expiry/failure branch.

### 5.6 Research posture contract

Legacy section items remain strings. Every newly generated item is exact `{text,posture:"LABELED",source_refs:[],applicability_predicate_ids:[],applicability_evidence_ids:[],verified_as_of:null}`. Text is trimmed NFC, 1–4,096 UTF-8 bytes. All four tail members are literals: the current model response supplies only a brief-level source array and no claim-to-source association, so Phase 2 never invents positional or all-sources attribution. Model output can never emit either verified posture; a purported verified value is normalized to LABELED only on the server generation boundary, and model locators/digests are discarded.

A newly generated source is exact `{source_id,title,publisher,url}`. Title is trimmed NFC 1–512 UTF-8 bytes, publisher 1–256, and guarded URL 1–4,096. `source_id = "rs1_" + first40(SHA-256(canonicalJSONString(url)))`. After URL guarding, duplicate exact URLs collapse; the first guarded occurrence wins title, publisher, and declared order. IDs therefore survive source reordering. A rejected URL removes only its source; new items retain literal empty references. A web brief with no surviving source is marked degraded exactly as the existing regeneration boundary; no source removal upgrades or verifies an item.

Non-force legacy ready-cache hits return byte-equivalent string items and legacy source maps `{title,publisher,url}` with no write. The Swift decoder accepts each item independently as a legacy string or the exact new map, and each source independently as a legacy map or the exact new map. A malformed new item, nonempty source/applicability array, nonnull verification date, purported verified posture, malformed source, duplicate new source ID, or new-source ID/URL mismatch skips only that map and records a local diagnostic; it never relabels it or poisons safe siblings. The rendered source list is explicitly brief-level. Tests freeze legacy cache bytes, mixed item/source arrays, empty new refs, stable IDs, duplicate first-winner, guard removal/degraded behavior, malformed item-local/source-local failure, and the absence of any generated verified posture.

### 5.7 Client policy gate and legacy preservation

`TasksStoreNamespace` is exact `{uid,listenerToken}` with opaque authenticated UID and a fresh lowercase UUID-v4 token on **every** `TasksStore.start(for:)`, including stop/start or same-UID restart. Store authority is exact `{namespace,readiness:"loading"|"ready"|"failed",snapshotRevision,tasks,error?}`: revision is a safe nonnegative counter scoped to that namespace, `error` is present exactly for failed, and tasks are publishable only for ready. Start first invalidates the prior token, removes its listener, and clears its values; it then installs the new loading namespace/listener. Both snapshot-success and error callbacks capture UID+token and compare exact equality with the current namespace immediately before **each** tasks/error/readiness/revision mutation; mismatch returns without any mutation. Stop invalidates/clears current namespace before listener removal and value clearing, so an in-flight callback cannot republish. Draft keys, route presentation, Home/Tasks grouping, and `PeezyMainContainer` consume readiness/revision only with the matching current auth UID+token. Tests deliver late A success/error after B starts, callbacks between invalidation/removal/clear, same-UID token reuse attempts, stop/start, listener cancellation, ready→error, route/draft gating, and revision reset.

`AppRootView` observes `authViewModel.currentUser?.uid` directly; `isAuthenticated` is never the identity guard because A→B remains true. Every UID change/sign-out first generates or invalidates a local `UserLoadToken` UUID, clears stale assessment/UserState, and captures `{uid,loadToken}` for the new load. The assessment fetch checks both values on every success/error/empty branch before continuing. `UserState.load` receives the captured UID, and its completion checks current UID+token again before publishing either value or error. `PeezyMainContainer` may start TasksStore only when published UserState's UID, current auth UID, and captured load token all match; otherwise it stops the store and renders loading/signed-out state. Tests hold A's assessment and UserState completions until after B authentication, reverse their order, cover A error/empty after B, same-UID reload token change, sign-out during each await, and prove no A state/store listener is published under B.

Every task mutation mount first switches on the mapper's tri-state and never on decode truthiness. A §4.7 `PLAIN` or `H54_REOPENED` `absentLegacy` preserves the pre-Phase-2 visible callback, payload meaning, field relation, and success/failure behavior while routing its task write through the generation/string-or-null-instance-fenced action; it performs no direct task/packing/readiness write. A contract-bearing or other audit-bearing `absentLegacy` exposes only its named Phase-1 server-owned contract/H54 surface and keeps unrelated generic task-detail/row mutation controls passive. The `H54_REOPENED` exception is deliberately active because the baseline restores a contractless row and its generic controls; its retained audit is validated and preserved byte-for-byte. `malformedPresent` renders the task read-only with one local diagnostic and no callable, direct Firestore writer, opener, status mutation, flow completion callback, or legacy fallback. `valid` exposes only capabilities/bindings from the pinned policy through `TaskDispositionSurface`, a tracked §2.2 handoff adapter, or §4.5's workflow producer; generic Done/Complete/Snooze/Dismiss buttons and direct `TaskActionService` status writes are absent.

This switch applies at shared Home/Tasks rows, task detail/content, generic FlowEngine completion/exit, both insurance flows, FindCleaners/SellItems/RemoveItems, RentTruck/SetupInternet, the policy-ineligible Supplies gate above, and every clean S6 adapter. A valid workflow COMPLETE/WAIT consumes only the typed already-persisted receipt; a valid flow-exiting disposition requires the exact proof and server-atomic clear. A valid policy with zero/mismatched capability, handoff key/kind/mode, producer binding, trigger, or profile renders that particular control passive rather than borrowing a legacy callback. Tests freeze legacy snapshots/callback bytes, malformed no-call behavior, every capability/binding, all eligible named callers' COMPLETE/WAIT/no-second-write paths, and TaskDetail/FlowEngine/insurance/Supplies embedded-control gating in `TaskDispositionSurfaceTests`, `TaskRowLegacySnapshotTests`, `ConversationFlowTests`, `DispositionContractTests`, and `TaskPlanDispositionTests`.

`MoversFlowViewModel.submitExpertReview` follows the same tri-state. Eligible `absentLegacy` preserves the visible message/marker/admin-queue lifecycle through §4.7's `beginLegacyExpertReview` and `markLegacyExpertReviewQueued`; `SupportChatService.swift` owns those fenced calls and never directly updates the task. `valid` never calls either task-mutating action and supplies no task marker; it emits only the existing local Chat navigation intent with bounded display context, preserving the task/handoff/disposition and making no task, support-thread, message, or outbox write. `malformedPresent` and contracted/audit-bearing legacy outside its named reducer are passive. `MoversFlowViewModel.swift`, `SupportChatService.swift`, `taskDisposition.js`, and rules own this integration; the protected Inventory caller remains untouched. Tests spy navigation, both fenced actions, task/support writers, and submitSupportMessage for all branches and prove valid-policy “Get help” cannot create `expertReview`, `SUPPORT_ACTIVE`, a thread/message, or an SLA claim.

`TaskFlowRouter` applies the tri-state before constructing any bespoke destination. Eligible `absentLegacy` preserves every existing route/callback while the authorized booking/readiness/packing destinations use §4.7's fenced actions. `valid` may instantiate only the explicitly integrated Phase-2 destinations named in §8 and otherwise routes back to the shared policy surface with the unsupported embedded control passive; `malformedPresent` is read-only. In particular it never mounts `BookYourMoversView` for a present policy. The same fail-closed rule covers every unlisted bespoke router branch, including future catalog activation; absence of active policy data is not a safety assumption. `TaskFlowRouter.swift` owns the gate; the three legacy destination files are authorized only for generation-fence plumbing. `TaskDispositionSurfaceTests`/`ConversationFlowTests` enumerate every current router destination and prove eligible legacy callback parity, contracted-legacy passivity outside named reducers, and zero present-policy direct status/snooze/complete writes.

## 6. Field and evidence traceability matrix

## 7. Firestore rules budget and exact fixtures

### 7.1 Owner-create proof

Each catalog-backed owner task create performs exactly two rule document-access calls:

1. `get(/databases/$(database)/documents/users/$(uid))` for the reset fence;
2. one `get(/databases/$(database)/documents/taskCatalog/$(documentId))`, reused for existence, row eligibility, immutable identity/provenance, and exact `taskInteraction` equality.

There is no separate `exists()`. Authentication and requested-map checks use local/request data. Every fresh owner candidate, policy-present or policy-absent, must carry `task_generation_epoch == user.taskGenerationEpoch` (absent root means 0), and rules deny omission, stale value, later change, or removal except §3.1's exact one-time legacy-synthetic upgrade. For a policy-bearing request, the catalog document must exist and the request additionally has path document ID equal to stored `id == taskId == catalog.taskId`, forbids a stored `documentId` key, matches the complete `CatalogOwnerTaskProjection` in §3.1, has a valid fresh `task_instance_id`, literal `flowWriteRevision:0`, literal `notesWriteRevision:0`, literal `quotesWriteRevision:0`, no tail/fence/state/contract, an eligible non-`rowGeneration` catalog row, and exact policy equality. Catalog equality applies to every catalog-derived field and optional-presence transform in that projection, not merely policy/identity; it deliberately excludes only runtime owner/timestamp/due-date/generation/instance/revision/move members. For a policy-absent catalog request, the same base projection and current generation are required, with the exact rowGeneration/flowRows exception; an existing catalog row that contains policy makes omission fail.

There is no fresh missing-catalog owner-create exception. Packing synthetic creates/regeneration run only through §4.7's Admin transaction; Rules allow solely the one-time current-epoch addition to an otherwise byte-identical pre-v5 generated-packing row and deny every other owner task/plan/readiness mutation. No arbitrary missing-catalog owner document is accepted. Thus this rules-call proof covers only catalog-backed TaskGenerationService chunks; the separate Node/emulator callable suite measures the 259-data-write plus one-operation-write packing transaction and its request limits without pretending Admin SDK writes consume client Rules calls.

| Chunk condition | SDK reads | Writes | Conservative rules calls |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| eight missing | root + eight target docs | 8 | 16 total; 2/write |
| final five | root + five targets | 5 | 10 total; 2/write |
| eight existing | root + eight targets | 0 | 0 write checks |
| mixed | root + eight targets | `m` | `2m` |

Owner creation is resumable, add-only, and capped at eight candidates per transaction. A reset observed before candidate reads aborts the invocation; a reset between chunks causes the next chunk to abort. After any error, a new invocation starts at C0 and uses existence checks; it never resumes a stale pre-reset candidate array. Ambiguous commit correctness is determined by final document identity, not a summed acknowledged-create count.

### 7.2 Exact 53-task emulator fixture

Use only demo project `demo-peezy-phase1`, owner UID `phase2-rule-owner`, other UID `phase2-rule-other`, reset alias `rsa1_20000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001`, and `due_date = 2026-10-01T12:00:00.000Z`. The reset fixture derives and asserts its canonical `rso1_` record ID from UID plus result epoch; it never substitutes the alias as a document ID. Every allowed create supplies `createdAt: serverTimestamp()` so the emulator resolves it to that write's `request.time`; after commit the test reads and freezes equality/type, never compares live rules authority to a historical wall-clock literal.

Synthetic catalog/task IDs are `P2_RULE_001...P2_RULE_053`, chunked C0=`001...008`, C1=`009...016`, C2=`017...024`, C3=`025...032`, C4=`033...040`, C5=`041...048`, C6=`049...053`. Every catalog row has canonical ID, `tier:"task"`, `spawnedOnly:false`, no `rowGeneration`, and exact `P2_VALID_MINIMAL_V1`.

Every owner candidate is exact except the numbered title:

```text
document path ID = id = taskId = P2_RULE_NNN; stored `documentId` absent
userId = phase2-rule-owner
title = "Rule fixture NNN"
desc = ""
category = "spec_fixture"
actionCategory = ""
actionType = "off-app"
taskType = "assessment"
urgencyPercentage = 50
estHours = 0
tips = whyNeeded = ""
conditions = {}
status = "Upcoming"
tier = "task"
selfServiceOnly = false
createdAt = serverTimestamp transform; dueDate = fixed timestamp above
taskInteraction = exact P2_VALID_MINIMAL_V1 map
task_instance_id = 00000000-0000-4000-8000-{NNN zero-padded to 12 decimal digits}
task_generation_epoch = 0
flowWriteRevision = 0
notesWriteRevision = 0
quotesWriteRevision = 0
```

Thus `P2_RULE_001` uses `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001` and `P2_RULE_053` uses `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000053`; these 53 exact values are unique valid lowercase UUIDs. They are fixture constants only. Production generation creates and retains a random UUID per candidate for the invocation/retry; a post-reset invocation generates a different UUID and never reuses a stale candidate array.

Required sequence and counts:

1. Baseline chunks create `[8,8,8,8,8,8,5]`; cumulative presence is `[8,16,24,32,40,48,53]`.
2. Full replay creates `[0,0,0,0,0,0,0]`; final count remains 53.
3. Inject an error after C0 commit but before client acknowledgement. Restart C0; it creates 0 and exactly eight correct docs exist.
4. After C0+C1, replace catalog `P2_RULE_021.taskInteraction` with the exact version-2 mismatch fixture. C2 is atomically denied and total remains 16. Restore catalog and restart from C0; creation counts are `[0,0,8,8,8,8,5]`.
5. After C0+C1, begin reset and atomically change root `taskGenerationEpoch 0→1` with state `deleting`. Production C2 stops and raw C2 with epoch 0 is denied. Delete the 16, change to `awaiting_local_reset`; both paths remain denied. Finalize while retaining root epoch 1. A delayed old C2 remains denied; a new invocation with epoch 1 reaches exactly 53.
6. Try policy omission, instance omission/malformed, nonzero/omitted any of the three initial revisions, omitted/stale generation epoch, initial tail/fence/state/contract, malformed policy, valid-but-not-exact policy, wrong same-document identity, a second arbitrary policy-bearing document ID, later add/change/remove policy/instance/generation/revision outside an exact server action, `rowGeneration + policy`, missing-catalog plus supplied policy, other user, and unauthenticated create; each is denied without partial writes. UUID uniqueness across different documents is a generator invariant and deterministic fixture assertion, not a rules claim; rules enforce only each document's immutable exact value.
7. Under root epoch 0, exercise `persistLegacyPackingPlan` with the exact policy-absent packing set including `PACKING_SUPPLIES_KIT`, then prove direct owner plan/readiness/task writes denied and the sole one-time otherwise-byte-identical current-epoch upgrade accepted for a preexisting generated-packing row. Begin/finalize reset to epoch 1, then prove a delayed captured epoch-0 callable and every direct captured batch are denied, an omitted-epoch request is denied, and a newly rebuilt epoch-1 callable succeeds with no policy/instance/revision fields and no non-fence byte drift. Repeat at the exact compatible 128-session+readiness+supplies/write-count maximum; assert the compact operation digests/ordered receipt, all declared stale deletions, and atomic failure at every expectation/shape/cap member.
8. Delete/reset then regenerate the same 53 document IDs with `task_generation_epoch = 1` and second frozen UUID set `10000000-0000-4000-8000-{NNN zero-padded to 12 decimal digits}`; all create, and every delayed epoch-0 create plus old-instance write/receipt/intent/route is stale. In the same reset fixture, establish root `activeMoveEventId`, prove owner root-path copy, deny nested owner paths and all caller-supplied/surplus aliases, rotate it, and deny every old-event task initialization/spawn/H54 reuse.
9. Against real-shape policy-absent catalog fixtures, exercise both TaskGenerationService loops for an ordinary owner row and each owner-reachable `rowGeneration` row: fresh epoch 0 accepted with unchanged base/`flowRows` bytes, delayed epoch 0 denied after reset/finalize, rebuilt epoch 1 accepted, and omitted/changed generation denied. The paired policy-on-rowGeneration fixture is denied before any chunk write.

The active catalog is not this fixture. It has 63 rows: 14 `spawnedOnly`, leaving 49 owner-reachable before condition gates; five of those owner-reachable rows have `rowGeneration`, leaving 44 structurally owner-policy-eligible rows (35 default-task, two conversation, seven nudge). There are six `rowGeneration` rows in total because one is spawned-only. Their IDs are `HANDLE_DMV_INTERSTATE`, `RESERVE_ACCESS_OLD`, `RESERVE_ACCESS_NEW`, `MEDICAL_RECORDS`, `FINANCIAL_ACCOUNTS`, and `MEMBERSHIPS`. Phase 2 applies no tier restriction beyond `spawnedOnly != true && rowGeneration absent` for policy eligibility.

### 7.3 Rules cases outside owner create

- Policy, task interaction state, contract, handoff, wake, resolution, history, H54 linkage, `task_generation_epoch`, `moveEventId`, and policy-task `dueDate` are server-only/immutable after validated create except the exact H56/content cases. User root `taskGenerationEpoch` is server-only; `activeMoveEventId` permits only the one-time absent→valid-ID establishment in §3.1 and is immutable thereafter until server reset rotation.
- Owner, other-user, and anonymous document deletion of a policy-present task is always denied, including a freshly created Upcoming task before state/contract initialization. Only the server reset/H54 reducers may delete or retire one. The emulator suite distinguishes this from field-change denial and proves all three principals against initialized and uninitialized policy tasks.
- Notification intents and `taskDeadlineEvidence` deny every owner/other/anonymous read and write. `taskPlanOperations` denies every client read and write—including ordinary operations, canonical `rso1_` active-reset records and finalized tombstones, any attempted `rsa1_` alias path, and `pcs1_` confirmation snapshots—because callables return the typed response; rollback/replay records are not a client query surface.
- Policy H56 initialization, continuation, full clear, notes, and quotes are five disjoint server-callable actions using §5.5's exact validators/+1 relations. Rules deny every direct owner/other/anonymous policy H56/content write. Every v5 legacy write uses §4.7; a fenced legacy row denies direct update/delete, while an old pre-v5 contractless row keeps its old-client rule only until it receives the exact synthetic upgrade or reset regeneration.
- The server policy clear action explicitly denies `prepared/open_requested/opened/returned` handoffs and every terminal/H54/reset/malformed state. Policy-absent packing synthetic fresh create/regeneration is Admin-only; owner Rules expose only the exact generatedBy/ID/current-root one-time epoch upgrade.
- Reset fence applies to owner catalog create, every §4.7 legacy action, and every server policy H56/content action. `packingPlan/**` and `readiness/**` are owner-readable/server-write-only.
- The rules suite asserts catalog call-budget assumptions through the eight-write catalog batch; the separate callable emulator asserts the 259-data-write plus one-operation-write packing maximum. Source diff review rejects any additional `get/exists/getAfter` on catalog owner-create paths. Tests cover owner/other/anonymous direct task update/delete and plan/readiness writes, plus read+write denial for notification intent, deadline source, ordinary operation, reset operation, and confirmation snapshot.

### 7.4 Frozen index configuration and budget registry

Root `firestore.indexes.json` after Phase 2 is exactly the following 4,432 UTF-8 bytes with one trailing LF and SHA-256 `a7a432ec8e0511176b890432c5e4cb4a07e59a6dba0c9d920948cc446f3a7bbb`:

```json
{
  "indexes": [
    {
```

The normalized registry used by `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` has the same array order, exact strings, and empty-index semantics. Tests serialize the parsed object with `JSON.stringify(value,null,2) + "\n"` and require both byte count/hash above before exercising a reducer. The task parent-map exemptions intentionally leave the three manual composites active; `taskPlanOperations.kind` is the sole child re-enable under its wildcard; the other seven wildcard groups are exact-ID/no-query transaction records. Any added, removed, reordered, or mode/scope-changed entry is a spec/hash mismatch and stops implementation for a renewed budget derivation rather than silently changing charge.

Main-root untracked `File.txt` is protected user data, not an authorized implementation path; its sole permitted Phase 2 analogue is the guarded empty worktree-only prerequisite in §9.4, which is never staged, manifested, reviewed as product code, patched, or copied from main. No seed/deploy/production call/write/send is permitted. Admin/test generators `diagnose-and-generate.js` and `functions/testProfile/seedTestUser.js` remain forbidden on a policy-activated target until a separately authorized Phase 3 preflight upgrades or retires them.

There is no `-testPlan`. `PEEZY_RUN_FIRESTORE_INTEGRATION` is unset. UI tests are skipped. Assert the result bundle with the installed command shape:

```sh
xcrun xcresulttool get test-results tests \
  --path '/tmp/peezy-phase2-v5-final-eedc6add.xcresult' --compact | \
/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin/node -e '
const fs=require("node:fs");
const E=new Set(["ConversationFlowTests","DispositionContractTests","TaskGroupingTests","TaskSupersessionTests","TaskInteractionTests","TaskInteractionProjectionTests","TaskCanonicalTests","HandoffSessionTests","ContextualOutcomeTests","TaskPlanDispositionTests","DispositionTriggerSelectionTests","TaskDispositionSurfaceTests","TaskRowLegacySnapshotTests","TasksStoreNamespaceTests","AppRootAuthRaceTests","LegacyTaskMutationFenceTests","PeezyNudgeAnswerTests","TaskRouteTests","FlowSceneFlushTests","ResearchPostureTests","MoversChainCoordinatorTests","MoversChainHandshakeTests"]);
const J=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(0,"utf8"));
const seen=new Set(),count=new Map([...E].map(x=>[x,0])),units=[],uis=[],bad=[];
function walk(n,owner=null){
  if(!n||typeof n!=="object")return;
  if(n.nodeType==="Unit test bundle")units.push(n.name);
  if(n.nodeType==="UI test bundle")uis.push(n.name);
  if(n.nodeType==="Test Suite"){
    if(E.has(n.name)){owner=n.name;seen.add(n.name)}
    else if(/Tests$/.test(n.name||"")&&n.name!=="Peezy 4.0Tests")owner=null;
  }
  if(n.nodeType==="Test Case"){
    if(!owner)bad.push(`unexpected case ${n.name}`);else count.set(owner,count.get(owner)+1);
    if(n.result!=="Passed")bad.push(`${owner||"?"}/${n.name}:${n.result}`);
  }
  for(const c of n.children||[])walk(c,owner);
}
for(const n of J.testNodes||[])walk(n);
if(units.length!==1||units[0]!=="Peezy 4.0Tests")bad.push(`unit bundles=${JSON.stringify(units)}`);
if(uis.length)bad.push(`ui bundles=${JSON.stringify(uis)}`);
for(const x of E)if(!seen.has(x)||count.get(x)===0)bad.push(`missing/empty ${x}`);
if(seen.size!==E.size)bad.push(`suite set=${JSON.stringify([...seen].sort())}`);
if(bad.length){console.error(bad.join("\n"));process.exit(1)}
console.log(`PASS ${E.size} suites ${[...count.values()].reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)} cases`);
'
```

### 9.4 Future worktree, review, and patch procedure

This procedure is recorded but is not run at the spec gate:

1. In main at absolute root `/Users/adampowell/Desktop/Peezy 4.0`, recheck branch/HEAD, every protected hash in §9.5, and absence of a Phase 2 worktree. Freeze `phase2_base=b4b047d29bea6a1721729b69e0d01aa7a1dc9923`, `phase2_main_root='/Users/adampowell/Desktop/Peezy 4.0'`, `phase2_main_file='/Users/adampowell/Desktop/Peezy 4.0/File.txt'`, and require the main file to remain a regular non-symlink of exactly 2,124 bytes with SHA-256 `560eb90395307e7f69315225bd2ba7b7f9f9d8d9afa4ba509d90ebe7f1f46f47`. `git cat-file -e "$phase2_base:File.txt"` must fail, while the base `Peezy 4.0.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` must contain the four existing `File.txt` reference/resource lines; this proves the prerequisite is absent from Git but required by the frozen project.
2. Create one private parent with `phase2_parent="$(mktemp -d /tmp/peezy-phase2-v5.XXXXXX)"`, set `phase2_tree="$phase2_parent/worktree"`, and run `git worktree add --detach "$phase2_tree" "$phase2_base"`. Require the worktree real path to differ from `phase2_main_root`, its HEAD to equal `phase2_base`, its initial tracked status to be clean, and its root `File.txt` to be absent. Do not stage or commit.
3. Immediately after worktree creation and before any scaffold/build/test command, set the only removable path explicitly as `phase2_empty_file="$phase2_tree/File.txt"`; reject unless that byte string equals the worktree root plus `/File.txt`, the real worktree remains outside main, and the path is absent. Never read or copy `phase2_main_file` into the worktree.
4. Run the following guard scaffold as one fail-fast shell before creating the prerequisite. Its cleanup may act only when the target is the exact regular non-symlink zero-byte/hash value; otherwise it stops without deleting an unknown path. The EXIT handler promotes a cleanup/main-guard failure to nonzero, and normal cleanup clears traps only after both proofs succeed. No recursive removal, unresolved path, glob, or main-root removal is permitted.

```sh
set -euo pipefail
phase2_main_hash='560eb90395307e7f69315225bd2ba7b7f9f9d8d9afa4ba509d90ebe7f1f46f47'
phase2_empty_hash='e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855'
phase2_main_real="$(cd "$phase2_main_root" && pwd -P)"
phase2_tree_real="$(cd "$phase2_tree" && pwd -P)"

sha256_phase2_path() {
  /usr/bin/shasum -a 256 -- "$1" | /usr/bin/awk '{print $1}'
}

assert_main_file_guard() {
  test "$phase2_main_file" = '/Users/adampowell/Desktop/Peezy 4.0/File.txt' &&
  test "$phase2_main_real" != "$phase2_tree_real" &&
  test -f "$phase2_main_file" &&
  test ! -L "$phase2_main_file" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f %z "$phase2_main_file")" = '2124' &&
  test "$(sha256_phase2_path "$phase2_main_file")" = "$phase2_main_hash"
}

assert_empty_worktree_file_absent() {
  test "$phase2_empty_file" = "$phase2_tree/File.txt" &&
  test "$phase2_main_real" != "$phase2_tree_real" &&
  test ! -e "$phase2_empty_file" &&
  test ! -L "$phase2_empty_file" &&
  test -z "$(git -C "$phase2_tree" ls-files -- File.txt)" &&
  test -z "$(git -C "$phase2_tree" status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all -- File.txt)"
}

assert_empty_worktree_file() {
  test "$phase2_empty_file" = "$phase2_tree/File.txt" &&
  test "$phase2_main_real" != "$phase2_tree_real" &&
  test -f "$phase2_empty_file" &&
  test ! -L "$phase2_empty_file" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f %z "$phase2_empty_file")" = '0' &&
  test "$(sha256_phase2_path "$phase2_empty_file")" = "$phase2_empty_hash" &&
  test -z "$(git -C "$phase2_tree" ls-files -- File.txt)" &&
  test "$(git -C "$phase2_tree" status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all -- File.txt)" = '?? File.txt'
}

cleanup_phase2_empty_file() {
  if test ! -e "$phase2_empty_file" && test ! -L "$phase2_empty_file"; then
    assert_empty_worktree_file_absent
    return
  fi
  assert_empty_worktree_file || return 70
  /bin/rm -f -- "$phase2_empty_file" || return 70
  assert_empty_worktree_file_absent
}

phase2_exit_cleanup() {
  phase2_exit_code=$?
  trap - EXIT HUP INT TERM
  cleanup_phase2_empty_file || exit 70
  assert_main_file_guard || exit 71
  exit "$phase2_exit_code"
}

assert_main_file_guard
assert_empty_worktree_file_absent
trap 'phase2_exit_cleanup' EXIT
trap 'exit 129' HUP
trap 'exit 130' INT
trap 'exit 143' TERM
: > "$phase2_empty_file"
assert_empty_worktree_file
assert_main_file_guard
```

The final three assertions prove: regular non-symlink; size `0`; empty-file SHA-256; no index entry; and path-limited porcelain status exactly `?? File.txt` before any later command.
5. Integrate S1→S2→S4→S5 on the critical path; S3 follows S2; S7 row routing follows S4 and outcome routing follows S5; S6 follows S5; S8/S9 may be authored independently only with disjoint files. Each slice is RED → GREEN → literal diff review. At worktree creation/scaffold completion, every slice checkpoint, and immediately before and after every build or test envelope, run both guards and require the only worktree status outside the current explicit Phase 2 path manifest to remain `?? File.txt`. Record base commit, dependency hashes, literal touched-path manifest, and cumulative patch SHA-256; a later edit invalidates that file's review and only its dependent review.
6. Run the exact build/XCTest/Node/rules envelopes, whole server/client diff reviews, authorized-path scan, diff checks, and protected-hash comparison in the integration worktree. No production-connected command is permitted. After the last successful verification, run exactly `cleanup_phase2_empty_file && assert_empty_worktree_file_absent && assert_main_file_guard && trap - EXIT HUP INT TERM`; any failure stops patch creation. The empty prerequisite is never a touched/new-path manifest member.
7. Build the tracked patch with `git diff --binary --full-index "$phase2_base" --`, passing every existing tracked path in §8 as a separate literal argument in unsigned UTF-8 byte order. Append each new path from §8, in the same order, by invoking `git diff --binary --no-index /dev/null` with that path as the next literal argument; exit 1 means differences, not failure. Store the canonical patch outside the worktree at the explicit `phase2_parent/PHASE2.patch`. Record a separate literal ordered path/SHA-256 manifest. Parse the completed patch with `git apply --numstat -z` and require its decoded paths to equal that manifest exactly; reject renames, surplus/duplicate paths, any root `File.txt`, or any path outside §8. Reassert empty-file absence and main guard before and after patch creation.
8. Recheck dirty main HEAD and every protected hash. With main `File.txt` still at its protected hash, run `git apply --check` on the accepted canonical patch, reassert both File proofs, apply it once, reassert both again, and compare every Phase 2 path hash with the accepted integration manifest. The parsed applied path set must still exclude `File.txt`; no apply command may use `--include=File.txt` or an unrestricted worktree diff. Do not run a production-connected smoke test.
9. Reassert main's protected `File.txt`, the absent worktree prerequisite, all other protected hashes, and the exact applied path manifest. Stop before commit and show Phase-2-only plus total-working-tree diff stats separately. Do not remove or edit Build 25 WIP. Worktree retirement, if later authorized, must use Git's worktree command and may occur only after the explicit empty-file absence proof; this procedure never recursively deletes its parent.

The executable-spec whole-file hash cannot be embedded in the bytes it hashes without becoming self-referential. After the final review patch, SHA-256 is computed over the exact file bytes and reported to the owner with byte/line count. That externally reported value is the sole adoption token.

## 10. Read-only adversarial review disposition

The former 0-P0/21-P1 table is retired by the owner-adopted first amendment and its P1-A through P1-U resolutions. The second-pass review was conducted against immutable tuple `9550097aaf8606b97a2fa24b7057d11d5903045bf106ed4c2640473af75bb384` / 381,697 bytes / 1,717 lines and returned eight P1 findings; the owner-adopted second amendment and its eight one-to-one rows in §8.6 retire that report. The third-pass review was conducted against immutable tuple `a9d9ac3471b4436e4f335a763ff1a4e8ed6ffdc7311935169f24e9518c4fb616` / 425,361 bytes / 2,073 lines and returned zero P0 plus nine P1 findings; the owner-adopted third amendment and its nine one-to-one rows in §8.6 retire that report. Review disposition is deliberately external to the bytes being reviewed so recording a result cannot mutate and invalidate the reviewed hash.

The next pass freezes one new SHA-256/byte/line tuple before any reviewer begins. At least three independent read-only scopes examine exactly those bytes: (1) policy/server/rules/transaction/index/scheduler closure; (2) client/race/durable-state/UI/worktree/test closure; and (3) schema/evidence/canonical fixture/manifest/register cross-consistency. Reviewers treat `PEEZY_STATE.md`, all three adopted amendments, and the amended institution master as authority, make no edit, and return every independently discovered P0/P1 finding with file:line evidence or explicit zero P0/P1. The coordinator unions and de-duplicates all reports and presents them together; it never edits the reviewed tuple.

Any P0/P1 yields STOP against that tuple. Zero P0/P1 yields only an adoption candidate; no Phase 2 worktree, RED test, implementation edit, seed, deploy, production-connected test, remote write, StoreKit action, or FCM/APNs send may begin until the owner explicitly adopts the externally reported whole-file SHA-256.
